# A07 — STUDY: Strategies, screening, filtering and trade decision

**Status:** DRAFT — the STUDY of topic A07 (Document 04, section 4, step 4), for ChatGPT's review
**Date:** 2026-11-22
**Revision history:** First version, 2026-11-22.
**Purpose and scope:** The factual study of how the legacy system decides, for a signal handed over at the screening call, whether it is accepted, rejected or advanced: the strategy-control resolver `strategy_will_trade` and the strategy taxonomy, the strategy lifecycle states and PAPER_ONLY, the secondary screener and its routes, statuses and result, the market data the screening steps receive and the ten steps with their executor, the quality scorer, the HardGate and the screener's V3 modes, the five V3 playbook gates (Rama's decision P1), the entry gate and its resume contract (Rama's decision P2), the statuses and companion rows A07 code writes and their readers, concurrency, retries and duplicated checks, the end-to-end paths and the handoff to A09, the three strategy scripts and the direction registry, the configuration A07 code reads, the unused and partially wired components, the legacy documents, records and tests against the code, the 51 items that earlier topics handed to A07 and the 10 Open Issues Register entries that mention it, and the cross-topic boundaries, at the fixed study baseline. It records what the code does; it does not compare the code with intended behaviour, which is the AUDIT's work.
**Source:** trading-system-prod-snapshot, branch `main`, commit `970aabf3c78d777d9d34549e021f7c8e756c7bdb` (Source Inventory, SRC-10), read on 20 to 22-Nov-2026 from the local read-only clone with `git show`, `git grep`, `git log`, `git diff`, `git blame` and `git ls-tree`; nothing in it was written or run, and no endpoint, scanner, broker, webhook or API was contacted. Library, broker-documentation and method behaviour from the public documentation and published sources named in section 1.5, as current external context.
**Related documents:** Documents 04, 05, 06, 07, 08 and 10; the Master System Investigation Index (entry A07 and Appendix A); the Decision Register, D-07, D-12, D-17, D-21, D-23 to D-25, D-28, D-30 to D-33, D-35 and D-37 (the approved topic packages whose records this STUDY reads), D-38 (the A06-to-A07 hand-over line) and D-39 (the A07 boundary decisions P1 to P3 and the working method); ChatGPT's A07 investigation direction of 20-Nov-2026 and Claude's first A07 fieldwork checkpoint and ChatGPT's review of it of the same day (not kept as files, D-17); the A01 to A06 RECONCILED TOPIC RECORDS and AUDITs; the Topic Completion Register (entry A07); the Open Issues Register; the Audit Findings Register; the A07 evidence files listed in section 20.

## 1. Purpose, scope, exclusions and fixed baseline

### 1.1 Purpose

This STUDY establishes, from the code at the fixed baseline, how the legacy system decides whether a signal that has reached the screening call is accepted, rejected or advanced, and what it hands to the price, sizing and risk stage: strategy selection and controls, secondary screening, hard gates, quality scoring, entry gates, step execution, the rejection and acceptance outcomes and the decision handoff (Master System Investigation Index, entry A07; Document 08, section 3, coverage items 5 and 6). It answers the 26 focused questions of ChatGPT's A07 investigation direction of 20-Nov-2026 (section 8 of the direction; section 1.9 here) and records A07's side of the 51 items that the closed topics handed to it and of the 10 Open Issues Register entries that mention it (section 16). It records what the code does; it does not compare the code with intended behaviour, which is the AUDIT's work.

### 1.2 Scope and paths

The A07 paths of the Master System Investigation Index (entry A07 and Appendix A): `strategies/control.py` and `strategies/taxonomy.py` (`strategies/schema.py` and `strategies/loader.py` are A02's); `screening/secondary_screener.py`, `screening/hard_gate.py`, `screening/quality_scorer.py`, `screening/entry_gate.py` and `screening/step_executor.py`; `core/strategy_direction.py`; `strategies/__init__.py` and `screening/__init__.py` (package initialisation and exports; provisional, see S1); the relevant `regime/` and `v3_chain/` integration call sites; and three scripts, `scripts/compute_strategy_metrics.py`, `scripts/strategy_registry_officer.py` and `scripts/strategy_status.py`. Their sizes, Git objects, SHA-256 values and histories, the A07 call sites in other topics' files, the declared invocations of the scripts, the related paths of other topics and the tests that reference A07 code are in the source file inventory (evidence, `A07_SOURCE_FILE_INVENTORY_2026-11-20.md`). The index's boundary: A07 owns the decision to accept, reject or advance a signal; A08 owns the technical-analysis and S&R calculations; A09 owns the entry, R:R, risk and capital decisions, and may cite the capital and governor handoff of `scripts/compute_strategy_metrics.py` (index, entry A07; see section 12 for what the code holds).

The start line (Decision Register, D-38, applied as decided): the hand-over is at the screening call (`signals/signal_processor.py` L998 at the baseline). The strategy lookup and the processor's orchestration up to that call stay recorded on A06's side, and A07's investigation of the processing flow starts at the screening call. The calls made before the screening call are recorded on A06's side at the interface only; their logic stays with its owners and is studied by them: the strategy-control check `strategy_will_trade(...)` (A07's strategy-control logic, which A07's investigation covers under its strategy-selection and controls scope although the call is made before the screening call), the strategy's own entry window (A01) and the strategy governor (A09) (A06 STUDY section 6, A06's evidence statement P04-S032); the screening logic is A07's from the screening call; every other interface keeps the owner that the Master System Investigation Index and A06's approved interface map give it. PQ-14 stays an A06 MATCH with A06 as owner. The approved A06 records are not edited. At the baseline the strategy-control call is at L959 (P02-S031).

The boundaries Rama decided on 20-Nov-2026 on ChatGPT's recommendations, without any change to the index (Decision Register, D-39), applied as decided:

- **P1.** A07 studies in full the five V3 playbook gate functions in `screening/hard_gate.py` (`gate_rr`, `gate_htf`, `gate_extreme`, `gate_confirm` and `gate_pullback`): their inputs, calculations, thresholds, units, missing-data and error behaviour and the returned `GateVerdict`; A18 owns their call sites, when its chain calls them and how it consumes the verdicts, and A07 does not audit A18's chain; `gate_rr`'s arithmetic and output are recorded faithfully, the entry, stop, target, risk and R:R decisions staying A09's and the production of S&R inputs A08's (section 7).
- **P2.** A07 studies `screening/entry_gate.py` fully (start and rehydration, add, polling, release, persistence calls, the callback contract and failure behaviour), traces the callback through A01's `main.py` wiring to A06's `SignalProcessor.continue_from_gate`, records that function's inputs, outputs, status and decision contract and its handoff to A09 at the interface, and studies the A07-owned `strategy_will_trade` logic invoked on the resume path, including its call at `signals/signal_processor.py` L2062; A07 does not audit the internal orchestration of `continue_from_gate`, which stays A06's, the A01, A06 and A09 portions are recorded at their interfaces only, and A06's records are not edited (section 8).
- **P3.** The comparison of the screening components with the actual Chartink scanner conditions (Open Issues Register, A05-25) is made when Rama supplies the conditions (screenshots or an export), and A07 proceeds meanwhile; the material, once supplied, is external evidence, admitted to the Source Inventory only on Rama's decision (Document 05, section 11.1; D-12); Claude does not contact Chartink or retrieve scanner or account data; if the conditions are not supplied, the comparison is recorded as UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, and no scanner condition is inferred from names, URLs, comments or memory. No conditions have been supplied for this STUDY; the comparison is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (sections 18.2 and 19).

The 51 inherited items keep their original owners, IDs, classifications, statuses and treatments; A07 records its own side only (Topic Completion Register, entry A07; section 16).

### 1.3 Exclusions

- Paper-only branches (Document 08, section 2; the project is live-only): marked "paper-only" where met, not traced.
- Other topics' paths, recorded at the interface only, with the interface fact A07's side needs: `main.py` as a file, start-up checks, `core/market_windows.py` and the strategy's own entry window (A01); configuration sources, schemas, loading and precedence, `strategies/schema.py`, `strategies/loader.py`, `core/config_loader.py`, `core/config_auditor.py` and `config/` (A02); database schemas, persistence, connections and write discipline (A03); the broker adapter, `get_quote` and product resolution (A04); the instrument cache and candle data (A05); the strategy lookup and the processor's orchestration up to the screening call, the rest of `signals/signal_processor.py` and the internal orchestration of `continue_from_gate` (A06); technical analysis, S&R and `screening/retest_monitor.py` (A08); prices, sizing, risk, reservation, capital and the strategy governor (A09); order placement and lifecycle (A10, A11); the MIS learned-blocklist module and the end-of-day square-off (A12); the kill switch (A13); alerts (A14); reports, the dashboard, the evidence recorder and telemetry (A15); schedules, `config/cron_registry.yaml`, `deploy/cron/` and `scripts/cron_officer.py` (A16); deployment (A17); `regime/`, `v3_chain/` and the allocator (A18); tests (A19), cited as stated expectations only (common brief, section 2; inventory, section 4).
- Runtime behaviour without surviving evidence (section 1.6).
- Credential values, tokens, account identifiers, environment and local-overlay values (D-07; section 1.10).

### 1.4 Fixed baseline

trading-system-prod-snapshot, branch `main`, commit `970aabf3c78d777d9d34549e021f7c8e756c7bdb` (Decision Register, D-21), written `970aabf` below. The last recorded deployment is commits `686df1c847d415958c4df406b77503bb45975749` and `7d4970a26991fd40774023f5293a00556228e14b` (both 2026-09-02); the baseline is 32 commits after `7d4970a` (`git rev-list --count`, checked at assembly; section 1.8; inventory, section 5). Every line number in this STUDY is the line of the file at the baseline unless another commit is named.

### 1.5 Method

The legacy repository was read only from a local read-only clone at the baseline, with `git show`, `git grep` (every repository-wide search excluding `config/accounts.csv`, `.env*` files, `*.local.yaml` files and token files, none of which was opened), `git log`, `git diff`, `git blame` (hashes and dates only), `git ls-tree` and `git rev-list`. Helper scripts read text and ran `git` read commands only, under `python3 -I -B`; no legacy code was run, imported, built or tested, and no broker, scanner, Chartink page, webhook or other endpoint was contacted (P02-S001, P02-S004; P08-S002; P10-S003, P10-S004).

The fieldwork was divided into ten parts (P01 to P10, section 20.2). Each part was written by a read-only helper and then checked statement by statement against its sources, and corrected in place, by a separate read-only helper; the check log of each part is the last section of its evidence file, and the corrected text governs. At most two helpers ran at a time, a new pair starting only when both helpers of the previous pair had handed back (the working method of D-39). The parts were written and checked on 21 and 22-Nov-2026. Working drafts were backed up after each section to the temporary branch `wip/a07-fieldwork`, scanned for identifiers before every push and never merged into `main` (D-39). Three addenda to the part briefs (21-Nov-2026) fixed which part cross-references each inherited item (ADDENDUM 1), the handoff points without item IDs (ADDENDUM 2) and the overlapping scope between parts (ADDENDUM 3: P04 owns the content of `_build_market_data`, P05 the internals of the screener's V3 methods, P03 the routes into them). This STUDY was assembled from the ten checked parts by a separate read-only helper (pair 11), with no new fieldwork beyond what reconciling two parts needed; each such check is stated with its source line. No part statement needed correcting at assembly: where two parts differ in scope or detail (the 65 ceiling and its timeout condition, P04-S107 and P05-AP28; the overlap settled by ADDENDUM 3, P03-AP21), the STUDY states both with their IDs (sections 6 and 17). The STUDY was then checked independently against the evidence and the code by a further read-only helper (pair 12), which corrected the STUDY in place and two part statements: P03-S140 (the lines `main.py` gained after `7d4970a`) and P06-U06 (a quoted A04 evidence ID made explicit); each is logged under "Corrections at assembly check" at the end of the check log of the part concerned.

Library, broker-documentation and method behaviour was taken from authoritative public sources only and is labelled current external context, with the date read (section 1.7). The pages the parts read, all on 21-Nov-2026 (the URLs are in each part's section 1, and in P03-S142 and P06-S032):

- Python documentation (current release pages): built-in functions (`getattr`, `bool`, `round`, `max`, `float`, `int`) (P02-S007, P05-S006, P06-S032; `max` in P05's check log); built-in types ("Truth Value Testing", "Comparisons", `dict.get`) (P02-S007, P04-S007); the import system, section 5.2.1 (P02-S007); expressions, "Value comparisons" (P02-S122, P04-S007, P05-S006); built-in constants (`NotImplemented`) (P04-S007); `concurrent.futures` (P03-S007, P04-S007, P06-S005); `threading` (P06-S005); `queue` (`SimpleQueue`) (P04-S007); `logging`, the logging HOWTO, `sys` and `argparse` (P07-S008); the Library and Extension FAQ, "What kinds of global value mutation are thread-safe?" (P09-S008).
- CPython published source: `Lib/concurrent/futures/_base.py` (branches `main` and `3.13`) and `Lib/concurrent/futures/thread.py` (`main`) (P04-S007); `Objects/floatobject.c` and `Objects/longobject.c` (`main`) (P05-S006); and, for P07-S039 only, the Python 3.13 standard-library source of `argparse` read from the checker's local interpreter (P07-S008).
- Zerodha Kite Connect v3, "Market quotes and instruments" (the `/quote` response attributes, its example response and the Limits section) (P04-S007).
- Pydantic documentation pages for the latest release (no single version stated; the baseline pins `pydantic==2.13.0`): standard library types (booleans), the conversion table, fields ("Default values") and the `BaseModel` reference (P02-S007, P05-S006, P08-S005).
- PyYAML documentation (P08-S005) and the python-dotenv page on PyPI (version shown 1.2.4; the baseline pins 1.2.2) (P08-S005).
- SQLite documentation: "The ON CONFLICT clause" (P07-S008) and "SQLite Foreign Key Support" (P03-S142; P09-S008 uses both through the parts' statements).
- W. F. Sharpe, "The Sharpe Ratio", and A. W. Lo, "The Statistics of Sharpe Ratios" (Financial Analysts Journal, 2002; abstract), for the method only (P07-S008).

Parts P01 and P10 read no external source (P01-S009, P10-S008). One local check of the work environment's own interpreter (Python 3.13.18) evaluated `round()` on literal numbers, with no legacy code (P05-S006). The production Python, Pydantic and other library versions are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED; the pins in `requirements.txt` are FACT about the file only (P04-S164; P05-S006).

### 1.6 Runtime evidence limit

Both legacy machines were terminated, and no backup of their databases, logs or crontab is known (reported by Rama, not independently verified; Document 06, section 4.2). No production database, log, journal or deployed configuration of the legacy period survives; a cron declaration, a test or a legacy document is not evidence that anything ran, and every committed configuration value is FACT about the file only (P09-S006; P08-S019). Every statement about what was screened, rejected, passed, released, written or computed in production is therefore UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, and which commit production ran is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (D-21; section 1.8).

The entry gate, in the fixed wording applied throughout this STUDY: `EntryGate.add()` has no non-test caller at the baseline, so the normal live path that creates a new gate entry is not established; `EntryGate.start()` rehydrates any persisted `gate_state` rows and can poll and release them; whether such rows existed in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (inventory, section 2.2; D-39; section 8).

### 1.7 Labels

FACT, UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, INTERPRETATION, HISTORICAL and OPEN QUESTION (Document 04, section 6); KNOWN DEFECT is not used in this STUDY (common brief, section 4). Anything Rama must decide is marked OPEN FOR RAMA. HISTORICAL marks what a legacy document, comment, docstring, commit message, test or record states; it is an intent source, never evidence that anything ran. Current documentation and published library source are labelled current external context, with the date read; they are not facts about the legacy period. Where a STUDY sentence summarises part statements, its label is that of the statements it rests on, and an INTERPRETATION in a part stays an INTERPRETATION here.

### 1.8 Changes after the last recorded deployment (D-21)

FACT (`git diff 7d4970a 970aabf`; inventory, section 5): two of the thirteen A07 files changed after `7d4970a`; the other eleven are identical at both commits (P02-S003; P03-S140; P04-S004; P05-S002; P06-S002).

- `screening/secondary_screener.py`: four commits of 2026-09-10 and 11 (`8d27fdb`, `d646347`, `02d1e3f`, `056905b`, the "evidence" CHECKPOINT series); 77 lines added, 15 removed. The diff changes only the evidence wiring: the constructor parameter `evidence` and its attribute, the four identity keywords (`symbol`, `strategy_name`, `trigger_price`, `triggered_at`) added to each of the 15 `_persist` calls, the keyword-only parameters of `_persist`, and the P3 evidence capture with its helper; no status string, route, threshold, comparison or order of checks differs (P03-S003; P05-S118; P08-S018). The text of `_build_market_data` and the lines that build the thresholds and call `run_all` are identical at both commits (P04-S005).
- `scripts/strategy_registry_officer.py`: one commit, `284698884aa7e113c4c8f89bfaae8a0ed04a4a05` (2026-09-09), 2 lines added and 1 removed: the alert title reads the account tag through `core.account_registry` instead of a tag fixed in the code (the earlier value is not reproduced) (P07-S006; P08-S055).
- In other topics' files at the interface: `signals/signal_processor.py` gained 216 lines and lost 1, none inside the caller's contract lines used here (L895 to L1028, L1233 to L1246, L1959 to L1970, L335 to L346); the P1 evidence block that reads the result after a pass (L1424 to L1445) and the `P2_REJECT` capture of the reject handler (L1202 to L1211) are among the added lines; `broker/zerodha_adapter.py` changed only on the order-placement path, not `get_quote` or the `Quote` dataclass; `main.py` gained 42 lines: the evidence recorder's construction and the `evidence=` argument to the screener (L3248 to L3280, L3297), the `evidence=` argument to the processor (L3622) and two MIS market-protection arguments of the end-of-day square-off (L3369 to L3375, A12's) (P03-S140, corrected at the assembly check; P04-S005; P09-S026; P02-S034).
- No value line of a configuration key that A07 code reads changed: `config/scoring_weights.yaml`, the 16 strategy files and `strategies/schema.py` are unchanged, and the changed lines of `config/system_config.yaml` are MIS square-off keys that no A07 file reads (P08-S018).

INTERPRETATION: apart from the evidence wiring and the officer's alert title, the A07 code recorded here is the code of the last recorded deployment commit (P03-S003; P05-S002; P05-S118). UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED: whether production ran any commit after `7d4970a`, so whether the evidence capture and the officer's account-file read were ever in force (P01-S115; P04-S165; P07-S006).

### 1.10 Security restriction

The project's security rules, applied verbatim: "no credential-validity, credential-history or exposure investigation, alert, GitHub issue or recommendation; do not read or derive values from the legacy account or credential files for any checking purpose; do not reproduce any value; credential variable NAMES are recorded only as configuration." "Never write an account identifier, personal name, IP address, e-mail address, key fingerprint, absolute legacy machine path or any secret value. Write the type and location only, with per-account names as <ACCOUNT_ID>." The Project Owner's name, Rama, may be written. `config/accounts.csv`, `.env*` files, `*.local.yaml` files and `*token*.json` files were never opened, even where code names one as a configuration input; the key or variable name and the file's type and location only are recorded. A line that carries an account identifier or a machine path is cited by file and line only, never quoted: in A07's files, the officer's alert title reads the primary account tag from the account file (`scripts/strategy_registry_officer.py` L44, L194), and the cron declarations of the scripts carry machine paths (`deploy/cron/trading-system.cron` L120 to L127); neither value is reproduced (P08-S055; inventory, section 3). No credential or security issue, alert or register entry is made (D-07).

## 2. A07 in brief

What decides that a signal is accepted, rejected or advanced on A07's side, in the order the code runs it at the baseline, and what is dormant or not wired. Committed configuration values are FACT about the files only; every production-effective value is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (section 1.6). Each point is detailed in the section named.

**In order, on the live path (one processor worker per signal):**

1. **Strategy control** (A07's logic at A06's call site, `signals/signal_processor.py` L959; also at L2062 and L2411 on the two resume paths). `strategy_will_trade` reads only the strategy's `enabled` and `intent` and the system's `trade_type` and `force_intraday_only`: a disabled strategy gets `DISABLED`, a DELIVERY strategy under the breaker `FORCE_BREAKER`, a trade-type mismatch `TRADE_TYPE`, otherwise WILL TRADE; A06's handler writes `REJECTED_STRATEGY_CONTROL` or `REJECTED_TRADE_TYPE` before any screening. At the committed values (`trade_type: BOTH`, breaker off) 15 strategies get WILL TRADE and `pb01_breakout_retest` is disabled (P02-S015, P02-S017, P02-S063, P09-S083; section 3). The resolver has no `delivery_enabled` input (P02-S061).
2. **Between the two A07 steps** come A01's per-strategy entry window and A09's governor, at the interface only (P02-S031, P02-S086; section 17).
3. **The screening call**, `screen()` at L998 (the D-38 start line), runs its routes in this order (P03-S030; section 4): R1 the MIS learned-blocklist pre-drop (committed `mis_filter.enabled: true`, `shadow: true`: a WARNING only, no reject) (P03-S031 to P03-S036, P08-S026); R2 the screener's own quote fetch (a missing quote or an error ends `SKIPPED_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE`); the V3 switch (committed `"shadow"`, so the default route runs and a comparison line is logged; `"enforce"` would run the HardGate first) (P03-S048); R3 the pre-fill circuit-proximity reject on the trigger price with A09's 2 % margin (`REJECTED_CIRCUIT_PROXIMITY`) (P05-S048 to P05-S052); R4 the ten steps on one shared step worker, each step's result awaited for at most 5 s, a wait that includes queueing behind other signals' steps, a timed-out step scoring 0.5 (P04-S023, P04-S028, P04-S033); R5 a step error (`REJECTED_STEP_ERROR`); R6 the quality scorer (weighted sum, tiers 80 and 65) (P05-S020, P05-S021); R7 the minimum score, 60 at the committed file, the strategies' `min_score: 0` leaving it global (`REJECTED_SCORE_<n>`) (P03-S064, P08-S038); R8 the signal-age reject when step 10 scores 0.0 (`REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE`); R9 `PASSED` with score and tier (P03-S068). The screener writes the status and a `screener_results` row itself, the status first, in one `try` whose failures are logged and swallowed (P03-S078 to P03-S083; section 9).
4. **What the live route can reach.** Four step inputs are always `None` (`avg_volume_20d`, `atr`, `rsi`, `sector`), so steps 1 and 3 always score 0.0 and steps 4 and 6 always 0.5; when no step times out the total cannot exceed 65 (V3 scale 56), `HIGH` is unreachable and `MEDIUM` is reached only at exactly 65; with timed-out steps the totals can reach 78 and 72 (P04-S107, P05-S029, P05-S068; section 6). The per-strategy `min_volume_surge` and `min_adr_pct` therefore cannot act (P08-S042); the units of `min_adr_pct` and `max_spread_pct` are in conflict between the sources and the code (P04-S103 to P04-S105). At the committed values a signal over 60 s old at step 10 ends `REJECTED_SCORE_<n>`, not `REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE`, when no step times out (P04-S113, P04-S136); P9a's 90 s exists nowhere in A07's code (P04-S124).
5. **What A07 hands on.** A `PASSED` result in memory (tier, score, step data) to A06's processor and, through it, to A09's sizing; A07 decides no entry, stop, target, size or product for the live order (P09-S073, P09-S074, P09-S076, P09-S034, P02-S022; section 11). A07 advances or defers no signal on the direct path (P09-S085).

**Dormant, not wired, or acting only on logs:**

- **The entry gate.** `EntryGate.add()` has no non-test caller at the baseline, so the normal live path that creates a new gate entry is not established; `EntryGate.start()` rehydrates any persisted `gate_state` rows and can poll and release them; whether such rows existed in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P06-S045). The end-of-day clear of the gate is not wired in `main.py`, against the FIX-046 message and the approved A03 records H-50, Q-11 and Q-45, which are not edited (P06-S074, P06-S075; reported, section 17). The six strategies with `pullback_wait_enabled: true` enter on the direct path (P06-S046, P06-S047; section 8).
- **The V3 HardGate and the five playbook gates.** The HardGate rejects only on the `"enforce"` path, which is not the committed mode; at `"shadow"` it writes a log line only (P05-S061 to P05-S066). The five playbook gates are called only by A18's code and reject on no live A07 path (P05-S093 to P05-S098, P09-S084; section 7).
- **PAPER_ONLY.** The metrics script's demotion writes no state that strategy control or the trading path reads; it logs one CRITICAL line, while its docstring and alert text and the index's Appendix A (L303) say that it sets a strategy to PAPER_ONLY (P02-S076 to P02-S079, P07-S030 to P07-S034, P07-S099; section 12).
- **Unread data.** No A07 file reads `daily_symbol_stats`, the F&O ban list, `order_protocol` or the two `pullback_wait_*` tuning fields (P03-S122, P09-S068, P09-S110, P08-S071, P08-S044; section 14).
- **The scripts.** The direction-registry officer writes its state file under `data_store/`, not the tracked `config/` file that comments name, and falls back to the seed on an unreadable state file without a log line; the status table's "can NEVER disagree" claim holds at the baseline file values but not when a file or flag changes after boot, a raw value is of another type or `trade_type` is out of set (P02-S103, P02-S111, P07-S073, P02-S052, P07-S083; section 12).
- **Repeated screening.** A FIX-069 retry re-runs strategy control and `screen()` for the same signal, up to four screenings in all, each adding a row and replacing the status; the screener has no idempotency check (P09-S088, P09-S089, P03-S079; section 10).

Sections 18 and 19 carry every point for the AUDIT, every item that cannot be established and the draft questions for Rama.

## 3. Strategy selection and control: `strategy_will_trade`, the taxonomy, lifecycle states and PAPER_ONLY (questions Q1 to Q5, Q16)

This section covers `strategies/control.py` (the strategy-control resolver) and its seven call sites, the strategy fields and system flags it reads at the A02 boundary, how the strategy is identified after A06's lookup (interface only, D-38), `strategies/taxonomy.py`, the per-strategy states and PAPER_ONLY, the interface with A01's window control, the control-layer vocabulary and the two package initialisers. The direction registry (`core/strategy_direction.py`) is in section 12 with the officer that writes it; the status table as a script in section 12; the statuses as a whole in section 9.

Evidence: `A07_STRATEGY_CONTROL_TAXONOMY_AND_DIRECTION_REGISTRY_2026-11-21.md` (P02), which read the five P02 files in whole and every call site at the interface; with P07 for the scripts and P09 for the statuses. None of the five P02 files changed after `7d4970a` (P02-S003).

**The resolver's declared purpose (Q2).**

- HISTORICAL: the module docstring (L1 to L27, last changed in `8116b74`, 2026-07-10) calls the module the "Slice 2 strategy-control resolver (single source of truth)" and `strategy_will_trade()` "the SOLE authority" for "will this strategy trade today?", imported by the entry gate (the processor) and the status table "so the gate and the displayed table can NEVER disagree" (P02-S008). It names four layers: LAYER 0 `force_intraday_only` (the emergency breaker; under "Option A (10-Jul-2026)" the loader no longer rewrites intent, so the breaker plus DELIVERY branch "is now the LIVE mechanism that dormants a DELIVERY strategy"), LAYER 1 `system_config.trade_type`, LAYER 2 `strategy.intent`, LAYER 3 `strategy.enabled`; "THE RULE": a strategy will trade iff `enabled AND (intent permitted by trade_type) AND (breaker doesn't block it)`; "Pure: no I/O, no mode branch" (P02-S009).
- FACT: the module imports only `__future__.annotations`, `dataclasses.dataclass` and two `typing` names; L33 to L117 contain no file, database, network or logging call and no assignment to module-level or external state (P02-S010). The function has no paper or live branch and reads no mode value (P02-S023).

**Constants and the `Verdict` type.**

- FACT: `VALID_TRADE_TYPES = {"INTRADAY", "DELIVERY", "BOTH"}` (L33) is read by no tracked file, and the resolver does not use it to check its `trade_type` argument (P02-S011). Four cause codes (L41 to L44): `OK`, `DISABLED` (LAYER 3), `FORCE_BREAKER` (LAYER 0) and `TRADE_TYPE` (LAYER 1 against 2). HISTORICAL: the comment L35 to L40 (`37b3db3`, 2026-06-26, "SLICE2.5-PHASE-4") says the codes let the gate split a trade-type mismatch into its own reject label without string matching, `CAUSE_TRADE_TYPE` being "the ONLY one" promoted to a distinct `TRADE_TYPE` label "so a delivery go-live shows "rejected because trade_type disallows" distinctly"; every other cause keeps `STRATEGY_CONTROL` (P02-S012).
- FACT: `Verdict` is a frozen dataclass with `will_trade: bool`, `reason: str`, `product: Optional[str]` and `cause: str = CAUSE_OK`; it validates nothing (P02-S013).

**Signature, inputs, defaults and the order of evaluation (Q3).**

- FACT: `strategy_will_trade(strategy: Any, *, trade_type: str, force_intraday_only: bool) -> Verdict` (L67 to L72); the two flags are keyword-only with no default (P02-S014). The function reads exactly two attributes of the strategy, `intent = getattr(strategy, "intent", None)` (L76) and `enabled = bool(getattr(strategy, "enabled", True))` (L77); no name, direction, entry window, `active_days`, `v3_playbook`, `pipeline` or `horizon`, and no `delivery_enabled` (P02-S015). A missing `intent` gives `None`, a missing `enabled` gives `True` (current external context, Python built-in functions, read 2026-11-21) (P02-S016).
- FACT: the checks run in this order and the first that matches returns (L79 to L117) (P02-S017):

| Order | Lines | Condition | `will_trade` | `reason` (start) | `product` | `cause` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | L81 to L83 | `not enabled` | False | `WON'T TRADE — switch disabled` | `intent` | `DISABLED` |
| 2 | L90 to L97 | `force_intraday_only and intent == "DELIVERY"` | False | `WON'T TRADE — emergency breaker (force_intraday_only) forces intraday; delivery strategy dormant` | `"INTRADAY"` | `FORCE_BREAKER` |
| 3 | L100 to L106 | `trade_type == "INTRADAY" and intent != "INTRADAY"` | False | `WON'T TRADE — master INTRADAY blocks this DELIVERY strategy` | `intent` | `TRADE_TYPE` |
| 4 | L107 to L113 | `trade_type == "DELIVERY" and intent != "DELIVERY"` | False | `WON'T TRADE — master DELIVERY blocks this INTRADAY strategy` | `intent` | `TRADE_TYPE` |
| 5 | L116 to L117 | none of the above | True | `WILL TRADE — enabled, master allows {intent}` | `intent` | `OK` |

- FACT: the comparisons are exact, case-sensitive string tests with no normalisation; `force_intraday_only` is used by truthiness (P02-S018). Precedence: a disabled strategy always gets `DISABLED`; a DELIVERY strategy under the breaker gets `FORCE_BREAKER` also when `trade_type` is INTRADAY, which alone would give `TRADE_TYPE`; an INTRADAY strategy is never stopped by the breaker branch, and with the breaker on and `trade_type` DELIVERY it gets `TRADE_TYPE` (P02-S019).
- FACT and INTERPRETATION: a `trade_type` other than `"INTRADAY"` or `"DELIVERY"` fails both tests and, for an enabled strategy not stopped by the breaker, reaches WILL TRADE (comment L114: "trade_type == "BOTH", or a matching intent → permitted"); the resolver treats any out-of-set value (a lower-case `"intraday"`, `None`, a typo) like `"BOTH"`, leaving validation to the caller's source (P02-S020). HISTORICAL: two legacy audits recorded this fall-through (`docs/audit/full_system_audit_04july2026.md` L204: it "fails **open** on an unrecognized `trade_type` (mitigated by the config_loader validator)"; `docs/audit/batch_classification_16jul2026.md` L145), and the code was not changed after either (P02-S121).
- INTERPRETATION: with `intent` missing and the strategy enabled, INTRADAY gives `TRADE_TYPE` with "blocks this DELIVERY strategy", DELIVERY gives `TRADE_TYPE` with "blocks this INTRADAY strategy", and BOTH gives WILL TRADE with "master allows None"; FACT: a validated `StrategyConfig` cannot carry such an intent (`intent` required and validated to INTRADAY or DELIVERY; `enabled` a `bool`, default True; `strategies/schema.py` L59, L78, L152 to L159); INTERPRETATION: these paths are therefore reachable only with other objects, such as the test stubs (P02-S021).
- FACT: `product` is `intent` in every branch except the breaker branch, where it is `"INTRADAY"` although `will_trade` is False (L95). HISTORICAL: the `Verdict` docstring (L53 to L57, last changed in `f503130`, 2026-06-24) calls `product` "the EFFECTIVE (post-force-rewrite) product the gate's placement path uses"; `8116b74` (2026-07-10) later removed the load-time intent rewrite (A02 T-13, T-34). FACT: none of the three processor call sites reads `Verdict.product`; the only non-test reader is the configuration auditor (P02-S022).

**Can the resolver raise? (A02 V-06, V-23).**

- FACT: the operations in L76 to L117 are two `getattr` calls with a default, one `bool()`, one `not`, string equality tests joined by `and`, one f-string of `intent` and a frozen dataclass construction (P02-S024). The non-test callers pass the strategies returned by `StrategyLoader.load_all_strategies` (each from `validate_strategy`) or, in the status table, the result of `validate_strategy(path)`; the flags come from the validated `SystemConfig` in the trading process and from a raw YAML read on the status-table route (P02-S025).
- INTERPRETATION (code reading, nothing run): for a validated `StrategyConfig` and a `str` `trade_type` none of these operations raises (current external context, Pydantic standard library types, read 2026-11-21) (P02-S026). On the status-table route `trade_type` is the raw `yaml.safe_load` value, possibly not a `str`; the resolver uses it only in `==` tests, which do not raise for other types (current external context, Python "Value comparisons", read 2026-11-21), so such a value reaches WILL TRADE without a raise for an enabled strategy not stopped by the breaker (P02-S122). Importing `strategies.control` first runs `strategies/__init__.py`, which imports A02's schema and loader; the deferred imports at `main.py` L3167 and `core/config_auditor.py` L268 and L306 can fail only if those imports fail (current external context, the Python import system, read 2026-11-21) (P02-S027).
- FACT: no test expects the resolver to raise. UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED: whether it or its import ever raised in production (P02-S028).

**The seven call sites and how each uses the verdict or a raise (Q2, Q3; interface only).**

- FACT: the tracked non-test callers are seven call sites in four files: `signals/signal_processor.py` L959, L2062, L2411 (module import L58 to L61, which also imports `CAUSE_TRADE_TYPE`); `main.py` L3172 (deferred import L3167); `core/config_auditor.py` L275 and L309 (deferred imports L268, L306); `scripts/strategy_status.py` L86 (P02-S029). The docstring names two importers; `scripts/cron_officer.py` reaches the resolver only through `scripts.strategy_status` (P02-S030).
- FACT (`_process_one`, A06's file): the check runs after A06's strategy lookup (L933 to L953) and before the per-strategy entry window (L977 to L982, A01), the governor (L985 to L990, A09) and the screening call (L998); the signal is already `PROCESSING` (L897) (P02-S031). Its inputs are `strategy_obj` from the processor's copy of the loaded strategies and the two processor attributes set only at construction from `app_config.system` (`main.py` L3620 to L3621; constructor defaults `"INTRADAY"` and `False`); no non-test code reassigns them (P02-S032). A verdict that is not `will_trade` raises `_PipelineReject` with the check `TRADE_TYPE` when the cause is `CAUSE_TRADE_TYPE`, else `STRATEGY_CONTROL`, and the verdict's reason; a will-trade verdict keeps nothing (P02-S033). HISTORICAL: the processor's comments call it the "strategy-control gate (LAYERS 1+3)"; FACT: the call also passes `force_intraday_only` and so applies LAYER 0 (P02-S118).
- FACT: the reject handler (L1193 to L1231) logs, counts `entries_rejected`, writes `REJECTED_TRADE_TYPE` or `REJECTED_STRATEGY_CONTROL` with the verdict's text (L1201), makes the `P2_REJECT` evidence capture (added after `7d4970a`), releases a reservation only if one exists (none exists at this point) and tallies the check (P02-S034). A raise inside the resolver would be caught by the generic handler (L1233 to L1246) and written `PLACEMENT_FAILED` with the exception text; INTERPRETATION: a raise would thus be recorded as a placement failure although no placement was attempted (P02-S035).
- FACT (the resume paths, A06's file; D-39 P2): `continue_from_gate` sets `PROCESSING` (L2034), checks the kill switch, the global window and the shadow guard, looks up `entry.strategy_name` (`UNKNOWN_STRATEGY` if absent), then calls the resolver with the same attributes and label mapping (L2062 to L2075) before the per-strategy window (L2078) and the governor (L2088); its reject handler writes `REJECTED_<check>` (L2309 to L2338), a raise `PLACEMENT_FAILED` (L2340 to L2345) (P02-S036). `continue_from_retest` does the same with `parked.strategy` (L2387 to L2418; reject L2594 to L2614; raise L2616 to L2620) (P02-S037). INTERPRETATION: the three sites pass the same attributes and loaded objects, so within one process run a strategy gets the same verdict on all three, and a file change after boot reaches none of them (P02-S038). FACT: on the resume paths the direction comes from the parked object, and the resolver reads no direction on any path (P02-S039).
- HISTORICAL: the processor's comment L213 to L216 says "the two resume-path dispatch sites are production-unreachable". FACT and UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, in the fixed wording: `EntryGate.add()` has no non-test caller at the baseline, so the normal live path that creates a new gate entry is not established; `EntryGate.start()` rehydrates any persisted `gate_state` rows and can poll and release them; whether such rows existed in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. The reachability of `continue_from_retest` is A08's; whether L2062 or L2411 ever executed is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P02-S040).
- FACT (boot summary, `main.py`, A01's file): after the strategy load, a `try` block evaluates every loaded strategy in name order with `app_config.system.trade_type` and `force_intraday_only` (no `v3_playbook` filter) and logs INFO `strategy_control.summary` with both counts and lists; any exception gives a WARNING `strategy_control.summary_failed` and start-up continues; nothing reads the result (P02-S041). INTERPRETATION: at the file values the summary would list 15 will-trade strategies and `pb01_breakout_retest (WON'T TRADE — switch disabled)` (P02-S042).
- FACT (configuration auditor, A02's file): probe A5 (L266 to L297), when strategies are given and `delivery_enabled` is truthy and conditional allocation off, evaluates every strategy (`trade_type` by `getattr` with default `None`, the breaker with default False) and adds a BLOCK when the will-trade verdicts all have `product == "DELIVERY"`; probe A3 (L304 to L319) warns when no strategy will trade; both end in `except Exception: pass` (P02-S043, P02-S044). A5 is the only non-test reader of `Verdict.product`, whose comment calls it "the EFFECTIVE post-coercion product"; FACT: for a will-trade verdict it equals the declared intent; INTERPRETATION: under Option A the product A5 tests is the declared intent (P02-S045). INTERPRETATION (A07's side of A02 V-06): the two silent handlers would be reached through strategy control only by a failure of the deferred import (P02-S046); (A07's side of A02 V-23) at the file values A5 finds 15 will-trade strategies, not all DELIVERY, and A3 finds 15, so neither fires (P02-S047).
- FACT (status table, P07's script; caller A16's): `build_status_rows` validates every strategy file at its own run time (a failing file becomes a `CONFIG ERROR` row), skips `v3_playbook` strategies ("Keep this table = the 15 live strategies"), calls the resolver outside that `try`, and shows type, master, switch, verdict, reason and the taxonomy category, but not the cause (P02-S048). Its caller reads `trade_type` (default `"INTRADAY"`) and `force_intraday_only` (default True, through `bool()`) from the raw YAML and catches any exception as a WARNING with no table (P02-S049); INTERPRETATION: a raise would lose the whole table (P02-S050). FACT: the table and the gate differ in four ways: the file read time, the flag source and defaults (raw YAML without the validator against the validated `SystemConfig`), the omission of playbook strategies, and neither reading `delivery_enabled` (P02-S051). INTERPRETATION: the docstrings' "can NEVER disagree" holds at the baseline file values; the two can differ when a file changes after boot, when a raw value is of another type than the validated one, or when `trade_type` is out of set (P02-S052). FACT (stated expectations, A19, never run): the strategy-control tests expect `REJECTED_STRATEGY_CONTROL` and `REJECTED_TRADE_TYPE` at the processor sites and the table to equal the resolver (P02-S053).

**The resolver's inputs at the A02 boundary (Q3, Q4).**

- FACT: `StrategyConfig` declares `intent: str` (required, validated to INTRADAY or DELIVERY) and `enabled: bool = True` ("Per-strategy master switch read by the strategy-control resolver"), with `extra="forbid"` and no `frozen` (P02-S054). Of the 16 strategy files, 12 declare `intent: "INTRADAY"` and `enabled: true`, 3 (`positional_momentum_long`, `positional_sector_rotation`, `positional_swing_long`) `intent: "DELIVERY"` and `enabled: true`, and `pb01_breakout_retest` `intent: "INTRADAY"`, `enabled: false` and `v3_playbook: true`; every file declares `enabled` explicitly; the values in force are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P02-S055). The six SHORT files are enabled and controlled exactly like LONG ones; strategy control does not read `direction` (A04 CG-05's A07 side) (P02-S126).
- FACT: the strategy objects passed to the resolver in the trading process come only from the load at `main.py` L3158 to L3162; the second boot load in `utils/startup_checks.py` (without `force_intraday_only`) validates only; at the baseline the flag changes only a log line in the loader, so both loads give equal objects (A01 C11; A02 AUDIT L1036); INTERPRETATION: the double load has no effect on the verdicts (P02-S056). No non-test code assigns `.enabled` or `.intent`, calls `setattr` or `model_copy` on a strategy, or writes a strategy file; HISTORICAL: `8116b74` dropped the loader's `model_copy(intent->INTRADAY)` rewrite (P02-S057).
- FACT: `SystemConfig` declares `force_intraday_only: bool = True`, `delivery_enabled: bool = False` and `trade_type: str = "INTRADAY"` with a validator refusing values outside the three, and its model validator fails start-up on the breaker plus `trade_type: DELIVERY` contradiction (A02 V-20) (P02-S058). File values: `force_intraday_only: false` (L142), `trade_type: BOTH` (L149), `delivery_enabled: true` (L155); HISTORICAL: the comment above L142 says the breaker forces every strategy to INTRADAY at load time (superseded by Option A, A02 T-13) and the comment above L155 says `delivery_enabled` stays false until a proof passes (A02 IC-06 to IC-08) (P02-S059). HISTORICAL: these three value lines date from `0197923` (2026-08-04); earlier texts call `trade_type=INTRADAY` with the breaker true "CURRENT", and a later comment (2026-08-22) says the breaker is false, delivery enabled and `trade_type` BOTH "and delivery has traded"; INTERPRETATION: under the earlier configuration the three DELIVERY strategies would get `FORCE_BREAKER`, under the later one WILL TRADE (P02-S063); UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED: which configuration was in force on which day (P02-S123; P02-U01).
- FACT: the routes and their defaults when a value is missing (P02-S060):

| Route | Where the flags are read | `trade_type` default | `force_intraday_only` default | Validated? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Processor (three sites) | `main.py` L3620 to L3621 from `app_config.system` | `SystemConfig` "INTRADAY"; constructor "INTRADAY" | `SystemConfig` True; constructor False | yes |
| Boot summary | `main.py` L3168 to L3169 | as `SystemConfig` | as `SystemConfig` | yes |
| Auditor A5, A3 | `core/config_auditor.py` L207, L272, L307 by `getattr` | `None` | False | as the caller gives |
| Status table | `scripts/cron_officer.py` L474 to L475, raw YAML | "INTRADAY" | True | no |
| Loader | `strategies/loader.py` L106 (parameter) | not an input | False | log only |

- FACT: the processor receives no `delivery_enabled` and the resolver has none; HISTORICAL: the loader docstring and `core/config_loader.py` L2031 to L2036 place the CNC lock at the broker (A04's); INTERPRETATION: with `delivery_enabled` false, the breaker off and `trade_type` BOTH or DELIVERY, an enabled DELIVERY strategy passes strategy control everywhere and is refused only at the broker; at the file values (`delivery_enabled: true`) the case does not arise (A02 K-05 records the different predicates) (P02-S061). HISTORICAL: `core/config_loader.py` L2038 to L2043 and the field comment L2030 still describe an intent rewrite; neither is among the texts A02 T-13 lists (A07's observation only) (P02-S062).
- INTERPRETATION (own reading at the file values, nothing run): with the breaker off and `trade_type` BOTH, 15 strategies get WILL TRADE and `pb01_breakout_retest` WON'T TRADE with `DISABLED`; the status table would show 15 rows, all WILL TRADE; under the shipped defaults (`trade_type` INTRADAY, breaker true) the three DELIVERY strategies would get `FORCE_BREAKER`, not `TRADE_TYPE` (P02-S063). FACT (A01 C11's A07 side): every route that calls the resolver reads the configured `force_intraday_only`; only the status table reads it without the validator (P02-S127).

**How the strategy is identified, typed and labelled (Q1; interface only after A06's lookup, D-38).**

- FACT (A06's lookup, interface only): the processor takes the scan-map entry for `scanner_name` (L933), the strategy name from it (L939 to L942) and `strategy_obj = self._strategies.get(strategy_name)` (L948); each miss raises `UNKNOWN_STRATEGY`. From L959 on, A07's logic receives only `strategy_obj`; `scanner_name` reaches A07 again as an argument of the screening call (P02-S064). The map model allows any scanner key to name any strategy; in the repository file the 16 keys equal their strategy names one for one, and one entry (`pb01_breakout_retest`) has `scanner_type: eod`; INTERPRETATION: no alias maps two scanners to one strategy at the baseline file (P02-S065). FACT: the processor never reads `scanner_type`, and the resolver reads neither the scanner nor the map; the only "type" strategy control applies is `intent` against `trade_type`; there is no per-scanner, per-symbol or per-time control input (P02-S066). FACT: the loader's S10 cross-check of the map runs only when the map path is given, which `main.py` does not do (A06 STUDY P04-S031, cross-referenced) (P02-S067).
- FACT (A06's receiver, interface only): a scanner of `scanner_type` "eod" is handed to `_handle_eod` and never enqueued; the only scanner mapping to `pb01_breakout_retest` is of that type; INTERPRETATION: a PB-01 webhook does not reach `_process_one`, so its `enabled: false` verdict acts there as a second lock; whether a PB-01 item could reach a resume path is A18's and A08's to establish (P02-S120).
- HISTORICAL and FACT (A06 PQ-14 and XA-11): both rows recorded the lookup's owner as OPEN FOR RAMA; D-38 decided reading (b), so A07 records the lookup at the interface only; XA-11's other A07 point, the delivery predicate (K-05), is recorded in P02-S051, P02-S061 and P02-AP09; neither row is reclassified or re-owned (P02-S128).

**`strategies/taxonomy.py` (Q2).**

- HISTORICAL: the docstring says "METADATA + DISPLAY ONLY": it formats the declared `pipeline` and `horizon` fields for the status table and the end-of-day report, with "NO routing, NO execution, NO capital split"; the schema calls the two fields "Deliberately INERT" (P02-S068).
- FACT: `taxonomy_label(pipeline, horizon)` returns `"{p}·{h}"` with `"—"` for a falsy value and reads no file (P02-S069). `build_taxonomy_map(config_dir="config")` validates every strategy file and maps name to `(pipeline, horizon)`, returning `{}` if the directory is absent and skipping a failing file; INTERPRETATION: the relative default resolves against the working directory (P02-S070). Its two non-test consumers are the status table's `category` column and A15's daily report (L1518 to L1519; L1604 to L1605) (P02-S071); HISTORICAL: the report's call passed no argument before 2026-07-25 (P02-S072). FACT: nothing else reads the `pipeline` or `horizon` attribute; the "pipeline" values in `main.py` are derived separately by A01's `_resolve_position_pipeline`; INTERPRETATION: the taxonomy takes no part in strategy control, screening or any trading decision (P02-S073). File values: `pipeline` equals `intent` in all 16 files; `horizon` is `SAME_DAY`, `SWING` or `NEXT_DAY` (PB-01) (P02-S074).

**Strategy status, enablement and lifecycle states; PAPER_ONLY (Q4).**

- FACT: the only per-strategy states strategy control reads are `enabled` and `intent`; both are set and persisted only in the strategy files, read at load and enforced at the three processor sites; nothing changes either at run time (P02-S075). The resolver has no input for any other state (PAPER_ONLY, shadow, demoted, paused, retired, registration) and no mode value (P02-S076).
- FACT: `PAPER_ONLY` occurs in tracked files outside `docs/` and `reports/` only in `scripts/compute_strategy_metrics.py`: its docstring L8 ("auto-sets strategy to PAPER_ONLY") and its alert text L224 ("Strategy set to PAPER_ONLY."); no module of the trading path contains it (P02-S077). The script's `_check_demotion` reads the latest `sharpe` rows, logs CRITICAL and, only when a notifier is passed, sends a "STRATEGY DEMOTED" alert, and writes no strategy state; its only write is `INSERT OR REPLACE INTO strategy_metrics` (section 12; P07) (P02-S078). No module of the trading path reads `strategy_metrics`; INTERPRETATION: "Strategy set to PAPER_ONLY" corresponds to no state the resolver, processor or screener reads, and a demoted strategy would keep trading if its file stayed `enabled: true`; whether the alert ever fired is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P02-S079). HISTORICAL: the legacy audit `docs/audit/audit_05jul2026.md` L445 recorded the same as "a dead safety net that claims otherwise"; no tracked text defines what a PAPER_ONLY state would mean (P02-S119).
- FACT (other per-strategy states, interface only): `v3_playbook` (true only for PB-01) is read by the processor for the allocator candidate (A18), by `main.py` and by the status table, not by the resolver; HISTORICAL: the PB-01 file header states that `enabled: false` makes the resolver return `CAUSE_DISABLED`, which matches the code (P02-S080). The strategy governor's pause (`STRATEGY_CIRCUIT_BREAKER`) is a separate state checked after strategy control and the per-strategy window; its logic is A09's (P02-S081). The direction registry's `registration_status` and `health` are read by no trading-path module (P02-S082; section 12).
- FACT (A15's dashboard, interface only): the dashboard reads `enabled` from the raw files with differing defaults for an absent key, without effect at the file values, which all declare it (P02-S083); its control client asks for `/control/status`, which no tracked trading-side code implements; HISTORICAL: `UNPUSHED_LEDGER.md` records that the control plane was "NEVER BUILT ON THE TRADING SIDE"; INTERPRETATION: there is no run-time route to switch a strategy on or off inside the trading process; the switch is the file value read at boot (P02-S084).
- INTERPRETATION (consistency of the routes): the three processor routes and the boot summary apply the same rule to the same validated inputs; the auditor uses its own `getattr` defaults; the status table uses fresh file reads and raw flags and omits the playbook strategy; the routes agree at the baseline file values and can differ only under the conditions of P02-S052 (P02-S085).

**The strategy's own entry window and A01's window control (Q5; interface only).**

- FACT: on all three processor paths the order is the global window (L911; L2043; L2400), then strategy control, then the per-strategy window `is_entry_allowed_for_strategy` (L977; L2078; L2420); both windows reject with the same label `OUTSIDE_ENTRY_WINDOW` and differ only in the reason text (P02-S086). FACT (A01's `core/market_windows.py`): the per-strategy function returns False when the global window is closed, parses the strategy's `entry_start_time` and `entry_end_time`, falls back to the global window on a parse error, and tests `start <= t < end` (P02-S087).
- FACT and INTERPRETATION: strategy control reads no window or `active_days` field and the window function reads neither `enabled` nor `intent`; the two checks are independent, and because control runs first a strategy that will not trade is labelled `TRADE_TYPE` or `STRATEGY_CONTROL` even outside its window; the global window is tested twice on each path (P02-S088). The status table shows each strategy's start and end, but its verdict ignores the window; a WILL TRADE row means the control layers allow the strategy, not that its window is open (P02-S089).
- FACT (file values): 15 strategy files declare 09:25 to 15:00 and PB-01 09:25 to 11:00; HISTORICAL: comments give `gap_fade_long` an 11:30 cut-off its file does not have (A02 T-11 hands the docstring to A01) and the PB-01 file says its authoritative window is the watchlist stage's (A02 T-38) (P02-S090). FACT: the global window passed to the processor is 10:00 to 15:00 at the file values; INTERPRETATION: the per-strategy test then changes no outcome for the 15 strategies, and only PB-01's 11:00 end would narrow it, while PB-01's webhook does not reach the processor; HISTORICAL: a legacy audit called the difference "cosmetic; global floor binds"; the values in force are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P02-S124).

**The control-layer vocabulary and how it becomes statuses (Q16).**

- FACT: the mapping from the resolver's outcome to every surface (P02-S091):

| Cause | Processor check label | `signals.status` | Reject reason | Tally key | Boot summary | Status table | Auditor |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `OK` | none | unchanged by control | none | none | name in `will_trade` | `WILL TRADE`, `ENABLED` | will trade; `product` tested by A5 |
| `DISABLED` | `STRATEGY_CONTROL` | `REJECTED_STRATEGY_CONTROL` | `WON'T TRADE — switch disabled` | `STRATEGY_CONTROL` | `name (reason)` | `WON'T TRADE`, `DISABLED` | not will trade |
| `FORCE_BREAKER` | `STRATEGY_CONTROL` | `REJECTED_STRATEGY_CONTROL` | `WON'T TRADE — emergency breaker ...` | `STRATEGY_CONTROL` | `name (reason)` | `WON'T TRADE`, `ENABLED` | not will trade |
| `TRADE_TYPE` | `TRADE_TYPE` | `REJECTED_TRADE_TYPE` | `WON'T TRADE — master ... blocks this ... strategy` | `TRADE_TYPE` | `name (reason)` | `WON'T TRADE`, `ENABLED` | not will trade |

- FACT: `STRATEGY_CONTROL` is a string literal at the three processor sites; `TRADE_TYPE` is the value of `CAUSE_TRADE_TYPE` (P02-S092). Two causes, `DISABLED` and `FORCE_BREAKER`, become the same status and differ only in the reason text; the table shows no cause; HISTORICAL: the comment L35 to L40 calls the generic label for every other cause deliberate (P02-S093). The file schema's status constraint accepts any `REJECTED*` value; the production schema is A03's (P02-S094). The release reasons `rejected_trade_type` and `rejected_strategy_control` are never produced, because no reservation exists at a strategy-control reject on any path (P02-S095).
- FACT (A15's dashboard reader, interface only): `REJECTED_TRADE_TYPE` is listed among the dashboard's risk-reject statuses, while `REJECTED_STRATEGY_CONTROL` is in neither named family and counts only as a generic reject; INTERPRETATION: a trade-type mismatch is counted with risk rejects and a disabled or breaker-dormant strategy outside both families (P02-S096). The status-table vocabulary is `WILL TRADE`, `WON'T TRADE`, `CONFIG ERROR`, `ENABLED` or `DISABLED` and `?`; the verdict's "WILL TRADE" and "WON'T TRADE" prefixes also appear in the stored reject reason (P02-S097).

**The package initialisers `strategies/__init__.py` and `screening/__init__.py`.**

- FACT: `strategies/__init__.py` (unchanged since `bcf03b5`) re-exports A02's `StrategyConfig`, `validate_strategy` and `StrategyLoader` and does not name `strategies.control` or `strategies.taxonomy`, added later (P02-S114); `screening/__init__.py` re-exports `QualityScorer`, `ScoreResult`, `StepExecutor`, `StepExecutorResult`, `SecondaryScreener` and `ScreeningResult`, not `HardGate`, `EntryGate`, `WatchEntry` or the retest monitor (P02-S115). No tracked file imports either package by its name; every importer names a submodule (P02-S116). INTERPRETATION: the two files act only through their import side effects (each submodule import first runs the package file and so imports the schema and loader, or the scorer, executor and screener with their own imports, current external context); their export lists are used by no tracked file (P02-S117; section 14).

**Inherited items on this section's side** (section 16 has every row): A01 C11 (P02-S056, P02-S127); A02 V-06 (P02-S025, P02-S046), V-23 (P02-S047), T-14 (P02-S125: the resolver reads exactly `intent` and `enabled`; `active_days` has no non-test reader outside the schema's validator) and K-05 (P02-S051, P02-S061, P02-S123); A04 CG-05 (P02-S126); A06 PQ-14 and XA-11 (P02-S064, P02-S067, P02-S128). A02 F-11 and A03 E-08 are in section 12 (P02-S107, P02-S129).

## 4. The secondary screener: routes in order, status strings, `ScreeningResult` and persistence calls (questions Q6, Q7, Q10, Q11, Q13)

This section covers `screening/secondary_screener.py` as the orchestrator of screening: its construction and stored state, `ScreeningResult`, every route of `screen()` in order, the status strings and the stored reason, `_persist` and the evidence capture, `shutdown`, failure handling, and the caller's contract at the D-38 start line (interface only). Under ADDENDUM 3 of the part briefs, the content of `_build_market_data` and the steps are in section 5 (P04), and the internals of the scorer, the HardGate and the V3 methods in sections 6 and 7 (P05); here they appear at the call level. The statuses across all components and their readers are in section 9.

Evidence: `A07_SECONDARY_SCREENER_ORCHESTRATION_AND_RESULT_2026-11-21.md` (P03), which read the 705-line file (17 commits, the last `056905b`, 2026-09-11) in whole (P03-S002); after `7d4970a` only the evidence wiring changed, so the decision routes recorded here are those of the last recorded deployment commit as well (P03-S003; section 1.8).

**Declared purpose and stated intent.**

- HISTORICAL: the file header says the module "Orchestrates the 10-step screening pipeline: fetches market data, runs step_executor, scores via quality_scorer, applies strategy min_score override, persists result per P18" under locked decisions SS1 to SS15, P9a and P18 (P03-S009); the class docstring states SS1 ("Pure orchestration"), SS2 (five constructor arguments), SS3 (`screen() -> ScreeningResult`) and SS12 ("No broker import; quote_fn injected") (P03-S010); `screen()`'s docstring states SS4 (the pipeline order) and SS5 ("DB failures logged but do not prevent returning ScreeningResult") (P03-S011). The module instruction 29 (`bcf03b5`, 2026-04-17) states P18 ("Every signal leaves with PASSED/REJECTED_<step>/SKIPPED_<reason>. No silent drops"), the SS4 eight-step order (with a step-error reject only for a step of weight above 0, `REJECTED_SCORE_<total_score>` and a "defense-in-depth" signal-age check), SS5's writes with `reason=rejected_step or "PASSED"`, SS8's circuit states "normal" and "unknown", the SS10 error routes and SS11's INFO line for every call (P03-S012). The later routes carry their own intent comments (the MIS learned blocklist, 2026-06-30; the pre-fill circuit-proximity reject, 2026-06-23; the V3 Hard-Gate modes, 2026-07-12; the quiet no-quote skip, 2026-07-14; the evidence capture, 2026-09-10) (P03-S013).

**Construction and stored state.**

- FACT: the constructor takes five arguments without defaults (`step_executor`, `quality_scorer`, `state_store`, `quote_fn`, `logger`) and eight with defaults (`circuit_proximity_reject_enabled=True`, `mis_blocklist=None`, `mis_filter_enabled=False`, `mis_filter_shadow=True`, `resolve_product=None`, `hard_gate=None`, `scoring_config=None`, `evidence=None`); SS2 names the first five only (P03-S014). From `scoring_config` it keeps the V3 mode (`getattr` default `"off"`), the gate steps (default `circuit_check`, `signal_age`), the V3 minimum and tier thresholds (defaults 50, 75, 56) and the step weights (`{}` without a configuration) (P03-S015); these defaults equal the `ScoringConfig` field defaults, whose validator accepts exactly `off`, `shadow` and `enforce` (P03-S016).
- FACT: every instance attribute is assigned in the constructor only; `screen()` takes no lock and changes no state of its own except the effect counter, the two store writes, the evidence capture and the log lines; the components it calls change their own state (the rate limiter's token on the live quote fetch, the HardGate's and the scorer's effect counters) (P03-S017).
- FACT (interface, `main.py` L3281 to L3298, A01's file): the only non-test construction passes the step executor (built with `logger` and `market_open` only), the scorer, the store, `broker_adapter.get_quote`, `entry_gate.circuit_proximity_reject_enabled`, the MIS blocklist with `mis_filter.enabled` and `mis_filter.shadow`, a product-resolver closure, the hard gate (built unconditionally), `app_config.scoring` and the evidence recorder (`None` if its construction raised); the construction itself is not wrapped (P03-S018). FACT about the committed files only: `v3_hardgate_mode: "shadow"`, `v3_gate_steps: [circuit_check, signal_age]`, V3 minimum and thresholds 50, 75, 56, `min_pass_score: 60`, `mis_filter.enabled: true`, `shadow: true` ("DO NOT set shadow: false without Rama"), `circuit_proximity_reject_enabled: true`; INTERPRETATION: at these values the screener would not take the enforce branch, would run the shadow comparison and would take the MIS shadow fall-through; the values in force are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P03-S019).
- FACT: the circuit-proximity switch is held twice, by the screener and by the HardGate, both fed from the same key (P03-S020). Three module imports are unused: `DEFAULT_CIRCUIT_MARGIN_PCT` (its comment calls it the "single source of truth for the circuit-band margin"; it is used in `screening/hard_gate.py` L85), `Decimal` and `date` (P03-S021).

**`ScreeningResult` (Q7).**

- FACT: a frozen dataclass with ten fields in SS3's order: `passed`, `status`, `score` ("0-100"), `tier`, `rejected_step`, `step_results`, `step_statuses`, `error_steps`, `latencies_ms`, `market_data_snapshot` (P03-S022). The `step_statuses` comment lists three values; the executor also writes `"TIMEOUT"` (P03-S023). `_make_skipped` builds every `SKIPPED_*` result with `score=0`, `tier="LOW"`, empty step data and the given snapshot; its `signal_id` parameter is unused (P03-S024).
- FACT (invariants by construction): `passed=True` only with `status="PASSED"`; every result built before the scorer or the re-scale runs carries `score=0` and `tier="LOW"` as fixed values; `rejected_step` is `None` for `PASSED`, `REJECTED_SCORE_<n>` and every skip (P03-S025). Not carried by the result: the effective minimum score (passed to `_persist` only), the proximity reason text, the HardGate's evidence, the V3 mode, the executor's `rejected_at`, the scorer's `passed`, `step_scores` and `missing_steps`, the MIS block date and the quote's time stamp (P03-S026). The step data are the executor's own objects passed by reference, and the snapshot is a shallow copy taken once after the fetch (`{}` on the two quote skips and the active MIS reject) (P03-S027).

**`screen()`: every route in order (Q6, Q10, Q13).**

- FACT: `screen(signal_id, symbol, scanner_name, trigger_price, triggered_at, direction, intent, strategy, market_data=None) -> ScreeningResult`, as SS3 states it (P03-S028). The only non-test call is `signals/signal_processor.py` L998 to L1004 with `market_data=None`, so the route on which a caller supplies market data is reached only from tests (P03-S029). The routes, in order: R1 the MIS learned-blocklist pre-drop; R2 the quote fetch and `_build_market_data`; the snapshot; the V3 enforce switch (to E1 to E7); R3 the pre-fill circuit-proximity reject; the thresholds and the signal dictionary; R4 the executor; R5 the step-error reject; R6 the scorer and the V3 shadow comparison; R7 the minimum score; R8 the signal age; R9 PASSED. Each ending route builds one result, calls `_persist` once and returns (P03-S030).
- FACT (R1, MIS pre-drop): runs only when the filter is enabled and the blocklist injected, first ("pure symbol/intent lookup") (P03-S031). The helper resolves the product for the strategy's `intent` (a resolver error returns "not blocked", fail-open), returns False for a product other than `MIS`, asks A12's blocklist `is_blocked(symbol)`, and on a block logs one WARNING with the mode, date, days and direction and returns True in both modes (P03-S032). In active mode the result is `REJECTED_NOT_MIS_TRADABLE` with `rejected_step="mis_tradable"` and an empty snapshot (P03-S033); in shadow mode nothing is built or written and the call continues, the only trace being the WARNING whose text reads `REJECTED_NOT_MIS_TRADABLE (shadow)` although the signal is not rejected (P03-S034). The blocklist calls and the WARNING are not inside a `try`; A12's blocklist is an in-memory dictionary loaded from `data_store/mis_blocklist.json`, with a symbol blocked while the days since its date are fewer than `ttl_days` (file value 1) (P03-S035). Without a resolver the blocklist alone decides; `main.py` always passes one (P03-S036). HISTORICAL: the configuration comment explaining the shadow setting cites line numbers that differ from the baseline; its description matches the code (P03-S037).
- FACT (R2, quote): a `None` quote for the symbol gives `SKIPPED_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` with an INFO line (HISTORICAL: "an EXPECTED, benign skip that fires ~hundreds of times a session") (P03-S038); any exception from `quote_fn`, `.get` or `_build_market_data` gives the same status with an ERROR line and traceback (P03-S039); INTERPRETATION: the two causes give the same status, reason and empty snapshot and differ only in the log (P03-S040). FACT (A04's adapter, interface): the live `get_quote` first takes a rate-limiter token (which can raise `BrokerRateLimitError`, `RateLimitAbortedError` at shutdown, or `ValueError`) and re-raises translated broker errors, all caught at L185 (P03-S041); INTERPRETATION: a rate-limit refusal of the screening quote ends the signal as a skip and is not retried, the processor's FIX-069 retry covering placement only (P03-S042). `_build_market_data` and the circuit state are at the call level here; their content is in section 5 (P04; P03-S043, P03-S044 record the same lines). FACT: the screener has no positive-price test on the last price, and makes no test of the quote's age (P03-S045, P03-S046). The snapshot is taken after R2 and stored by every later result (P03-S047).
- FACT (the V3 enforce switch, call level): when the mode is `"enforce"` and a HardGate is injected, the call returns `_screen_v3_enforce(...)` (HISTORICAL: "default off never here"); at the committed `"shadow"` the branch is not taken (P03-S048). The enforce routes, in order: E1 `HardGate.evaluate`, whose failure writes `REJECTED_<reason>` (`AT_CIRCUIT`, `CIRCUIT_PROXIMITY` or `SIGNAL_AGE`) with `score=0`, `tier="LOW"` and `rejected_step` the reason in lower case; E2 the same thresholds; E3 `run_all` without the `v3_gate_steps` (`SKIPPED_EXECUTOR_ERROR` on an exception); E4 `REJECTED_STEP_ERROR` on any error step, with no log line; E5 `rescaled_total` and `tier_for`; E6 `REJECTED_SCORE_<total>` below the minimum, with no log line; E7 `PASSED` with an INFO line ending "(v3-enforce)" (P03-S049, P03-S050). On this branch the scorer is not called, R3 and R8 do not run (the gate's own copies take their place), and there is no `try` around `evaluate` or the three re-scale helpers (P03-S051). The minimum is `rescale_min_score(strategy.min_score)` when positive, otherwise `v3_min_pass_score` (file value 50); INTERPRETATION: a positive `min_score` that re-scales to 0 leaves no failing total (P03-S052). The internals are in section 7 (P05).
- FACT (R3, pre-fill circuit proximity): on the OFF and shadow paths the screener calls the shared rule `circuit_proximity_reason(trigger_price, direction, market_data, enabled=...)`; a reason gives `REJECTED_CIRCUIT_PROXIMITY` with `rejected_step="circuit_proximity"` and a WARNING with the reason text (P03-S053). The rule reads the scanner's `trigger_price`, the direction and the quote's circuit limits, outside any `try`; HISTORICAL: "Hard reject, not a soft score — runs before the 10-step pipeline", relocated to `screening/hard_gate.py` so that "the OFF path is byte-identical" (P03-S054; section 7 for the arithmetic). On the shadow path the comparison later evaluates the gate's own copy of the rule again, for the log only (P03-S055).
- FACT (thresholds): `{"min_volume_surge", "min_adr_pct", "max_spread_pct"}` from the strategy object and a six-key signal dictionary, outside any `try` (P03-S056). The screener reads exactly four strategy fields (`min_volume_surge`, `min_adr_pct`, `max_spread_pct`, `min_score`) and `name`, and none of the fields A02 T-14 lists as unread (P03-S057). FACT about A02's schema and files: defaults `min_score: 0` ("0 = use global"), `min_volume_surge: 1.3`, `min_adr_pct: 0.005`, `max_spread_pct: 0.005`; in the 16 files `min_score: 0`, `min_adr_pct: 0.005` and `max_spread_pct: 0.005` everywhere and `min_volume_surge` 1.2 to 2.0 (P03-S058).
- FACT (R4, executor): `run_all(signal_dict, market_data, thresholds)` with all ten steps; an exception gives `SKIPPED_EXECUTOR_ERROR` with an ERROR line; HISTORICAL: the executor states "run_all() never raises" (P03-S059). (R5) any non-empty `error_steps` gives `REJECTED_STEP_ERROR` with the joined names and a WARNING ("FIX-169 F40"); there is no test of the step's weight, against SS4's "AND that step has weight > 0" (P03-S060). A timed-out step is scored 0.5 with status `TIMEOUT` and is not an error step, so it does not reach R5 (P03-S061).
- FACT (R6, scorer): an exception gives `SKIPPED_SCORER_ERROR`; otherwise the total and tier are taken; then, in shadow mode with a gate, `_log_v3_shadow_compare` evaluates the gate and the re-scale inside one `try`, logs one INFO line and changes neither the result nor any write (P03-S062). INTERPRETATION: the comparison is logged only for signals that reached the scorer without a step error, before the minimum-score decision (P03-S063).
- FACT (R7, minimum): `effective_min = strategy.min_score if strategy.min_score > 0 else score_result.min_pass_score`; `total_score < effective_min` (strict, so a total equal to the minimum passes) gives `REJECTED_SCORE_<total_score>` with the scorer's score and tier, persisted with `eligible_score=effective_min` and no log line, against SS11 (P03-S064). The global minimum is the scorer's `min_pass_score` from `app_config.scoring` (file value 60); INTERPRETATION: at the committed files it applies to every strategy (P03-S065).
- FACT (R8, signal age): `step_results.get("signal_age", 1.0) == 0.0` (exact float equality; 1.0 when absent) gives `REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE` with the scorer's score and tier and no log line; it is reached only by a signal whose total met the minimum (P03-S066). INTERPRETATION: a stale signal below the minimum is recorded `REJECTED_SCORE_<n>`; apart from R8, a step that returned 0.0 never ends the call by itself, because the screener reads neither `rejected_at` nor the step statuses (A19's test comments state the same expectation) (P03-S067).
- FACT (R9): `PASSED` with the scorer's score and tier, persisted with `eligible_score`, then one INFO line (the write precedes the log) (P03-S068).
- FACT (summary of the routes) (P03-S069):

| Route | Condition | Status written | Score / tier | `rejected_step` | `eligible_score` | Log |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 active | filter on, not shadow, product MIS (or no resolver), symbol blocked | `REJECTED_NOT_MIS_TRADABLE` | 0 / LOW | `mis_tradable` | None | WARNING |
| R1 shadow | as above, shadow | none (continues) | — | — | — | WARNING "(shadow)" |
| R2 no quote | quote `None` | `SKIPPED_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` | 0 / LOW | None | None | INFO |
| R2 error | fetch or build raised | `SKIPPED_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` | 0 / LOW | None | None | ERROR + traceback |
| E1 | enforce; gate fails | `REJECTED_AT_CIRCUIT`, `REJECTED_CIRCUIT_PROXIMITY` or `REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE` | 0 / LOW | reason in lower case | None | WARNING |
| E3 | enforce; executor raised | `SKIPPED_EXECUTOR_ERROR` | 0 / LOW | None | None | ERROR + traceback |
| E4 | enforce; step errors | `REJECTED_STEP_ERROR` | 0 / LOW | joined names | None | none |
| E6 | enforce; total below minimum | `REJECTED_SCORE_<total>` | total / V3 tier | None | `eff_min` | none |
| E7 | enforce; otherwise | `PASSED` | total / V3 tier | None | `eff_min` | INFO "(v3-enforce)" |
| R3 | rule returns a reason | `REJECTED_CIRCUIT_PROXIMITY` | 0 / LOW | `circuit_proximity` | None | WARNING |
| R4 | executor raised | `SKIPPED_EXECUTOR_ERROR` | 0 / LOW | None | None | ERROR + traceback |
| R5 | step errors | `REJECTED_STEP_ERROR` | 0 / LOW | joined names | None | WARNING |
| R6 | scorer raised | `SKIPPED_SCORER_ERROR` | 0 / LOW | None | None | ERROR + traceback |
| R7 | total below the effective minimum | `REJECTED_SCORE_<total_score>` | scorer's | None | `effective_min` | none |
| R8 | `signal_age` result `== 0.0` | `REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE` | scorer's | `signal_age` | `effective_min` | none |
| R9 | otherwise | `PASSED` | scorer's | None | `effective_min` | INFO |

**The status strings and the stored reason (Q10).**

- FACT: the screener writes a status only through `_persist`, always the result's own status. The complete set is nine fixed strings and one family: `PASSED`; `REJECTED_NOT_MIS_TRADABLE`; `REJECTED_CIRCUIT_PROXIMITY`; `REJECTED_AT_CIRCUIT` (enforce only); `REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE`; `REJECTED_STEP_ERROR`; `REJECTED_SCORE_<n>` (the integer total, always below that route's minimum); `SKIPPED_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE`; `SKIPPED_EXECUTOR_ERROR`; `SKIPPED_SCORER_ERROR` (OFF and shadow paths only) (P03-S070).
- FACT and HISTORICAL: the `status` comment, P18 and SS3 describe rejects as "REJECTED_<step>"; only `REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE` carries a step name; the others name a cause; module 29's tests expected statuses such as "REJECTED_volume_surge", which no route writes (P03-S071). Two statuses come from different mechanisms by mode: `REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE` is R8 (after scoring, score kept) on the OFF and shadow paths and the HardGate's freshness test (before scoring, `score=0`) on the enforce path; `REJECTED_CIRCUIT_PROXIMITY` is R3 or E1, the same rule function under two switches; the status string does not show which (P03-S072).
- FACT (the stored reason): `reason = result.rejected_step or result.status` is written to `signals.rejection_reason`: `PASSED` writes `PASSED`; the MIS reject `mis_tradable`; the proximity reject `circuit_proximity`; E1 the reason in lower case; R8 `signal_age`; the step-error reject the joined step names; `REJECTED_SCORE_<n>` and the three skips their own status. The detailed texts go to the log only. HISTORICAL: SS5 gave `reason=rejected_step or "PASSED"`; the code writes the status instead (P03-S073).
- FACT (A03's file schema, interface): the `signals.status` CHECK accepts `PASSED` and every `REJECTED*` or `SKIPPED_*` value, so every screener string satisfies the file's constraint; `screener_results.status` has no CHECK (P03-S074). FACT (A15's evidence classifier, interface): it labels the P3 record by status; HISTORICAL: its docstring says the enforce path "reuses the same status for a PRE-screen reject when it ENFORCES -- it does not today"; INTERPRETATION: on the enforce path E1's `REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE` would be labelled a scored step reject although it is built before any step or score (P03-S075).

**`_persist` and the evidence capture: what is written, in what order, what is swallowed (Q10, Q13).**

- FACT: `_persist(signal_id, result, eligible_score=None, *, symbol, strategy_name, trigger_price, triggered_at)` is called at 15 places, once per route; `eligible_score` is given at five (R7, R8, R9, E6, E7) (P03-S076). Order: (1) the effect counter (outside any `try`; documented "Cannot raise"); (2) `ts = now_ist().isoformat()`; (3) inside one `try`, `update_signal_status(signal_id, result.status, reason=reason, ts=ts)`, then `insert_screener_result(...)` with the score, tier, status, the step results, latencies and snapshot as JSON, `ts` and `eligible_score`; (4) `except Exception`: one ERROR line, nothing re-raised; (5) outside that `try`, the P3 evidence capture (P03-S077).
