- FACT (A03's store, interface): `update_signal_status` runs one `UPDATE ... WHERE signal_id = ?` in its own transaction with no test of the current status ("No-op if signal_id does not exist"), and its `ts` is "accepted for SP9 API symmetry; not stored"; `insert_screener_result` runs one INSERT in its own transaction; `screener_results` has an autoincrement id, a foreign key to `signals` and no unique constraint on `signal_id` (P03-S078). The screener's `ts` is therefore stored in `screener_results.ts` and the P3 record only; INTERPRETATION: a second screening of the same signal would append a second row and overwrite the status (P03-S079). `step_statuses`, `error_steps` and `rejected_step` have no `screener_results` column (HISTORICAL: `step_statuses` "is ... persisted NOWHERE else"); the snapshot is serialised with the `SafeJSONEncoder`, the step data with the default encoder (P03-S080).
- FACT: one `try` holds both writes and the three JSON encodings; if the status write raises the insert is not attempted; if an encoding or the insert raises the status stays written without a row; the error is logged once and `_persist` returns normally, with no retry and no field telling the caller (HISTORICAL: SS5 and SS10 state this as intended; A19's test states the same) (P03-S081). INTERPRETATION (A06 EV-02, case (c), the screener's side): a failed status write leaves the processor's `PROCESSING`; after a reject or skip the processor returns, so the run ends `PROCESSING` with no `screener_results` row (P03-S082). INTERPRETATION (A03 P-11, case (5)): the status and the analytics row are two transactions in that order; a failure or stop between them leaves a status with no row, never the reverse (P03-S083).
- FACT (A03 P-02, the screener's side): among the non-test calls of `update_signal_status`, the screener's is the only one that passes `ts` (L619), and the store discards it; A03 AUDIT P-02 records "no caller passes it"; the code at L619 differs from that sentence (reported, not resolved; P03-AP14) (P03-S084). The effect counter counts every `_persist` call whatever the writes' outcome (P03-S085).
- FACT (the P3 payload): `signal_id`, `symbol`, `strategy`, `ts`, `trigger_price`, `triggered_at`, `status`, `rejected_step`, `reject_reason`, `score_total`, `tier`, `step_results`, `step_statuses` and the snapshot; no `error_steps`, latencies, `eligible_score`, scanner, direction or intent; the payload is built only when a recorder is wired (P03-S086). `_evidence_capture` returns at once without a recorder, otherwise calls `capture`, and on any exception calls `note_failure` and logs ERROR, raising nothing; A15's recorder documents `capture` as "NEVER raises" and runs the append synchronously on the trading thread with an fsync under one process-wide lock; HISTORICAL: the capture sits outside the database `try` because "a failed write ... silently took the evidence record with it" (P03-S087). INTERPRETATION: where wired, every route that reaches `_persist` makes one capture attempt, even when both writes failed; all of the evidence wiring is after `7d4970a`, and whether it ran is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P03-S088). HISTORICAL: the comment's "68.99 % of the corpus is a REJECTED_SCORE_*" is not checkable from the repository; FACT: `config/expected_managers.yaml` names `_persist` at L592 and the constructor at `main.py` L2826, which differ from the baseline (L598; L3281) (P03-S089).
- FACT (the `eligible_score` column): described as the "per-strategy min_score threshold", it holds the route's effective minimum on five routes and NULL on the other ten; INTERPRETATION: at the committed files every stored value is a global minimum (60 on the OFF and shadow paths, 50 on enforce) (P03-S141). FACT and current external context (SQLite foreign keys, read 2026-11-21): the store enables foreign keys on every connection; INTERPRETATION: for a `signal_id` without a `signals` row the status update is a no-op and the insert fails, and `_persist` logs and returns; whether a screened signal could lack its row is A06's and A03's (P03-S142).

**`shutdown` (A01 C20; A06 PQ-02).**

- FACT: `shutdown()` only calls the step executor's `shutdown()` (FIX-100); it sets no flag of its own, so the screener does not refuse a later call (P03-S090). The executor's `shutdown` is idempotent and waits for its current pool, swallowing any error; pools retired by a timeout rotation are not waited for (section 5) (P03-S091). The sole caller is the processor's `stop()`, after its dispatcher join and its own pool's `shutdown(wait=True)`; `main.py` makes no direct call, although the executor's docstring names "main.py shutdown sequence" (P03-S092).
- INTERPRETATION (with current external context, Python `concurrent.futures`, read 2026-11-21): in the code's order no screening call from the processor starts after the screener's `shutdown()`; if one did, every step would be `ERROR` and the screener would write `REJECTED_STEP_ERROR` (P03-S093). FACT (A01 C20, the screener's side): `continue_from_gate` makes no screening call; INTERPRETATION: the screener's shutdown order has no dependency on the gate (P03-S094).
- FACT (A06 PQ-02): the screener sets no overall time limit on `screen()`; its parts are the quote fetch (a rate-limiter wait of at most 30 s by default), up to ten steps each waited for up to the executor's 5 s default, the scorer, two store transactions (each connection with `busy_timeout` 30 s) and one synchronous evidence append; UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED: their durations in production (P03-S095). FACT: `main.py` sets the shutdown event first and stops the processor later; INTERPRETATION: during the drain a screening quote fetch that must wait for a token ends at once as `SKIPPED_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` with an ERROR line; whether this occurred is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P03-S143).

**Missing, malformed or stale inputs, exceptions and store failures (Q13).**

- FACT (handling by condition) (P03-S096):

| Condition | What the screener does | Status | How it is surfaced |
|---|---|---|---|
| No quote entry for the symbol | `_make_skipped` | `SKIPPED_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` | INFO |
| Quote fetch, `.get` or `_build_market_data` raised (including a rate-limit refusal or a wait aborted at shutdown) | caught | `SKIPPED_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` | ERROR with traceback |
| Optional quote fields absent | `None` in the market data; circuit state `""` | none of its own | none |
| Last price `0.0` (A04 RD-12) | used as a price; may set `"lower_circuit"` | none of its own | none |
| Quote of unknown age | no test | none | none |
| Product resolver raised | not blocked (fail-open) | none | none |
| Blocklist call, proximity rule or a strategy attribute raised | not caught | leaves `screen()` | the processor's handler |
| `run_all` raised | caught | `SKIPPED_EXECUTOR_ERROR` | ERROR with traceback |
| A step raised | listed in `error_steps` | `REJECTED_STEP_ERROR` | WARNING (OFF/shadow) or none (enforce), plus the executor's ERROR per step |
| A step timed out | scored 0.5 | depends on the score | the executor's WARNING |
| Scorer raised | caught | `SKIPPED_SCORER_ERROR` | ERROR with traceback |
| Shadow comparison raised | caught in the helper | none | ERROR with traceback |
| HardGate or a re-scale helper raised (enforce) | not caught | leaves `screen()` | the processor's handler |
| A store write or JSON encoding raised | caught | result still returned | ERROR with traceback |
| Evidence capture or payload build raised | caught | none | `note_failure`, then ERROR |

- FACT: the screener contains no `raise` statement and makes no notifier or alert call; A15's recorder documents that its own failures are counted, persisted and once a day alerted (P03-S097). INTERPRETATION (A06's handlers, interface): an exception that leaves `screen()` reaches the processor's generic handler and is written `PLACEMENT_FAILED`, although no placement was attempted, and `_record_screener_stats` is not reached; an exception in the two log calls after a `_persist` would overwrite the screener's status (P03-S098).
- FACT (A06 WA-07 and WA-06, the screener's side): no finiteness, range or tick test of `trigger_price`, which is passed unchanged to the proximity rule, the steps, the HardGate and the P3 record (P03-S099); no clock test of `triggered_at`, which is passed unchanged to the steps, the HardGate (whose freshness test is skipped when the value is `None`) and the P3 record, so a time later than receipt reaches them unchanged (P03-S100).
- INTERPRETATION ("converted to a default decision"): with no retry anywhere, a quote, executor or scorer failure becomes a terminal skip, a step exception a reject, a timeout a neutral score, a resolver failure "not blocked" and a store failure "result returned as if written"; the processor treats every skip as final (P03-S101). FACT (log levels): ERROR with a traceback for the quote error, the executor, scorer, store and shadow-comparison failures; WARNING for the MIS would-drop, the proximity reject, the gate reject and the OFF/shadow step-error reject; INFO for the no-quote skip, the two `PASSED` routes and the shadow line; none for R7, R8, E4 and E6; the executor logs its own ERROR per error step and WARNING per timeout (P03-S102).

**Bypass, double evaluation, loss and ambiguous states at the screener (Q11).**

- FACT: within one call the routes run in the fixed order and no route returns to an earlier one; but some checks apply only on some paths: on the enforce path the HardGate's three tests replace R3 and R8; on the OFF and shadow paths a price at a circuit limit is a score input of step 9, not a reject (as module 29 expected), while on the enforce path it is `REJECTED_AT_CIRCUIT`; a 0.0 step rejects only through the score, except `signal_age`; a timeout scores 0.5; an MIS shadow would-drop continues; a caller-supplied `market_data` skips R2 (tests only). INTERPRETATION: the same market condition is a score input in two modes and a hard reject in the third (P03-S103).
- FACT and INTERPRETATION: the screener reads nothing from the store, so it neither knows a signal's earlier status nor refuses a second screening; a signal screened twice gets two analytics rows, and its status follows the later call, including a `PASSED` after a reject; whether the processor screens a signal twice is in section 10 (P09) (P03-S104). INTERPRETATION (loss): a swallowed status write leaves `PROCESSING`; a swallowed analytics write leaves no row; an exception out of `screen()` writes neither row nor the P3 record and is recorded `PLACEMENT_FAILED`; an MIS shadow would-drop leaves a log line only (P03-S105). INTERPRETATION (ambiguous states): two causes behind `SKIPPED_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE`; two mechanisms behind two statuses; fixed scores stored in the same columns as computed ones; `PASSED` as a stored reason; a WARNING naming a status not written; `PASSED` being the screener's last word only until the processor's next write (P03-S106). FACT: the screener has no per-signal or per-symbol guard and no lock; two workers can be inside `screen()` at once for different signals (P03-S107; section 10).

**The caller's contract at L998 to L1028 (interface only; A06's file; Q7).**

- FACT (A06 PQ-15 and EV-19, the screener's side; D-38 line (b)): the call passes `signal_id, symbol, scanner_name, trigger_price, triggered_at` by position and `direction=strategy_obj.direction, intent=strategy_obj.intent, strategy=strategy_obj, market_data=None`; `screen()` takes them in that order; every returning route gives a frozen `ScreeningResult` and writes the status except the MIS shadow fall-through, which continues to a later route that writes; the exceptions of P03-S098 return and write nothing (P03-S108). The processor times the call with `time.monotonic()`, so `screen_ms` covers the fetch, the steps, the scorer, both writes and the capture (P03-S109).
- FACT (`_record_screener_stats`): under the processor's stats lock it adds the time and counts skips by status, rejects by status (INTERPRETATION: one key per distinct `REJECTED_SCORE_<n>` value) and passes (P03-S110). FACT: a `SKIPPED_` status logs a WARNING and returns; `passed` false logs INFO and returns; otherwise the heartbeat and A09's `_derive_prices` (L1028); the processor writes no status on these branches (P03-S111). HISTORICAL: the processor's comments describe a subset of the statuses; "screener wrote status" holds unless the screener's write failed (P03-S112).
- FACT: before L1028 the processor uses `status` (prefix only) and `passed`; after a pass it reads `tier`, `score`, `step_results`, `step_statuses` and the snapshot (the P1 record, the V3 signal) and passes the object on to the allocator; `rejected_step`, `error_steps` and `latencies_ms` are read by no non-test code after `screen()` returns (P03-S113). FACT, in the fixed wording: no code path leads from a screening result to the entry gate; `EntryGate.add()` has no non-test caller at the baseline, so the normal live path that creates a new gate entry is not established; `EntryGate.start()` rehydrates any persisted `gate_state` rows and can poll and release them; whether such rows existed in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P03-S114).

**The sequence from the screening call (Q6).**

- FACT (before the call, interface only): the processor reaches L998 after `PROCESSING`, A06's intake checks and strategy lookup, the strategy-control check (section 3), the strategy's own entry window (A01) and the governor (A09) (P03-S115).
- FACT (mode `off`): L998 → R1 (if enabled) → R2 → snapshot → R3 → thresholds → R4 `run_all` with all ten steps → R5 → R6 `QualityScorer.score` → R7 → R8 → R9; each ending route writes through `_persist` and returns to L1005 → `_record_screener_stats` → return, or the heartbeat and A09's price derivation (P03-S116). (mode `shadow`, the committed value) as `off`, plus the shadow comparison after R6, which changes nothing (P03-S117). (mode `enforce`, with a HardGate) L998 → R1 → R2 → snapshot → E1 `HardGate.evaluate` → E2 → E3 `run_all` without `circuit_check` and `signal_age` (the defaults and committed value) → E4 → E5 `rescaled_total` and `tier_for` (no `QualityScorer` call) → E6 → E7, then as in `off` from L1005 (P03-S118).
- FACT (the question's terms mapped to the code): "secondary screening" is `screen()` and `_screen_v3_enforce`; "hard gates" are R1 (active) and R3 on the OFF and shadow paths and the HardGate's three tests on the enforce path; "quality scoring" is R6 and R7 (or E5 and E6); "step execution" is R4 or E3; "entry gates": nothing after L998 creates an entry-gate entry, and `continue_from_gate` does not call the screener; the five V3 playbook gates are not called by the screener, which imports only `circuit_proximity_reason`, `rescale_min_score`, `rescaled_total` and `tier_for`; their callers are A18's (P03-S119).

**Inherited items on this section's side** (section 16 has every row): A01 C20 (P03-S120); A02 T-14 (P03-S121); A03 H-16 (P03-S122: no screener line names `daily_symbol_stats`), P-02 (P03-S123: agrees on the method, differs from "no caller passes it"), P-11 (P03-S124) and Q-22 (P03-S125); A04 RD-12 (P03-S126) and PR-23 (P03-S127); A06 WA-06 (P03-S128), WA-07 (P03-S129), PQ-02 (P03-S130), PQ-15 (P03-S131), EV-02 (P03-S132) and EV-19 (P03-S133: agrees with the line (b) contract, with two qualifications).

## 5. The market data the steps receive, the step executor and the ten steps (questions Q8, Q9, Q13, Q14)

This section covers `screening/step_executor.py` (construction, the shared single-worker pool, `run_all`, the timeout and rotation, `shutdown`), the market-data dictionary that the screener's `_build_market_data` builds for the steps (its content is P04's under ADDENDUM 3), the ten steps with their inputs, thresholds, formulae, units, boundaries and missing-data results, and how the step results are used. The scorer that weights them is in section 6.

Evidence: `A07_STEP_EXECUTOR_AND_THE_TEN_STEPS_2026-11-21.md` (P04), which read the 423-line executor in whole and the screener, the scorer and A04's `Quote` and `get_quote` at the interface (P04-S002). The executor (12 commits, the last `8d753a0`, 2026-07-25, "M-S3 ... [INERT]") is the file of the last recorded deployment commit; `_build_market_data`, the threshold lines and A04's `Quote` and `get_quote` are identical at `7d4970a` and the baseline (P04-S004, P04-S005). One local check of the work environment's interpreter confirmed generic language rules (for example that `None.upper()` and `0.0 >= None` raise), with no legacy code (P04-S008).

**The file and its declared contract.**

- FACT: the executor imports only standard-library names and `now_ist`; it imports no configuration loader, store, broker or logger module (the logger is injected), and `field` is imported unused (P04-S010). It defines the frozen `StepExecutorResult` (`step_results`, `step_statuses` with "PASSED" | "REJECTED" | "ERROR" | "TIMEOUT", `rejected_at`, `error_steps`, `latencies_ms`), the constant `_DEFAULT_MARKET_OPEN = 09:15` and the class `StepExecutor` with its ten step methods and the helper `_minutes_since_open` (P04-S011).
- HISTORICAL: the header says each step "is a pure function returning a raw score (0.0-1.0)" and "run_all() never raises"; the class docstring states SE2 ("Stateless. Constructor takes only logger"), SE5 (every step wrapped; errors recorded, never raised), SE6 (latency tracked), SE7 ("ALL 10 steps always run (no short-circuit)") and SE8 (missing keys use per-step defaults) (P04-S012). The locked decisions SE1 to SE10 in `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` give the same contract, the ten step definitions (SE4) and "NEVER raise on missing key" (SE8) (P04-S013). P9a asks the screener to "reject if now - signal.triggered_at > 90s" and to "reject if upper/lower circuit hit", and step 1's "'allow through' on insufficient data" to become `REJECTED_INSUFFICIENT_DATA`; P9b asks for "All weights, thresholds, and tier boundaries in config/scoring_weights.yaml. NO scattered constants in screener code"; P18 asks for `REJECTED_<step>` with "the 10 screener step names"; SS7 sets eight fields to `None` "(v2)" with providers promised for "v2.1"; S4 says "Percentage float fields: > 0, < 1 (expressed as decimal)" (P04-S014). The weights file's comments describe each step (for example "volume vs 20-day avg", "bid-ask spread vs ATR", "signal freshness (now - triggered_at ≤ 90s, P9a)") (P04-S015). The history of the 423 lines (most step bodies from `bcf03b5`, 2026-04-17; the pool FIX-100; the timeout `f08b485`; the V3 `exclude_steps` `536172c`; the M-S3 rotation `8d753a0`) is in P04-S016.

**Construction, the market-open parameter, the timeout and the pool.**

- FACT: `StepExecutor.__init__(logger, market_open=None, step_timeout_sec=5.0)` keeps the logger, the market open (09:15 when not given), the timeout, a lock, one pool, a shutdown flag and a rotation counter (P04-S017). HISTORICAL: the FIX-114 comment says production "MUST inject market_open from SystemConfig.trading_hours.market_open to support special sessions" and that the 09:15 constant "exists only for backward compatibility with tests"; its first line calls the constant the default "for signal_age calculation", while the value is used only by step 7 (P04-S018).
- FACT: the only non-test construction (`main.py` L3234 to L3237) passes the logger and `market_windows.market_open`, and no `step_timeout_sec` (P04-S019); A01's `MarketWindows` keeps the regular open and applies special sessions only through `_get_effective_times(d)` (P04-S020); the committed file sets `trading_hours.market_open: "09:15"` and declares no special session (P04-S021). INTERPRETATION: the executor receives the regular-session open as a fixed time for the life of the process and never calls `_get_effective_times`, so on a special-session date step 7 would measure from the regular open; at the committed values the two coincide (P04-S022). FACT: no configuration key sets the step timeout; on the `main.py` route it is the constructor default, 5.0 s per step, a constant of the code (P04-S023).
- FACT: `_new_executor()` returns `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=1, thread_name_prefix="step-exec")`; one pool is created at construction and replaced only by a rotation; HISTORICAL: FIX-100 calls it "single-worker (steps run sequentially per signal)", and M-S3 notes that "run_all() may be entered by several signal workers at once" (P04-S024). Current external context (Python `concurrent.futures` and `queue`, and the CPython source, read 2026-11-21): the pool's threads are joined before the interpreter exits, and its work queue is an unbounded FIFO `SimpleQueue` (P04-S025). FACT: the only non-test callers of `run_all` are the screener's default route (all ten steps) and its V3 enforce route (`exclude_steps`) (P04-S026); the processor runs `_process_one` on a pool of `worker_count` threads (committed 5) (P04-S027). INTERPRETATION: up to five processor threads can be inside `run_all` at once, all submitting to the one worker thread; each signal's steps run one after another, the steps of concurrent signals are executed one at a time in submission order, interleaved, and the pool serialises all step execution of the process without adding parallelism (P04-S028).

**`run_all`: order, statuses, latencies, `error_steps` and `rejected_at`.**

- FACT: `run_all(signal, market_data, thresholds, exclude_steps=None)`; the default runs all ten steps ("the OFF path is byte-identical"); the V3 enforce route passes the `v3_gate_steps` (committed `[circuit_check, signal_age]`, mode `"shadow"`) (P04-S029). Its first statement, `signal.get("direction", "LONG").upper()`, is outside any `try`; on the screener's routes the direction is a schema-validated `LONG` or `SHORT`; INTERPRETATION: a `None` or non-`str` value would raise there despite "run_all() never raises", and the screener would return `SKIPPED_EXECUTOR_ERROR` (P04-S030).
- FACT: the fixed order: 1 `volume_surge`, 2 `vwap_position`, 3 `atr_filter`, 4 `rsi_range`, 5 `price_action`, 6 `sector_strength`, 7 `time_of_day`, 8 `spread_check`, 9 `circuit_check`, 10 `signal_age`; only `exclude_steps` removes a step (P04-S031). For each step the code snapshots the current pool, submits `fn(signal, market_data, thresholds, direction)` with the caller's own objects (no step assigns to them) and waits `future.result(timeout=...)` (P04-S032).
- FACT: on a timeout a WARNING, the value 0.5 ("neutral score"), the status `TIMEOUT`, the latency and a pool rotation; a timed-out step is not an error step and does not set `rejected_at` (P04-S033). On any other `Exception` an ERROR line with the traceback, 0.0, `ERROR`, the name in `error_steps` and `rejected_at` if unset; exceptions that are not `Exception` subclasses are caught by neither handler (P04-S034). On a normal return the value is stored unchanged; exactly `0.0` is `REJECTED` (and sets `rejected_at`), anything else `PASSED` (P04-S035). The result carries all ten names on the default route; the executor applies no weight, aggregation or rounding (P04-S036).
- INTERPRETATION: `PASSED` means only "returned a value other than 0.0", including the neutral 0.5 of a missing input and a negative value; `REJECTED` does not by itself reject the signal; the screener's `step_statuses` comment omits `TIMEOUT` (P04-S037). FACT: `rejected_at` is read by no tracked non-test file (P04-S038); `error_steps` holds ERROR steps only, and the screener rejects `REJECTED_STEP_ERROR` whenever it is non-empty (P04-S039). FACT and current external context (Python `concurrent.futures`, read 2026-11-21): the latency is taken around `submit` and `result`; INTERPRETATION: it includes the wait in the pool's queue behind other signals' steps; HISTORICAL: the legacy M-S3 record says the same (P04-S040). Current external context: on a timeout `result()` raises `TimeoutError` (the imported alias), and a step's own exception or a `CancelledError` are `Exception` subclasses (P04-S041). FACT: SE7's "no short-circuit" holds in the code (P04-S042).

**The pool rotation (M-S3) and `shutdown`.**

- FACT: `_rotate_executor(stale)`, under the lock and only if the stale pool is still current and no shutdown is set, installs a new pool, counts the rotation, logs a WARNING and calls `stale.shutdown(wait=False, cancel_futures=True)`, discarding any exception (P04-S043). HISTORICAL: its docstring explains that `result(timeout=...)` "abandons the WAIT, not the WORK" and that without the rotation every later step would queue behind the stuck one (P04-S044). Current external context (Python `concurrent.futures` and the CPython source, read 2026-11-21): `cancel_futures=True` cancels pending futures not yet started; `submit` after shutdown raises `RuntimeError` (P04-S045). INTERPRETATION: the overrunning step keeps running on the abandoned worker and its result is discarded; later submissions go to the new pool (P04-S046).
- INTERPRETATION (code with current Python's documented behaviour; not stated in the docstring or the M-S3 record): steps that other processor threads had already queued on the same pool are cancelled at the rotation; each waiting thread receives `CancelledError`, recorded as an ERROR step, so that other signal is rejected `REJECTED_STEP_ERROR`; a thread whose own wait times out first takes the TIMEOUT route; a thread that snapshotted the old pool just before the swap and submits just after its shutdown receives `RuntimeError`, also an ERROR. The mechanism holds for current Python (the parameter dates from 3.9); the production Python version and whether two signals ever overlapped a timeout are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P04-S047). FACT: timeouts and rotations are counted nowhere but in WARNING lines and the `TIMEOUT` step status (P04-S048).
- FACT: `shutdown()` is idempotent, sets the flag under the lock and calls the current pool's `shutdown(wait=True, cancel_futures=False)`, discarding any error; pools abandoned by rotations are not touched (P04-S049). Its only route is the screener's `shutdown()`, called by the processor's `stop()` after its own pool has drained; `main.py` makes no direct call (P04-S050). INTERPRETATION (A06 PQ-02's A07 side): every drained signal is screened with the executor open; the executor adds no limit of its own to the stop sequence, and one signal's `run_all` can add at most about 50 s of waiting only when every step overruns; a worker abandoned by a rotation is joined at interpreter exit, so a step that never returned would delay the exit (P04-S051). FACT (A19, stated expectation only): a test expects `run_all` after `shutdown()` to return ten statuses; INTERPRETATION: every submit would then raise and the screener would write `REJECTED_STEP_ERROR`, a route not reached on the `main.py` path (P04-S052).
- HISTORICAL: the legacy M-S3 record states the timeout is "INERT, and this is measured" ("33,171 rows / 287,600 individual step measurements", slowest step 66.061 ms against 5,000 ms, "The timeout has never fired") and that removing the pool "is a design call for Rama. Recorded, not acted." (P04-S053). UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED: whether any step timed out in production; the record's data does not survive; INTERPRETATION: the step bodies make no database or network call and take no lock of their own, apart from logger calls whose effect depends on the injected logger (P04-S054).

**The market-data dictionary the steps receive (ADDENDUM 3: P04's content; Q14).**

- FACT: on the live route the processor passes `market_data=None`, so the screener fetches its own quote through `broker_adapter.get_quote`; a missing entry or any exception gives `SKIPPED_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` before any step runs (P04-S055). FACT (A04's adapter, interface only): `get_quote` takes a rate-limiter token, calls Kite's `quote` with `"NSE:<symbol>"` and builds a `Quote` with `symbol`, `last_price`, `bid`, `ask`, `volume`, `ts` and six optional fields (`vwap`, `open_price`, `day_high`, `day_low`, `upper_circuit`, `lower_circuit`) (P04-S056). Its mapping: `last_price` 0.0 when absent; `bid` and `ask` the first depth level's price, 0.0 when empty; `volume` 0 when absent; `vwap` from `average_price`, and the OHLC values and circuit limits, each `None` when absent or falsy (current external context, Python "Truth Value Testing", read 2026-11-21); INTERPRETATION: a zero `average_price`, OHLC value or band reaches the screener as `None`, an absent price or depth side as 0.0 (A04 RD-12) (P04-S057).
- Current external context (Zerodha Kite Connect v3, "Market quotes and instruments", read 2026-11-21): `volume` is "Volume traded today"; `average_price` "The volume weighted average price of a stock at a given time during the day"; `ohlc.close` "Closing price of the instrument from the last trading day"; the page lists no ATR, RSI, sector or 20-day average volume attribute (P04-S058); a key with no data is absent from the response; the example response shows depth levels with price 0; the page states no value range; INTERPRETATION: a depth level of price 0 would pass on as a bid or ask of 0.0; whether such quotes reached the legacy system is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P04-S059).
- FACT: `_build_market_data` takes `ltp = quote.last_price`, derives `circuit_state` as `"upper_circuit"` if `ltp >= upper`, else `"lower_circuit"` if `ltp <= lower` (each only when the band is not `None`), else `""`, and returns a dictionary of 16 keys (P04-S060):

| Key | Value | A04 source and its empty value | Read by step |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ltp` | `quote.last_price` | `last_price`, 0.0 if absent | 2, 3, 5 (and `circuit_state`) |
| `bid`, `ask` | `quote.bid`, `quote.ask` | first depth price, 0.0 if none | 8 |
| `volume` | `quote.volume` | `volume`, 0 if absent | 1 |
| `circuit_state` | `"upper_circuit"`, `"lower_circuit"` or `""` | derived | 9 |
| `upper_circuit`, `lower_circuit` | `getattr(quote, ..., None)` | band limits, `None` if absent or zero | none (the proximity rule and the V3 gate, section 7) |
| `vwap` | `getattr(quote, "vwap", None)` | `average_price`, `None` if absent or zero | 2 |
| `open`, `day_high`, `day_low` | from the quote's OHLC | `None` if absent or zero | 5 |
| `atr`, `rsi`, `sector` | `None` always | none | 3, 4, 6 |
| `prev_close` | `None` always | none (the adapter maps no `ohlc.close`) | none |
| `avg_volume_20d` | `None` always | none | 1 |

  (P04-S061.)
- HISTORICAL: the docstring cites SS7 and SS8; the comments say the bands serve "the pre-fill circuit-proximity reject (NOCIL fix) + forensic snapshot", that `vwap` and the OHLC values are "populated from Quote fields (Kite API response)" ("v2.1"), and that the five `None` fields are "Still not in Kite quote API; would need instruments cache"; SS7 had set four of the now-populated fields to `None` "(v2)" (P04-S062). FACT and current external context: the Kite page lists `ohlc.close` while the comment groups `prev_close` with fields not in the quote; the adapter's `Quote` has no close field and no step reads `prev_close`; no module under `screening/` imports `core/candle_math.py`, `core/daily_stats.py`, an instrument cache or any `daily_symbol` helper (P04-S063). FACT: the dictionary carries no quote time stamp, exchange time or depth quantity, and no step tests the age of the quote; the snapshot persists the five always-`None` keys as nulls (P04-S064).
- INTERPRETATION: a band of 0 can never mark a circuit; a price equal to a band marks that circuit; the upper test comes first; an absent last price (0.0) with a positive lower band marks `"lower_circuit"` for a LONG or a SHORT signal alike (A04 RD-12) (P04-S065). On the adapter route the price is always a `float`, so the circuit comparisons do not raise; a `None` price with a band present would raise and give `SKIPPED_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` (P04-S066).

**The ten steps (Q8, Q9).** Each step has the signature `(self, signal, md, thr, direction) -> float`; the thresholds dictionary holds three strategy fields only; "live route" means the screener's default route with the dictionary above; steps 7 and 10 each read the clock (`now_ist()`, a fixed +05:30 offset, A01's) when they run (P04-S067). File values are FACT about the files only.

| Step | Inputs | Rule (baseline lines) | Output | Missing or bad input | On the live route |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 `volume_surge` | `avg_volume_20d`, `volume`, `min_volume_surge` | 1.0 if `volume > avg_vol * min_surge` (strict), else 0.0 (L247 to L256) | 0.0, 1.0 | `avg_volume_20d` falsy → 0.0, before the threshold is read | always 0.0 (`REJECTED`); the threshold never read (P04-S068 to P04-S070) |
| 2 `vwap_position` | `ltp`, `vwap`, direction | LONG 1.0 if `ltp > vwap`, else (SHORT) 1.0 if `ltp < vwap`; equality 0.0 (L258 to L269) | 0.0, 1.0 | `vwap` or `ltp` `None`, or `ltp` 0.0 → 0.0 ("No VWAP data (ETFs, bonds) -> fail gate") | VWAP is the quote's `average_price`; a missing one counts against the signal (P04-S072, P04-S073) |
| 3 `atr_filter` | `atr`, `ltp`, `min_adr_pct` | `adr_pct = (atr / ltp) * 100.0`; 1.0 if `>= min_adr` (L271 to L281) | 0.0, 1.0 | `atr` or `ltp` falsy → 0.0 | always 0.0; the threshold never read; named ATR, documented as ADR (P04-S074 to P04-S076) |
| 4 `rsi_range` | `rsi`, direction | LONG 1.0 if `40 <= rsi <= 80`, else (SHORT) `20 <= rsi <= 60`, else 0.0; inclusive; code constants (L283 to L316) | 0.0, 0.5, 1.0 | `None` → 0.5; non-numeric or outside [0, 100] → WARNING and 0.5; a NaN passes the range test and scores 0.0 (current external context, Python "Value comparisons", read 2026-11-21) | always 0.5 (`PASSED`) (P04-S077 to P04-S080) |
| 5 `price_action` | `ltp`, `open`, `day_high`, `day_low` | `min(1.0, abs(ltp - open) / (day_high - day_low + 1e-9) * 2.0)`; direction unused (L318 to L329) | [0.0, 1.0]; negative only if high < low | a key present with `None` raises → ERROR → `REJECTED_STEP_ERROR` | variable (P04-S081 to P04-S084) |
| 6 `sector_strength` | `sector` | 1.0 if truthy, else 0.5 ("Placeholder") (L331 to L336) | 0.5, 1.0 | → 0.5 | always 0.5 (`PASSED`) (P04-S085, P04-S086) |
| 7 `time_of_day` | the clock, the open time | minutes since open: `< 15` 0.5, `< 60` 1.0, `< 180` 0.8, else 0.5; negative clamped to 0 (L338 to L370) | 0.5, 0.8, 1.0; never 0.0 | none | variable (P04-S087 to P04-S089) |
| 8 `spread_check` | `bid`, `ask`, `max_spread_pct` | `mid = (ask + bid) / 2`; `spread_pct = (ask - bid) / mid * 100`; 1.0 if `<= max_spread`, else 0.0 (L372 to L395) | 0.0, 0.5, 1.0 | either `None` → 0.5; `mid <= 0` → WARNING and 0.5 | variable; the threshold read on every screening (P04-S090 to P04-S093) |
| 9 `circuit_check` | `circuit_state` | 0.0 if `"upper_circuit"` or `"lower_circuit"`, else 1.0; direction unused (L397 to L404) | 0.0, 1.0 | absent or other string → 1.0 | variable (P04-S094, P04-S095) |
| 10 `signal_age` | `triggered_at`, the clock | age ≤ 30 s 1.0, ≤ 60 s 0.5, else 0.0; inclusive; code constants (L406 to L423) | 0.0, 0.5, 1.0 | `None` → 0.5; a future time gives a negative age and 1.0 | variable (P04-S096 to P04-S099) |

- Step 1 (detail). FACT: the screener always supplies `min_volume_surge` from the strategy (schema default 1.3; files 1.2 to 2.0), so the step's own default 1.5 never applies; `volume` is Kite's day volume, while `avg_volume_20d` has no producer anywhere and its unit is stated only as "volume vs 20-day avg" (P04-S069). INTERPRETATION: on the live route step 1 always returns 0.0 and `min_volume_surge` is never read; HISTORICAL: the legacy integrity audit records that `min_volume_surge` and `min_adr_pct` have "ZERO effect"; FACT: P9a (c)'s `REJECTED_INSUFFICIENT_DATA` exists nowhere in the screening, processor or core code (P04-S070). OPEN QUESTION: whether `avg_volume_20d` was meant as a full-day average compared with the part-day `volume` is stated by no source (P04-S071; section 19).
- Step 4 (detail). FACT: the WARNING logs `signal.get("scanner")`, while the screener's dictionary has `"scanner_name"`, so that field would read `None` (P04-S079).
- Step 5 (detail). FACT and current external context (Python `dict.get`, read 2026-11-21): the `ltp` defaults apply only to absent keys, and `_build_market_data` always includes the three OHLC keys, possibly `None`, which then raise (P04-S082). INTERPRETATION: the value is 0.0 exactly when `ltp == open`, 1.0 when the day's body is at least half the day's range; with an absent price (0.0) and OHLC present it is normally 1.0 (P04-S083). The "candle" is the day so far, not the triggering bar; the step is direction-blind; SE4 gives the formula without `+ 1e-9`; the weights file's "swing confirmation" has no code (P04-S084).
- Step 6 (detail). INTERPRETATION: SE4 calls it a "placeholder for v2.1"; the processor's own `_sector_for` (A06's) is not passed to the screener (P04-S086).
- Step 7 (detail). INTERPRETATION: at 09:15 the buckets are 09:15 to 09:30 0.5, 09:30 to 10:15 1.0, 10:15 to 12:15 0.8, then 0.5; inside the committed global entry window (10:00 to 15:00, A01's) a signal can score 1.0 only between 10:00 and 10:15; the step measures the time it runs, not `triggered_at` (P04-S088). FACT: the helper's DEBUG text names "signal_age"; HISTORICAL: the weights file's "later in session = lower weight" does not cover the lower value for the first 15 minutes (P04-S089).
- Step 8 (detail). FACT: the screener supplies `max_spread_pct` (schema default 0.005; all 16 files 0.005), so the step's own default 0.1 never applies (P04-S091). INTERPRETATION: the adapter never supplies `None` for `bid` or `ask`; both 0.0 give the neutral 0.5; an empty bid with a positive ask gives +200 % and 0.0; an empty ask with a positive bid −200 % and 1.0; a crossed or locked quote 1.0; at the file value the step passes only when `ask - bid <= 0.00005 × mid` (P04-S092). HISTORICAL: SE4 defined the spread over the last price; FIX-043 uses the mid price; the weights file's "vs ATR" has no code (P04-S093).
- Step 9 (detail). FACT: the state comes from the last price against the bands, not the trigger price; the trigger-price proximity test is the separate pre-step reject (section 4, R3; section 7) (P04-S094). INTERPRETATION: P9a and the docstring ("Reject if upper or lower circuit") describe a reject, while the step returns a score of 0.0 that the default route does not treat as a reject by itself; on the enforce route the step is excluded and the gate owns the circuit (P04-S095).
- Step 10 (detail). FACT: the screener passes the processor's queued `triggered_at` unchanged; A06 records it as IST-aware, while the processor's docstring says "naive datetime (IST)", and the processor's own age check attaches the IST zone before screening (P04-S097). INTERPRETATION: on the live route both values are aware and the age is a true elapsed time; a naive value would receive the same fixed zone; a future time gives 1.0 (no lower bound); a non-`datetime` would raise, which the processor's earlier call makes unreachable; the age is measured when step 10 runs, after the queue wait, the processor's checks, the quote fetch and steps 1 to 9 (P04-S098). HISTORICAL: P9a's 90 s, the weights file's "≤ 90s" and the V3 gate's `v3_freshness_max_sec: 60.0` ("matches OLD signal_age>60s reject"), while SE4 gives 30 s and 60 s as the code does (P04-S099).

**Thresholds and constants: from configuration to the value the step uses (Q9).**

- FACT: the configured thresholds are three strategy fields (`min_volume_surge` for step 1, `min_adr_pct` for step 3, `max_spread_pct` for step 8), read from the strategy object as plain attributes and always present, so the step fallbacks 1.5, 0.5 and 0.1 never apply; every other step constant (the RSI bands and valid range, the price-action multiplier, cap and epsilon, the time buckets, the 30 s and 60 s age limits) is a code constant; steps 2, 6 and 9 compare with no threshold (P04-S100, P04-S101). INTERPRETATION: the dormant step fallbacks differ from the schema defaults by factors of 100 and 20 for the two `_pct` fields and read as percentages under the steps' arithmetic (P04-S102).
- FACT (the unit of `min_adr_pct` and `max_spread_pct`; the sources disagree; each HISTORICAL except the code): the steps compute percentages (`* 100.0`) and compare the field value with them directly; SE4 gives the same formulas; S4 calls "Percentage float fields" decimals between 0 and 1 without listing which, and the schema validates the two fields only `>= 0`; the module instruction for the strategy schema says "default 0.5%"; the strategy guide gives default 0.005 with "%" descriptions; A02 K-21 records that the `_pct` suffix means a fraction in some keys and a percentage in others (P04-S103). HISTORICAL: the legacy integrity audit raised the point for `max_spread_pct` (IA-P2-02) and left it as open question OQ-P2-1 for Rama, with no answer recorded at the baseline; it called `min_adr_pct` "ALSO ... units-suspect" (P04-S104). INTERPRETATION: under the steps' arithmetic the file value 0.005 means 0.005 % (step 8: a spread of at most 0.005 % of the mid price, read on every live screening); read as a fraction it would mean 0.5 %; the sources do not settle the intended unit (P04-S105; section 19).
- FACT and INTERPRETATION: the step weights are in `config/scoring_weights.yaml`, whose header says "this file is the single source", but the step thresholds above are code constants, which P9b's wording covers; A02 T-20 records the age constants against P9a's 90 s (P04-S106). INTERPRETATION (reachable values on the live route, at the committed weights 15, 10, 10, 10, 15, 10, 5, 5, 10, 10): steps 1 and 3 always 0.0 and steps 4 and 6 always 0.5 contribute 10 and withhold 35, so the weighted sum of the ten values cannot exceed 65; the scorer's own comment states the same ceiling; no step or executor route rounds a value (P04-S107; the timeout condition is in section 6).

**How the step results are used: hard gate, score or neither (Q8).**

- FACT: on the screener's default route the executor's result decides the signal in exactly three places: a non-empty `error_steps` rejects `REJECTED_STEP_ERROR` before scoring; all values go to the scorer and a total below the effective minimum rejects `REJECTED_SCORE_<total>`; and only after the score test a `signal_age` of `0.0` rejects `REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE`; no other step value is tested by name, and neither the step statuses, `rejected_at` nor `TIMEOUT` are tested (P04-S108).
- INTERPRETATION: no step's 0.0 rejects the signal by itself, except step 10's, and only after the score test; any step that raises is a hard reject; all ten values are scores; no step result advances, defers or parks a signal (P04-S109). Names, comments and actual semantics differ in eleven places: the `REJECTED` step status and `rejected_at` name a step that does not reject; step 9's "Reject" and P9a's circuit reject against a scored 0.0; P18's `REJECTED_<step>` names against the statuses written; P9a (c) not implemented; "pure function" against steps 7 and 10 reading the clock; "SE2: Stateless. Constructor takes only logger" against three parameters and the pool, lock, flag and counter; "run_all() never raises" against L132; the missing `TIMEOUT` in the screener's comment; the weights file's descriptions of steps 5, 6, 8 and 10; step 3 named ATR and documented as ADR; and the "signal_age" texts of step 7's helper and of the FIX-114 comment (P04-S110).
- FACT and INTERPRETATION (the timeout against the scorer's FIX-042 docstring): the scorer's docstring says that a timed-out step is "excluded from denominator ... instead of penalizing with 0.0", and the scorer excludes a step only when its name is absent; the executor records a timed-out step with 0.5, so it is counted at half its weight, not excluded, and the two describe different treatments (P04-S111). A timed-out step 10 escapes the age test whatever the real age, and a timed-out step 9 scores 0.5 at a circuit; whether any timeout occurred is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P04-S112).
- INTERPRETATION (the age reject at the committed values): every strategy has `min_score: 0`, so the minimum is the global 60; a signal whose step 10 is 0.0 has a weighted sum of at most 55 and is rejected `REJECTED_SCORE_<n>`, so at these values the `REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE` route is not reached on the live route; a signal aged more than 30 s and at most 60 s can reach 60 only with steps 2, 8, 9 and 7 at 1.0 and step 5 at about 0.967 or more, possible only between 10:00 and 10:15 inside the committed entry window; HISTORICAL: the legacy integrity audit describes the same geometry from production data ("Max achievable live score ... = 65"), which does not survive and is not relied on (P04-S113). FACT: on the V3 enforce route steps 9 and 10 are not run and the HardGate owns circuit and freshness; at the committed `"shadow"` the ten-step route decides; HISTORICAL: the integrity audit records `shadow` on the deployed tree on 01-Aug-2026; the effective value is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P04-S114). FACT: no step computes or reads an entry price, stop, target, R:R, quantity, risk, capital or position (P04-S115; section 11).

**Failure behaviour of the executor (Q13).**

- FACT (missing input): converted silently to a step value (step 1 0.0; step 2 0.0; step 3 0.0; step 4 0.5; step 6 0.5; step 8 0.5; step 9 1.0; step 10 0.5); with a WARNING (step 4 non-numeric or out of range; step 8 `mid <= 0`); turned into an ERROR step (step 5 when an OHLC key holds `None`); SE8's "NEVER raise on missing key" holds for absent keys, not for a key present with `None` (P04-S116). INTERPRETATION (malformed or contradictory input): no step checks the quote fields against each other: a crossed quote scores 1.0 at step 8, a high below the low gives a negative step 5, a price outside the day's range is not tested, a NaN RSI scores 0.0, a future `triggered_at` 1.0, and an absent price marks a lower circuit (P04-S117). FACT (stale input): no step tests the age of the quote; INTERPRETATION: the data's age at a step includes queueing in the shared pool (P04-S118).
- FACT (exceptions): a step exception becomes an ERROR step, logged with its traceback and not retried, and the signal `REJECTED_STEP_ERROR`; a timeout becomes 0.5, logged, not retried, with a rotation; an exception at L132 escapes `run_all` (`SKIPPED_EXECUTOR_ERROR`); exceptions in the pools' `shutdown` calls are discarded without a log; INTERPRETATION: a non-`Exception` exception is caught by neither handler (P04-S119). FACT: the executor performs no database, file or network operation and has no partial write (P04-S120); with no quote, no step runs (P04-S121). INTERPRETATION (summary): surfaced as a signal status: any step exception and a raise before the loop; in logs only: the WARNINGs of steps 4 and 8 and the timeout and rotation WARNINGs; converted to a default value without a log: every unlogged missing input, including the four inputs always `None` on the live route; swallowed: the pools' `shutdown` exceptions; retried: nothing (P04-S122). FACT: no per-signal deadline bounds `run_all`: `signal_processor.pipeline_timeout_sec` (committed 30, "hard per-signal processing deadline") has no reader other than its schema field and validator (A06 PQ-01; A02 K-13); the per-step wait bounds the caller's wait, not the step's work (P04-S171).

**Inherited items on this section's side** (section 16 has every row): A02 T-20 (P04-S123, P04-S124: step 10 compares with the literals 30 and 60; no 90-second limit in A07's screening code); A04 RD-12 (P04-S125, P04-S126: only steps 2 and 3 treat a 0.0 price as missing); A05 CM-07 and A05-01 (P04-S127, P04-S128: the screener's VWAP is the adapter's `average_price`; no A07 file names `session_vwap`; no answer added to the intent question) and CM-22 (P04-S129, P04-S130: `atr` and `rsi` are `None`, as the MATCH row says); A06 WA-06 (P04-S131, P04-S132: step 10 has no lower bound; no step reads `trigger_price`), PQ-02 (P04-S133, P04-S134), PQ-08 (6) (P04-S135, P04-S136: the screener carries an age check of its own, not P9a's 90 s) and ET-06 and A06-02 (P04-S137, P04-S138: no order among concurrent signals; FACT about the code only, no ranking inferred); A05 RTR L120 and A05-25 (P04-S139: the ten step conditions are recorded so that a comparison can be made when Rama supplies the scanner conditions; UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED until then, D-39 P3).

## 6. The quality scorer: arithmetic, tiers, minimum scores and ceilings (questions Q8, Q9)

This section covers `screening/quality_scorer.py`: its declared purpose and intent sources, the result type, the constructor, the arithmetic of `score()`, the tiers, failure behaviour and side effects, the fields its caller reads, the values reachable at the committed weights on the live route (with the timeout condition that Claude's assembly notes ask the STUDY to state), and the scoring configuration as A07 reads it. The minimum-score decision is the screener's (section 4, R7 and E6); the V3 re-scale and the HardGate are in section 7.

Evidence: `A07_QUALITY_SCORER_HARD_GATE_AND_V3_PLAYBOOK_GATES_2026-11-21.md` (P05), with P04 for the step values the scorer receives. The scorer (150 lines, four commits, the last `132e571`, 2026-08-01) and `screening/hard_gate.py` are identical at `7d4970a` and the baseline (P05-S002).

**Declared purpose and intent sources.**

- HISTORICAL: the header and class docstring: "Compute quality score (0-100) ... All weights and thresholds come from ScoringConfig (scoring_weights.yaml). NO inline constants (P9b)"; QS3 (`score(step_results) -> ScoreResult`), QS4 ("weighted_score = raw_score * weight; total capped at 100"), QS5 (tiers from the two thresholds), QS7 ("scorer returns tier string only; sizer applies size multiplier") and QS9 ("pure, deterministic, thread-safe"); the FIX-042 method docstring: "Proportional scoring — missing steps excluded from denominator. If circuit_check times out, recalculate score as (achieved / weights_present) * 100 instead of penalizing with 0.0" (P05-S008).
- HISTORICAL: `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` holds QS1 to QS5 and QS7 to QS9; QS3 says "Missing step -> 0.0 contribution + WARNING log + appears in missing_steps", QS4 "total = sum(all weighted_scores), capped at 100", QS5 the tiers at 80 and 65, QS1 "scorer only aggregates; screener decides pass/reject"; module 28 states the same and adds QS6 (a note headed "Audit Bug 3 fix") and QS10 ("NOT in scope: ... Rejecting signals ... Per-strategy score overrides"); P9b asks for all weights, thresholds and tier boundaries in the weights file (P05-S009). HISTORICAL and FACT: QS8's import list ("stdlib, core.config_loader (type only), core.logger") is not the scorer's dependency set at the baseline, which imports `core.effect_telemetry` (added by `132e571e161c54f0ac944c1e8e2876e8db066a9d`) and no `core.logger` (P05-S117).

**The result type and the constructor.**

- FACT: `ScoreResult` is a frozen dataclass with `total_score` ("0-100"), `tier`, `passed` ("total_score >= min_pass_score"), `step_scores` (weighted integers), `missing_steps`, `min_pass_score` and `tier_thresholds`, matching QS3's list (P05-S015).
- FACT: `QualityScorer.__init__(weights, logger)` keeps both, resolves three process-wide effect counters (`quality_scorer`, `scorer.tier_high`, `scorer.score_gt_ceiling`; A15's telemetry) and derives the step names (FIX-101): from `weights.steps.model_fields` when that is a `dict` (a real Pydantic model), otherwise from the numeric public attributes of the object (mock objects in tests); nothing is inside a `try` (P05-S016). The only non-test construction passes `app_config.scoring`, whose `steps` model has ten required `int` fields; current external context (Pydantic `BaseModel` reference, read 2026-11-21): accessing `model_fields` from an instance is deprecated and "will not work in Pydantic V3"; INTERPRETATION: under the pinned `pydantic==2.13.0` the first branch is taken and the ten names come in declaration order; the installed version is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P05-S017).

**`score()`: the arithmetic (Q9).**

- FACT (per step): for each step name, the configured weight is added to `total_weights_possible`; a name absent from `step_results` logs one WARNING ("excluded from scoring denominator"), goes into `missing_steps` and gets `step_scores[name] = 0`; otherwise `raw = float(step_results[name])`, `weighted = raw * weight`, `step_scores[name] = int(round(weighted))`, and `weighted` and the weight are added to the achieved and present totals; extra keys are ignored (P05-S018).
- FACT (the total): with present weights, `final_score = (total_achieved / total_weights_present) * 100.0` and `total_score = min(100, int(round(final_score)))`, with one INFO line giving the present and possible weights; with none, `total_score = 0` and a WARNING; the total is capped at 100 and has no floor (P05-S019).
- INTERPRETATION and current external context (Python `round()`, read 2026-11-21; a local check of the work environment's interpreter): `round()` without `ndigits` rounds half to even, so 59.5 gives 60, 60.5 gives 60 and 64.5 gives 64, and a weighted step value of 2.5 gives 2 and 7.5 gives 8; the total is computed from the unrounded values, so the rounded `step_scores` need not add up to it; the total is a percentage of the weights present (P05-S020).
- FACT (tier and `passed`): `total_score >= high_score_threshold` gives `"HIGH"` (and increments the `tier_high` counter), else `>= medium_score_threshold` `"MEDIUM"`, else `"LOW"`; `passed = total_score >= min_pass_score`; all three inclusive (P05-S021). The `score` counter is incremented on every call that reaches L138, and the `score_gt_ceiling` counter when the total exceeds the module constant 65, whose comment calls it "the G2 achievable ceiling — 25/100 pts dead-at-0 + 20 pinned-at-half make >65 algebraically impossible today"; the HIGH-tier comment says "unreachable today (IA-P2-03: threshold 80 > ceiling 65)"; the counters change no result (P05-S022).

**Failure behaviour and side effects (Q13).**

- FACT: the scorer has no `try`, `except` or `raise`; every configuration read, `float()`, `round()` and both divisions are unguarded (P05-S023). INTERPRETATION: on the screener's route every value is a float the executor stored, so `float()` does not raise; a NaN or infinite value would make `int(round(...))` raise (current external context, CPython source, read 2026-11-21), and no step route recorded by P04 returns one from finite quote fields; the INFO line's division is by zero only with a negative weight, which no schema check prevents and no committed weight has; any exception becomes `SKIPPED_SCORER_ERROR` at the screener (P05-S024). FACT and INTERPRETATION: the scorer reads and writes no store, broker, file or clock; its effects are its log lines (one INFO per screening on the OFF and shadow paths) and unlocked counter increments on process-wide objects; QS9's "Pure ... Thread-safe" holds for the returned value; whether concurrent increments lose counts is A15's matter (section 10) (P05-S025).

**Callers and the fields of the result that are used.**

- FACT: the only non-test call is the screener's on the OFF and shadow paths (the enforce path does not call the scorer); the processor stores the same scorer object and reads it nowhere else (P05-S026). Non-test code reads `total_score`, `tier` and `min_pass_score`; `passed`, `step_scores`, `missing_steps` and `tier_thresholds` are read by no non-test code (P05-S027). INTERPRETATION: the screener decides with its own comparison against the effective minimum (the strategy's `min_score` when above 0), while the scorer's `passed` uses the global minimum and is not read; at the committed files (`min_score: 0` in all 16) the two coincide; QS1 and QS10 give the decision and the override to the screener, which matches (P05-S028).

**Values at the committed weights on the live route: the ceilings, with the timeout condition.**

- INTERPRETATION (from the arithmetic and P04's step values; the committed values are FACT about the file only): on the OFF and shadow paths every step name is present, so the denominator is the full weight sum (100 at the committed file) and the proportional total equals the plain weighted sum; the missing-step and all-missing branches are not reached from the executor's output. When no step times out, steps 1 and 3 are always 0.0 and steps 4 and 6 always 0.5 on the live route, so the total cannot exceed 65 (P04-S107; P05-S029): `HIGH` (80) is unreachable, `MEDIUM` (65) is reached only at exactly 65, and a pass at the global minimum needs a total in [60, 65], a weighted sum of 59.5 being recorded as 60 (round half to even) and 64.5 as 64 (`LOW`) (P05-S029). The scorer's comments and the manifest's "expected-dormant" counters state the same unreachability; HISTORICAL: the legacy integrity audit's IA-P2-03 records it from production data (P05-S014, P05-S029).
- INTERPRETATION (the timeout condition; P05-AP28; Claude's assembly notes): P04's statement of the 65 ceiling (P04-S107) does not carry the timeout route that P04 itself records: a timed-out step is scored 0.5 whatever its live value (P04-S033, P04-S119). A timed-out step 1 adds 7.5 points and a timed-out step 3 adds 5, so with both timed out and every other step at its maximum the weighted sum is 77.5 and the total `round(77.5)` is 78 on the OFF path (P05-S029). The ceiling of 65 therefore holds only when no step times out: `HIGH` (80) stays unreachable either way, while `MEDIUM` above 65 and the `score_gt_ceiling` tripwire become reachable through timeouts, which the comment "make >65 algebraically impossible today" does not mention; on the enforce path the same route raises the V3 ceiling from 56 to 72 (section 7; P05-S068). HISTORICAL: the legacy M-S3 record says the timeout "has never fired" (P04-S053); whether any step timed out in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P04-S054; P05-U11).
- INTERPRETATION (the docstrings against the code): the FIX-042 docstring names a timed-out `circuit_check` as the case for the denominator exclusion, while the executor records a timed-out step as present with 0.5, so the scorer counts it at half its weight (P05-S030; P04-S111); QS3 and module 28 say "Missing step -> 0.0 contribution", while the code since FIX-042 (`0c28d6a`, 2026-05-14) excludes a missing step from the denominator (A19's test states the FIX-042 expectation); QS4's "total = sum(all weighted_scores)" and the weights file's "final score = sum of weights for passed steps" describe a sum, while the code computes a proportion of the weights present, which equals the sum only when the present weights total 100 (P05-S030).

**The scoring configuration as A07 reads it (Q9; A02's files and schema).**

- FACT about the committed `config/scoring_weights.yaml` only: step weights 15, 10, 10, 10, 15, 10, 5, 5, 10, 10 (sum 100); `min_pass_score: 60`; `high_score_threshold: 80`; `medium_score_threshold: 65`; `v3_hardgate_mode: "shadow"`; `v3_gate_steps: [circuit_check, signal_age]`; `v3_freshness_max_sec: 60.0` ("matches OLD signal_age>60s reject"); `v3_min_pass_score: 50`, `v3_high_score_threshold: 75`, `v3_medium_score_threshold: 56`. HISTORICAL: the header says "final score = sum of weights for passed steps"; the V3 block calls its thresholds "the analytic fresh-reference start (min 60→50 / med 65→56 / high 80→75)" that "MUST be data-fit before enforce", with the flip to enforce "ONLY after the offline parity recompute ... is signed off" (P05-S031).
- FACT (A02's schema, interface): the ten weights are required `int` fields with no validator; the three live thresholds are required `int`; the six `v3_*` fields have defaults (`"off"`, the two gate steps, 60.0, 50, 75, 56); validators accept the mode only in `off`, `shadow`, `enforce` and require `high > medium > min_pass` for both sets; no validator checks the sign or sum of the weights, a 0 to 100 range, that `v3_gate_steps` names steps, or the sign of `v3_freshness_max_sec`; A02's auditor repeats the ordering check as B5 (P05-S032).
- FACT (every scoring key A07 code reads) (P05-S033):

| Key (`scoring.`) | A07 reader | How read | Fallback in A07 code | Schema default | Committed value |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `steps.<name>` (10) | scorer L87; screener L103 to L106 | `getattr`; `model_dump()` | none; `{}` without a configuration | required | 15, 10, 10, 10, 15, 10, 5, 5, 10, 10 |
| `min_pass_score` | scorer L134, L148; screener L320 | attribute | none | required | 60 |
| `high_score_threshold` | scorer L122 | attribute | none | required | 80 |
| `medium_score_threshold` | scorer L123 | attribute | none | required | 65 |
| `v3_hardgate_mode` | screener L96 | `str(getattr(..., "off") or "off")` | `"off"` | `"off"` | `"shadow"` |
| `v3_gate_steps` | screener L97 to L99 | `set(getattr(...) or [])` | the two gate steps | the same | the same |
| `v3_freshness_max_sec` | `main.py` L3245, then `HardGate.__init__` | attribute, then `float()` | 60.0 | 60.0 | 60.0 |
| `v3_min_pass_score` | screener L100 | `int(getattr(..., 50))` | 50 | 50 | 50 |
| `v3_high_score_threshold` | screener L101 | `int(getattr(..., 75))` | 75 | 75 | 75 |
| `v3_medium_score_threshold` | screener L102 | `int(getattr(..., 56))` | 56 | 56 | 56 |

  Two other inputs reach the same code: the strategy field `min_score` (default 0) and `entry_gate.circuit_proximity_reject_enabled` (`true`), passed to both the HardGate and the screener (P05-S033).
- INTERPRETATION: every fallback in A07's code equals the schema default, and `main.py` always passes the validated `app_config.scoring`, so the fallbacks apply only to tests that pass no configuration (P05-S034). FACT and INTERPRETATION (`v3_gate_steps`): the screener uses the one set both as the executor's exclusions on the enforce path and as the steps excluded from the re-scaled total on both V3 paths, while the HardGate's tests are fixed in its code and do not read the key; a list other than the committed one would change which steps are scored while the gate would still test circuit and freshness (P05-S035).
- HISTORICAL (the history of `min_pass_score` and of the mode): the committed `min_pass_score` was 60 (2026-04-17), then 30 ("TEMP: Day 6 crash test"), 60, 55, 60, 55 and 60 (`61ae9cc`, 2026-07-10, "min_pass_score 55->60"), the baseline value; `v3_hardgate_mode` went from `"off"` to `"shadow"` on 2026-07-12; legacy records name a "LIVE edit + restart" 60→55 on 2026-07-06, a machine value of 30 during a crash test, and "min_pass was 55 from 06→10-Jul, else 60"; INTERPRETATION: the committed value changed six times after the first commit, and the legacy records name moments when a machine's value differed from the committed file; UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED: the value in force at any moment of production (P05-S036; section 13).
- FACT (A02 K-27, A07's side): `config/system_config.yaml` holds under `v3_chain:` a second `min_pass_score: 60`, `medium_score_threshold: 65` and `high_score_threshold: 80` ("forward-compat; 10a records the score, does NOT gate on it — SEEDS"), which no A07 file and no module under `v3_chain/`, `regime/` or `allocation/` reads; the only A07 readers of the three names are the scorer's reads of `app_config.scoring` and the screener's read of the result (P05-S037). The committed values satisfy both ordering checks (P05-S038).

## 7. The HardGate, the V3 modes and the five playbook gates (questions Q8, Q9, Q17; D-39 P1)

This section covers `screening/hard_gate.py` in full: the HardGate and the shared circuit-proximity rule, the re-scale helpers, the internals of the screener's two V3 methods (`_screen_v3_enforce`, `_log_v3_shadow_compare`; their routes are in section 4), and, under Rama's decision P1 (D-39), the five V3 playbook gates `gate_rr`, `gate_htf`, `gate_extreme`, `gate_confirm` and `gate_pullback`: their inputs, calculations, thresholds, units, missing-data and error behaviour and the returned `GateVerdict`. A18 owns their call sites, when its chain calls them and how it consumes the verdicts; A07 does not audit A18's chain; `gate_rr`'s arithmetic and output are recorded faithfully, the entry, stop, target, risk and R:R decisions staying A09's and the production of S&R inputs A08's. The callers are recorded at the interface only.

Evidence: `A07_QUALITY_SCORER_HARD_GATE_AND_V3_PLAYBOOK_GATES_2026-11-21.md` (P05). `screening/hard_gate.py` (466 lines, four commits from `536172c`, 2026-07-12, to `132e571`, 2026-08-01) is identical at `7d4970a` and the baseline; the two V3 methods changed after `7d4970a` only in their `_persist` keywords (P05-S002, P05-S118).

**Declared purpose and intent sources.**

- HISTORICAL: the module header calls it "The pre-scoring binary Hard-Gate battery" that, "For the CURRENT LIVE flow", houses only the rules relocated out of the score (at-circuit, circuit proximity and freshness at 60 s); "Liquidity is a NO-OP (spread_check stays in the 8-step score, A8)"; the V3 playbook gates are "PLAYBOOK-shadow scope — evaluated + logged on the V3 path only, they NEVER reject a live order"; the module also owns the shared re-scale helpers so the live V3 path and the offline parity tool use "ONE implementation"; `circuit_proximity_reason` is "the RELOCATED body" of the screener's rule; `GateVerdict`'s `passed=False` "short-circuits screen() to REJECTED_<reason>"; "evaluate() never raises"; the playbook gates are "PURE FUNCTIONS ... and, on the V3 path, LOG-ONLY", with "G-RR must-have → missing S&R FAILS", "G-HTF must-NOT → missing data PASSES", "G-EXTREME must-NOT → missing/OFF regime PASSES", and G-CONFIRM and G-PULLBACK "BOTH ... must-have: any missing input → FAIL" (P05-S010).
- HISTORICAL: the plan the header cites (`docs/v3/V3_STEP4_HARDGATE_SCORER_PLAN.md`) derives the threshold re-scale ("age ≤30s ...: new = 1.25·old − 25"; "age 30-60s ...: new = 1.25·old − 18.75"), tabulates the gate rules with a missing-data rule each (liquidity "fail-CLOSED"; freshness "missing `triggered_at` → admit"; the playbook rules), and leaves open the final thresholds ("MUST be data-fit"), the freshness cutoff ("the 90s-vs-60s-vs-600s ambiguity — needs a single authoritative number") and the per-strategy `min_score` on the eight-step scale (P05-S011). `PATHS.md` L203 records Step 4b as "IMPLEMENTED (11-Jul-2026) behind default-OFF flag" and a parity run whose data does not survive (P05-S012).
- HISTORICAL and FACT (the cited specification): the gates cite "the V3 DECISION CONTENT SPECIFICATION v1.0 §3"; no tracked file of that title exists at the baseline, and the legacy pending register records that it "does not exist ... A DECISION is missing, not a document" (P05-S013); other legacy records call it the authority, say the step was "Ratified via" it, or say the decision content "needs Rama/ChatGPT to specify"; INTERPRETATION: whether such a document existed outside the repository is stated differently by these records and is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED; the conflict is preserved (P05-S116; section 19). HISTORICAL: the legacy integrity audit (01-Aug-2026, from production data that does not survive) states that `HIGH` is unreachable and `MEDIUM` reachable only at 65 (IA-P2-03) and that the shadow gate showed "`gate=PASS` on 3,536/3,536 compares (31-Jul)" (IA-P2-05) (P05-S014).

**The HardGate (`screening/hard_gate.py` L1 to L171).**

- FACT: the live reasons are `GATE_AT_CIRCUIT = "AT_CIRCUIT"`, `GATE_CIRCUIT_PROXIMITY = "CIRCUIT_PROXIMITY"` and `GATE_STALE = "SIGNAL_AGE"` ("screen() maps these to REJECTED_<reason>"), and `DEFAULT_FRESHNESS_MAX_SEC = 60.0` ("matches the OLD signal_age>60s → 0.0 hard reject"); `GATE_STALE`'s value makes the enforce path's freshness reject the same string as the OFF path's step-10 reject (P05-S039). `GateVerdict` is a frozen dataclass with `passed`, `reason` (default `None`) and `evidence` (default `{}`), returned by the HardGate and by the five playbook gates (P05-S040).
- FACT: `HardGate.__init__(*, now_fn, logger=None, freshness_max_sec=60.0, circuit_proximity_reject_enabled=True)` keeps the clock, the cutoff and the switch and resolves an effect counter; the stored logger is never read, so the HardGate writes no log line of its own; the `datetime` import is unused. The only non-test construction passes `now_ist`, `scoring.v3_freshness_max_sec` and `entry_gate.circuit_proximity_reject_enabled`, unconditionally (P05-S041).
- FACT (`evaluate(*, trigger_price, triggered_at, direction, market_data)`): the counter is incremented first; then (1) at-circuit: `circuit_state` in `("upper_circuit", "lower_circuit")` gives `GateVerdict(False, "AT_CIRCUIT", {"circuit_state": cs})`; (2) proximity: the shared rule with the gate's switch gives `GateVerdict(False, "CIRCUIT_PROXIMITY", {"detail": reason})`; (3) freshness, only when `triggered_at` is not `None`: the age in seconds against the gate's clock (naive and aware values aligned as in step 10), `age_sec > freshness_max_sec` giving `GateVerdict(False, "SIGNAL_AGE", {"age_sec": age_sec})`; (4) otherwise `GateVerdict(True, None, {})`, with "liquidity = NO-OP (A8)" (P05-S042).
- INTERPRETATION: the first failing test decides; a stale signal at a circuit is reported `AT_CIRCUIT` only, and the clock is read only when the first two tests pass; the evidence holds only the failing test's data (P05-S043). Freshness: seconds, strict (an age of exactly 60 s passes, the same boundary as step 10), no lower bound (a future time passes; A06 WA-06), a missing `triggered_at` passes ("matches the OLD 0.5 default") (P05-S044). On the enforce path the gate reads the clock before the executor, so earlier than step 10 would; on the shadow path it reads it inside the comparison, after the steps and the scorer, so a signal at most 60 s old at step 10 and older at the gate is logged `gate=SIGNAL_AGE`; a timed-out step 10 records 0.5 whatever the age (P05-S045). At-circuit: the same strings as step 9, direction-blind, derived from the quote's last price, not the trigger price; a reject before scoring on the enforce path, a log entry on the shadow path, and only step 9's 0.0 on the OFF path (P05-S046).
- FACT and INTERPRETATION ("evaluate() never raises"): `evaluate` has no `try`; on the screener's routes its inputs are of the expected types, but an exception could still arise if `market_data` were not a mapping, `triggered_at` were neither `None` nor a time, `now_fn` raised or the subtraction mixed types; on the enforce path it would leave `screen()`, on the shadow path it would be caught and logged by the helper (P05-S047).

**The circuit-proximity rule (`circuit_proximity_reason`, L53 to L112).**

- FACT: it returns `None` when its switch is off; `_pos_num(x)` keeps only a positive `int` or `float` that is not a `bool`; the upper and lower limits and the entry (the trigger price) pass through it, and a missing entry or two missing limits return `None` (P05-S048). The margin is `DEFAULT_CIRCUIT_MARGIN_PCT = 0.02`, imported from A09's `orders/price_math.py` ("FIX-190 (Bug D): default safety margin inside the circuit band (2%)"), the same constant as A09's band clamp; `upper_ceiling = upper * 0.98`, `lower_floor = lower * 1.02`; a long is `LONG` or `BUY` after upper-casing (P05-S049). For a long entry `entry >= upper_ceiling` refuses ("no profitable TGT fits the band"), else `entry <= lower_floor` ("no valid SL fits the band"); for any other direction the same two tests in the other order with the texts swapped; otherwise `None` (inclusive) (P05-S050).
- INTERPRETATION: with limits 110 and 90 the ceiling is 107.8 and the floor 91.8 (P05-S051). The rule admits when the switch is off, when the entry is missing, zero, negative, a `bool`, not an `int` or `float`, or NaN, and when both limits are unusable; a positive infinite entry is refused by the leg that compares with an upper limit; a direction text other than `LONG` or `BUY` takes the SHORT legs and is not stripped of spaces (current external context, Python "Value comparisons", read 2026-11-21) (P05-S052). The rule's decision does not depend on the direction (both branches refuse at the same two bounds; the direction decides only the order and the text), and when `upper / lower` is at most about 1.0408 every positive entry is refused; whether any symbol had such a band is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P05-S119).
- FACT: the body is the same text, ignoring indentation, as the screener's former rule at the parent of `536172cfadfdd28ec9e5f8c00f9d549f0cd57dc2`, supporting "byte-identical logic" for the rule itself (P05-S053). Its two callers are the screener's R3 (OFF and shadow paths, the screener's switch) and the HardGate's second test (the gate's switch), both switches fed from one key (P05-S054).

**The shared re-scale helpers (L174 to L212).**

- FACT: `rescaled_total(step_results, step_weights, gate_steps)` sums `float(step_results[name]) * weight` and the weights over the names neither in `gate_steps` nor absent, returns 0 when no weight is present and otherwise `min(100, int(round(achieved / present * 100.0)))`, with no log, counter or `try` ("Mirrors quality_scorer's proportional formula on the kept subset") (P05-S055). INTERPRETATION: with no gate steps and the same weights it computes the scorer's total; it writes no warning, line or counter and returns no tier; both take their weights from the same `app_config.scoring` object; with the committed gate steps the denominator is the eight kept weights, 80 (P05-S056).
- FACT: `tier_for(score, high, medium)` is the scorer's inclusive rule, without the counter (P05-S057). `rescale_min_score(old)` returns 0 for a zero or negative value, otherwise `max(0, int(round(1.25 * old - 25.0)))`; its docstring says "All 15 strategies are currently 0, so this is inert today"; FACT: there are 16 committed strategy files, each `min_score: 0`, so the count differs while "inert" holds (P05-S058). INTERPRETATION: inputs 1 to 20 give 0; 60 gives 50, 65 gives 56, 80 gives 75; half-way results round to even; the plan's second map for ages of 30 to 60 s (`1.25·old − 18.75`) is used nowhere in the code; on the enforce path a `min_score` of 1 to 20 yields an effective minimum of 0 (P05-S059). FACT: the screener calls the helpers on the enforce path and in the shadow comparison; A18's parity script calls `rescaled_total` and `tier_for` (OLD and NEW totals) and not `rescale_min_score` (P05-S060).

**The screener's V3 modes: internals (ADDENDUM 3; routes in section 4).**

- FACT (`_screen_v3_enforce`): its docstring says "the Hard-Gate runs BEFORE scoring; survivors are scored on the 8 kept steps and judged against the re-scaled v3 thresholds; there is NO signal_age defense-in-depth"; E1 calls `evaluate` with the full market data and, on a failure, logs one WARNING with the reason and evidence and writes `REJECTED_<reason>` with `score=0`, `tier="LOW"`, `rejected_step` the reason in lower case and empty step data; the gate's evidence goes to the log only (P05-S061). E2 to E4 build the same thresholds, run the executor without the gate steps (which are then absent from `step_results`) and reject `REJECTED_STEP_ERROR` on any error step with no screener log line (P05-S062). E5 to E7: `rescaled_total` and `tier_for`; `eff_min` from `rescale_min_score(min_score)` when positive, else `v3_min_pass_score`; `total < eff_min` (strict) writes `REJECTED_SCORE_{total}`; otherwise `PASSED` with an INFO line ending "(v3-enforce)" (P05-S063). INTERPRETATION: the enforce path never calls the `QualityScorer` (so its lines, counters and live thresholds do not occur), applies neither R3 nor R8, skips the gate steps twice over, and reads `min_score` with a `getattr` guard where the OFF path reads it directly; at the committed files `eff_min` is 50 (P05-S064).
- FACT (`_log_v3_shadow_compare`): on the shadow path, after the scorer, it evaluates the gate with the same four inputs, computes the re-scaled total and tier over the OFF run's ten step results, and logs one INFO line "v3_shadow ... OLD score=... tier=... | NEW score=... tier=... gate=..." (`gate.reason or "PASS"`), all inside one `try`; it writes nothing and changes no field (P05-S065). INTERPRETATION: the line states neither the NEW decision (no NEW minimum, no pass or reject) nor the OLD one (computed after the helper); the NEW score uses the OFF run's step values, and the gate's clock reading is later than step 10's (P05-S066).
- INTERPRETATION and HISTORICAL (coverage of the comparison, against IA-P2-05): the comparison runs for every signal that reached the scorer without a step error; a signal refused by R3 ends before it; R3 refuses, in either direction, a trigger price at or above `0.98 × upper` or at or below `1.02 × lower`, so a quote at a circuit limit reaches the comparison (and would be logged `gate=AT_CIRCUIT`) only when its trigger price lies strictly inside those bounds (for example a 0.0 last price against a positive lower limit, A04 RD-12) or the switch is off; IA-P2-05's "the OLD path pre-rejects at-circuit/proximity BEFORE the compare runs" therefore matches the code for proximity and for at-circuit quotes within the 2 % margin, while the code has no at-circuit pre-reject of its own on these paths; the measured data does not survive (P05-S067).
- INTERPRETATION (what the V3 total can reach on the live route; the committed values; effective values UNKNOWN): with `circuit_check` and `signal_age` excluded the kept weights are 80; when no step times out steps 1 and 3 give 0.0 (25 of the 80 points) and steps 4 and 6 0.5 (10 of their 20 points withheld), so at most 45 of 80 points and a total of at most `int(round(56.25)) = 56`: `v3_high_score_threshold` 75 is unreachable, `v3_medium_score_threshold` 56 reached only at the maximum, and `v3_min_pass_score` 50 needs at least 39.6 points; a pass on the enforce path then needs step 2 (VWAP position) at 1.0, and a total of 56 needs steps 2, 7 and 8 at 1.0 and step 5 above 0.96. The timeout route applies here too: a timed-out step 2 (0.5) with every other kept step at its maximum gives 50, which passes, and timed-out steps 1 and 3 raise the kept sum to 57.5 and the total to `round(71.875)` = 72, so 75 stays unreachable while totals above 56 become reachable (P05-S068; section 6).
- INTERPRETATION (the OFF and enforce decisions compared, for a signal that passes the gate): with `a8` the points achieved on the eight kept steps, the OFF total is `round(a8 + c + g)` (`c` 10 for step 9 off-circuit; `g` 10 for an age up to 30 s, 5 for 30 to 60 s); the OFF path passes at 60 when `a8 >= 39.5` (up to 30 s) or `a8 >= 44.5` (30 to 60 s), and the enforce path at 50 when `a8 >= 39.6` whatever the age up to 60 s; the decisions differ for `a8` in [39.5, 39.6) up to 30 s (OFF passes, enforce rejects) and in [39.6, 44.5) for 30 to 60 s (OFF rejects, enforce passes), the "rounding-edge" and "age-band" residuals the plan and `PATHS.md` name; `g` is also 5 when `triggered_at` is missing or step 10 timed out, and `c` 5 when step 9 timed out; a quote at a circuit is a score reject on the OFF path and `REJECTED_AT_CIRCUIT` on enforce; a signal older than 60 s is a score reject on the OFF path at the committed values and `REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE` on enforce (P05-S069).

**The five V3 playbook gates (L215 to L466; D-39 P1).**

- FACT (common to the five): module-level functions returning a `GateVerdict`, added by `474e10b` (2026-07-12; `gate_rr`, `gate_htf`, `gate_extreme`) and `5a71fb6` (2026-07-13; `gate_confirm`, `gate_pullback`); reasons `RR`, `HTF`, `EXTREME`, `CONFIRM`, `PULLBACK`; `_is_long(side)` is `str(side).strip().upper() in ("BUY", "LONG")`; each body is wrapped in `try` / `except Exception`; no gate logs, counts, reads a clock, the store, a file, the broker or any configuration: every threshold is a parameter supplied by the caller, and only `gate_htf`'s two swing lists have defaults (P05-S070).
