INTERPRETATION (error routes): an exception in `gate_rr`, `gate_confirm` or `gate_pullback` gives a FAIL "error: ..."; in `gate_htf` or `gate_extreme` a PASS "error → pass (fail-open)"; the split follows the stated must-have and must-not rules (P05-S071).
- FACT (`gate_rr`, recorded as written; the decisions stay A09's): `gate_rr(side, entry, *, sl_zone_edge, tgt_zone_edge, atr30, rr_floor, sl_buffer_atr_mult)` with no defaults (P05-S072). A missing or non-positive `entry` gives FAIL "no entry price"; a missing zone edge FAIL "missing S&R zone (no safe SL or TGT)" (P05-S073). `buffer = sl_buffer_atr_mult * atr30` if `atr30` is truthy, else 0.0; for a long side `sl = sl_zone_edge - buffer`, `tgt = tgt_zone_edge`, `risk = entry - sl`, `reward = tgt - entry`; otherwise `sl = sl_zone_edge + buffer`, `tgt = tgt_zone_edge`, `risk = sl - entry`, `reward = entry - tgt`; `entry` is used as given (P05-S074). The evidence starts as `{"v3_sl": round(sl, 4), "v3_tgt": round(tgt, 4)}`; a risk or reward not above 0 gives FAIL "invalid geometry" with `v3_rr` `None` (P05-S075); `rr = reward / risk` is stored to four places; `rr >= rr_floor` passes, otherwise FAIL "R:R ... < floor ..."; any exception FAIL "error: ..." ("a gate must never raise into the shadow worker") (P05-S076).
- INTERPRETATION (`gate_rr` meaning and units): the stop is the supplied stop-side edge moved away by the buffer and the target the supplied target-side edge with no buffer; any side other than `BUY` or `LONG` takes the mirror arithmetic; prices are in the caller's price unit and the floor and multiplier are dimensionless; the gate selects no zone, does not round to a tick, test the circuit band or compare with the live stop or target; the caller decides which band edges are passed (A18), A08 produces the zones, and the entry, stop, target, risk and R:R decisions for live trades stay A09's (D-39, P1) (P05-S077). Worked figures at the inputs of A19's tests: BUY, entry 100, edges 95 and 115, ATR 2.0, floor 2.0, multiplier 0.20 give buffer 0.4, stop 94.6, risk 5.4, reward 15.0, R:R 2.7778, PASS; with a target edge of 104, R:R 0.7407, FAIL; SELL with edges 105 and 85 gives stop 105.4 and R:R 2.7778, PASS; BUY with a stop edge of 101 and ATR 0 gives risk −1.0, FAIL "invalid geometry" (P05-S078). An R:R equal to the floor passes; the decision uses the unrounded ratio while the text shows two places (P05-S079). A missing or zero ATR gives a zero buffer, not a failure; a negative ATR or multiplier moves the stop with no sign test; NaN inputs end in a FAIL (the floor test is false); an infinite entry is an invalid geometry; a text entry raises and fails (P05-S080). HISTORICAL and FACT: the docstring's formulas match the code; its statement that the values are returned "EVEN on a fail" holds for the geometry and floor failures and not for the no-entry, missing-zone and error failures (P05-S081).
- FACT (`gate_htf(side, *, htf_close, htf_ema, htf_swing_highs=None, htf_swing_lows=None)`): a missing close or EMA passes ("fail-open"); for a long side it fails only when `close < ema` and the last two swing highs descend; for any other side when `close > ema` and the last two swing lows ascend; an exception passes (P05-S082). INTERPRETATION: both conditions must hold; comparisons are strict; fewer than two swing values pass; the gate does not check that the series is hourly, as the docstring states; NaN values pass; A19's tests state the same expectations (P05-S083).
- FACT (`gate_extreme(regime_state)`): `None` passes; a truthy `extreme_flag` fails; otherwise it passes; an exception passes (P05-S084). INTERPRETATION (with A18's model at the interface): the docstring's "status UNKNOWN → PASS" describes a code that reads no status, only the flag; A18's fail-safe state sets the flag false, and `main.py` builds the regime engine with no exchange-status feed ("extreme_flag stays FALSE"), so on the wired path the gate passes at the baseline; the regime's production behaviour is A18's and UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P05-S085).
- FACT (`gate_confirm(*, candle_open, candle_high, candle_low, candle_close, candle_volume, level, baseline_5m_volume, min_body_frac, volume_mult)`): any of the seven data values `None` fails ("must-have"); a range not above 0 fails; a baseline not above 0 fails; then a close not above the level fails; `body_frac = abs(c - o) / (h - l) < min_body_frac` fails; `volume < volume_mult * baseline` fails; otherwise it passes; an exception fails; all failures carry `CONFIRM` (P05-S086). INTERPRETATION: a pass needs the close strictly above the level, a body fraction at least the minimum and a volume at least the multiple (inclusive); the gate has no side parameter and describes a long confirmation only; the body is direction-blind; the baseline is computed by the caller (A18's); a `None` input fails, but a NaN input can pass every test because ordered comparisons with NaN are false (P05-S087).
- FACT (`gate_pullback(*, level, session_low, lowest_5m_close, atr30, proximity_pct, proximity_atr_mult, hold_buffer_atr_mult)`): any of the four data values `None` fails; `proximity = max(proximity_pct * level, proximity_atr_mult * atr30)`; `hold_floor = level - hold_buffer_atr_mult * atr30`; `touched = session_low <= level + proximity`; `held = lowest_5m_close >= hold_floor`; not touched fails ("no touch ... straight-line run"), not held fails ("the S-R flip failed"), otherwise it passes; an exception fails; all failures carry `PULLBACK` (P05-S088). INTERPRETATION: "touched" is one-sided; both tests are inclusive; `proximity_pct` is a fraction of the level; an ATR of 0 is not "missing"; unlike `gate_confirm`, NaN data inputs fail; a NaN `proximity_atr_mult` is dropped by `max()` (current external context, Python `max()`, read 2026-11-21); the gate is long-only by construction (P05-S089).
- FACT (the gate parameters, A02's schema and file, FACT about the files only; the callers are A18's): `rr_floor` 2.0 (validated `> 0`), `sl_buffer_atr_mult` 0.20, `atr30_period` 14 (validated `> 0`), `htf_ema_period` 20, `htf_swing_pivot_n` 3, `confirm_min_body_frac` 0.50, `confirm_volume_mult` 1.20, `pullback_proximity_pct` 0.005, `pullback_proximity_atr_mult` 0.50, `hold_buffer_atr_mult` 0.20, all under `v3_chain:` and equal to the `V3ChainConfig` defaults; the file calls them "ALL SEEDS to be calibrated from shadow data"; no validator checks the others (P05-S090). INTERPRETATION: no surviving source states the intended values or their derivation; the values in force are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P05-S091).
- FACT and INTERPRETATION (summary of the five) (P05-S092):

| Gate | Reason | Stated rule | A `None` input | A NaN input | An exception | Pass condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `gate_rr` | `RR` | must-have, missing fails | entry or either edge: FAIL; `atr30`: buffer 0 | FAIL | FAIL "error" | risk > 0, reward > 0, `rr >= rr_floor` |
| `gate_htf` | `HTF` | must-not, missing passes | close or EMA: PASS; swings treated as empty | PASS | PASS (fail-open) | not (close beyond EMA against the side and last two swings against the side; strict) |
| `gate_extreme` | `EXTREME` | must-not, missing passes | PASS | not applicable | PASS (fail-open) | `extreme_flag` falsy |
| `gate_confirm` | `CONFIRM` | must-have, missing fails | FAIL | can PASS | FAIL "error" | close > level; body fraction >= minimum; volume >= multiple × baseline |
| `gate_pullback` | `PULLBACK` | must-have, missing fails | FAIL | FAIL (a NaN `proximity_atr_mult` is dropped by `max()`) | FAIL "error" | session low <= level + proximity; lowest close >= hold floor |

**The callers of the playbook gates and helpers, at the interface only (A18's code; Q17).**

- FACT: no A07 file imports `regime/`, `v3_chain/` or `allocation/`; every dependency runs from A18's code to A07's (P05-S093).
- FACT (`v3_chain/runner.py`, the Step 10a worker): it imports `gate_rr`, `gate_htf`, `gate_extreme` and their reasons; it passes to `gate_rr` the signal's side and entry price, the zone edges it selects (long: the nearest support's and resistance's `band_low`; short: their `band_high`; `None` when absent), its 30-minute ATR and the `v3_chain` floor and multiplier; to `gate_htf` an hourly close, EMA and swing pivots; to `gate_extreme` the signal's regime state; it reads back `passed`, `reason` and `evidence`, forms `WOULD_REJECT_<reason>` from the first failing gate in the order EXTREME, HTF, RR (else `WOULD_PASS_GATES`), and copies `v3_sl`, `v3_tgt` and `v3_rr` beside the live entry, stop and target ("LOG-ONLY — recorded, never enforced") (P05-S094). FACT (A06's file, interface): the runner's signal is built by the processor after sizing with the processor's derived entry price and the screening result's score, tier, step values and snapshot, and passed to `v3_chain.observe` inside a `try` that logs and ignores any error; INTERPRETATION: on this path `gate_rr`'s `entry` is the derived entry price, not the trigger price, and only signals that passed screening and sizing reach the gates (P05-S095).
- FACT (`v3_chain/pb01_runner.py`): it imports all five gates; it calls `gate_rr` with side `"BUY"`, a stop edge `min(pullback_low, level)`, the nearest resistance's `band_low` and the `v3_chain` floor and multiplier, computes its own `pb01_sl` with the same buffer expression, calls `gate_htf` and `gate_extreme`, and recomputes `gate_confirm` and `gate_pullback` "to record the evidence ... They already passed"; it forms the verdict text from the first failing gate in the order EXTREME, HTF, RR, PULLBACK, CONFIRM (P05-S096). FACT and INTERPRETATION (`v3_chain/pb01_entry.py`): for each closed five-minute candle it calls `gate_pullback` and, on a pass, `gate_confirm`, whose pass marks A18's watchlist row `CONSUMED` and hands a confirmation to the runner; before the gates the caller applies its own hold floor (none when the ATR is falsy) and ends the row `INVALIDATED` below it; on this path two verdicts decide a state transition of A18's watchlist row ("ANALYSIS ONLY / G-NO-ORDER"), and the caller's hold floor repeats the gate's with a different treatment of a falsy ATR (P05-S097).
- FACT and INTERPRETATION (`scripts/v3_hardgate_parity_recompute.py`, A18's): it reconstructs the HardGate's outcome from stored step values (`circuit_check == 0.0` "AT_CIRCUIT", `signal_age == 0.0` "SIGNAL_AGE") rather than calling `evaluate`, does not re-derive proximity, and keeps a copy of the committed weights (A02 T-20); the reconstruction's adequacy is A18's (P05-S098). FACT (activation, A18's; FACT about the files only): `v3_chain_mode: "shadow"` and `watchlist.enabled: true`; `main.py` builds the Step 10a runner only when the mode is not `"off"` and the PB-01 stage only when the watchlist is enabled, each wiring inside a `try` that continues without the component; whether either ran is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P05-S099). FACT: A07's only contact with `regime/` is `gate_extreme`'s read of `extreme_flag` on the object its caller passes (P05-S100).
- INTERPRETATION (Q17, the line between A07 and A18): A07's side is the five pure gate functions returning `passed`, `reason` and `evidence`, the three re-scale helpers, and the screener's own V3 modes; A18's side is which inputs are computed and passed, when the calls are made, how the verdicts are combined into a would-be verdict or a watchlist transition, and what is recorded; nothing returned by a playbook gate reaches the screener, the screening result or a live status at the baseline (P05-S101).

**Hard gates, scores and log-only checks; names against semantics (Q8).**

- INTERPRETATION (classification by path) (P05-S102):

| Component | OFF path | Shadow path (committed) | Enforce path | Advances or defers? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `QualityScorer.score` | a score and tier; a reject only through the screener's minimum | the same | not called | no |
| `circuit_proximity_reason` | hard reject before the steps (R3) | the same, and evaluated again in the shadow gate for the log | inside the HardGate: hard reject before the steps | no |
| HardGate at-circuit | not run (step 9 scores 0.0) | log only | hard reject (`REJECTED_AT_CIRCUIT`) | no |
| HardGate freshness | not run (step 10 scores; R8 after the score test) | log only | hard reject (`REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE`) | no |
| `rescaled_total`, `tier_for` | not run | log only | the score and tier compared and stored | no |
| `rescale_min_score` | not run | not run | the per-strategy minimum (when `min_score > 0`) | no |
| the five playbook gates | not called by A07 code | not called by A07 code | not called by A07 code | A18's callers record the verdicts; in the PB-01 entry stage two verdicts decide an analysis-only watchlist transition |

- INTERPRETATION: none of these components can advance, defer or park a live signal; the screener's outcomes after them are reject, skip or `PASSED` only (P05-S103). Names, comments and documents differ from the code at nineteen points (P05-S104): (a) the "Hard-Gate" rejects only on the enforce path; (b) "evaluate() never raises" without a `try`; (c) "Liquidity is a NO-OP" against the plan's "fail-CLOSED"; (d) `GATE_STALE` carrying `"SIGNAL_AGE"`; (e) QS3's "0.0 contribution", QS4's "sum" and the weights file's "sum of weights for passed steps" against FIX-042's proportion; (f) FIX-042's timed-out step "excluded" against 0.5 present; (g) QS9's "Pure ... Thread-safe" against the log lines and counters; (h) `ScoreResult.passed` unread and computed with the global minimum; (i) the tier ladders against the reachable ranges (65 and 56 without timeouts, 78 and 72 with timed-out steps 1 and 3); (j) `v3_gate_steps` steering the screener while the gate's battery is fixed; (k) "All 15 strategies" against 16 files; (l) `gate_extreme`'s "status UNKNOWN" against a code that reads no status; (m) `gate_rr`'s "EVEN on a fail" holding for two of five failure routes; (n) the must-have rule against NaN inputs passing `gate_confirm`; (o) "LOG-ONLY" against the PB-01 entry stage's transition (A18's use); (p) stale line references in `config/expected_managers.yaml` (A02's file); (q) the HardGate's unused logger; (r) the "make >65 algebraically impossible today" comments against the timeout route; (s) QS8's import list against the scorer's imports.

**Thresholds, units, formulae, rounding and boundaries; configured and effective values (Q9).**

- FACT and INTERPRETATION (summary; committed values FACT about the files only; every effective value UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED) (P05-S105):

| Quantity | Formula | Source | Units | Rounding | Boundary | Committed value |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Scorer total | `min(100, int(round(Σ raw·w / Σ w_present × 100)))` | `scoring.steps.*` | percentage of the weights present | half to even; cap 100, no floor | 0 when no weight present | 15, 10, 10, 10, 15, 10, 5, 5, 10, 10 |
| Scorer tier | `>= high` HIGH, `>= medium` MEDIUM, else LOW | `high_score_threshold`, `medium_score_threshold` | points | none | inclusive | 80, 65 |
| OFF-path minimum (screener) | `total < effective_min` rejects | `min_score` if > 0, else `min_pass_score` | points | none | strict reject | 0 (all 16); 60 |
| HardGate freshness | `age_sec > freshness_max_sec` rejects | `v3_freshness_max_sec` | seconds | none | strict; no lower bound | 60.0 |
| HardGate at-circuit | `circuit_state` in the two strings | code constants | text | — | exact | — |
| Circuit proximity | `entry >= upper×(1−m)` or `entry <= lower×(1+m)` refuses, either direction | `m` = A09's 0.02; switch `entry_gate.circuit_proximity_reject_enabled` | prices; `m` a fraction | none | inclusive | 0.02 (code); `true` |
| V3 total | as the scorer over the kept steps | `steps.*`, `v3_gate_steps` | percentage of the kept weights (80) | half to even; cap 100 | 0 when none present | 80 kept |
| V3 tier | `tier_for` | `v3_high_score_threshold`, `v3_medium_score_threshold` | points | none | inclusive | 75, 56 |
| V3 minimum | `rescale_min_score(min_score)` if > 0, else `v3_min_pass_score`; strict reject | strategy `min_score`; coefficients 1.25 and 25 in code | points | half to even; floor 0 | strict | 0; 50 |
| `gate_rr` | buffer, stop, risk, reward and `rr = reward / risk` as above | caller: `rr_floor`, `sl_buffer_atr_mult` | prices; ratio | evidence 4 places, text 2; decision unrounded | risk, reward > 0; `rr >= floor` | 2.0, 0.20 |
| `gate_htf` | close beyond EMA and last two swings against the side | caller's EMA and swings | prices | none | strict | 20, 3 |
| `gate_extreme` | `extreme_flag` truthy fails | caller's regime state | flag | — | — | — |
| `gate_confirm` | close > level; body fraction; volume multiple | caller: `confirm_min_body_frac`, `confirm_volume_mult`, `baseline_candles_per_session` | prices; volumes; fraction; multiplier | evidence 4 places | strict close; inclusive body and volume | 0.50, 1.20, 75 |
| `gate_pullback` | touched and held as above | caller: `pullback_proximity_pct`, `pullback_proximity_atr_mult`, `hold_buffer_atr_mult` | prices; fraction; multipliers | evidence 4 places | inclusive | 0.005, 0.50, 0.20 |

- INTERPRETATION (configured to effective): every configured value is FACT about the committed file only; the committed `min_pass_score` changed six times and legacy records name moments when a machine's value differed, so no committed value can be taken as the value in force (section 13); several quantities are code constants (the 0.02 margin in A09's file, the HardGate's battery and order, the tier rule, the coefficients 1.25 and 25, the circuit-state strings and the tripwire 65), and whether P9b's "All weights, thresholds, and tier boundaries" was meant to cover coefficients, a margin and a telemetry constant is stated by no source (P05-S106). Configuration not used as declared: `v3_gate_steps` steers the screener's exclusions but not the gate's battery; the three `v3_chain` score thresholds are read by no code; `ScoreResult.passed` decides nothing; the proximity rule's decision ignores the direction it is given (P05-S114).

**Inherited items on this section's side** (section 16 has every row): A02 T-20 (P05-S107: the screener's `v3_*` `getattr` defaults equal the schema defaults; two further fallbacks and several code constants not in the weights file; no 90-second value in the scorer or the gate) and K-27 (P05-S108: A07 reads the three score thresholds from `app_config.scoring` only; the `v3_chain` copies have no reader); A04 RD-12 (P05-S109: the proximity rule reads the trigger price; the HardGate reads only the derived circuit state); A06 WA-06 (P05-S110: the gate's freshness test has no lower bound), WA-07 (P05-S111: a NaN trigger price is admitted by the proximity rule; a positive infinity is refused by the upper-limit leg) and PQ-08 (P05-S112: the gate carries a 60 s age check of its own, a reject only on the enforce path; neither route carries P9a's 90 s).

## 8. The entry gate and its resume contract (questions Q10, Q11, Q12; D-39 P2)

This section covers `screening/entry_gate.py` in full (Rama's decision P2, D-39): its declared contract and intent sources, `WatchEntry`, the constructor, `start()` and the rehydration from `gate_state`, `add()` and its persistence, `remove()`, the poll loop and `_check_one`, `_release()` and the callback, `stop()`, `clear_all()` and the end-of-day call site, threading and locking, and failure behaviour; then the callback through A01's `main.py` wiring to A06's `SignalProcessor.continue_from_gate`, whose inputs, outputs, statuses, decision contract and handoff to A09 are recorded at the interface only (A07 does not audit its internal orchestration, which stays A06's; A06's records are not edited); the `strategy_will_trade` logic invoked on the resume path at L2062 is section 3's.

The fixed wording applies throughout: `EntryGate.add()` has no non-test caller at the baseline, so the normal live path that creates a new gate entry is not established; `EntryGate.start()` rehydrates any persisted `gate_state` rows and can poll and release them; whether such rows existed in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P06-S006).

Evidence: `A07_ENTRY_GATE_AND_RESUME_CONTRACT_2026-11-21.md` (P06). The file (537 lines, ten commits, the last `132e571e161c54f0ac944c1e8e2876e8db066a9d`, 2026-08-01) is identical at `7d4970a26991fd40774023f5293a00556228e14b` (P06-S002, P06-S017); current external context: the Python `concurrent.futures` and `threading` pages, read 2026-11-21 (P06-S005).

**Declared contract and intent sources.**

- HISTORICAL: the module docstring says the gate holds "screened signals in a price-watchlist", polls the last price at a fixed interval and releases to `on_release` when the price "retraces into entry_price +/- tolerance zone", or on a hard time-out (EG6) or three consecutive quote failures (EG9); its fifteen locked decisions include EG7 ("Release protocol: remove -> state_store -> log -> on_release"), EG10 ("_watchlist guarded by Lock, snapshot outside lock"), EG14 ("Integration with signal_processor deferred to Module 33") and EG15 ("NOT in scope: wiring, non-price strategies, priority ordering"); it "Does not wire into signal_processor (Module 33's job)" (P06-S007). FACT: the module 33 instruction file names neither the gate nor `continue_from_gate`; INTERPRETATION: the module EG14 names as the integrator does not mention the gate (P06-S008).
- HISTORICAL: the gate's design instruction (module 31) says a signal that passes screening for a strategy with `pullback_wait_enabled=True` "is held in a watchlist until price retraces to the entry level (or timeout). Only then does it flow to sizing + placement"; P11a gives a 5 s poll and a 180 s default time-out; EG2 lists the reasons `"PRICE_HIT" | "TIMEOUT"` only; EG5 one symmetric window for both directions; EG7 "Release MUST NOT raise" (P06-S009). Module 34's MAIN18 asks for `continue_from_gate(entry)` on `PRICE_HIT` and "no action" on `TIMEOUT` or `QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` (P06-S010). The `gate_state` banner in A03's schema says the table is "written on entry_gate.add(); deleted on entry_gate._release()" (P06-S011).
- HISTORICAL and FACT: an audit closure asked to rehydrate from signals with status `GATE_WAITING`; the implementation (`13b3d093a7e931be61c478d9a0c297221960596f`, 2026-04-25) reads the `gate_state` table, not `signals`; INTERPRETATION: the stated design and the implementation differ (P06-S012). HISTORICAL: the FIX-046 commit (`9e31a4fd7fbf388ff3023188709e5ae4d5f0875e`, 2026-05-14) says "Yesterday's waiting signals no longer fire at 09:15 next morning" and "EOD sequence now includes gate.clear_all() as step 0", and changed no `main.py` line (P06-S013).
- HISTORICAL (reachability statements; none is evidence of production behaviour): the legacy integrity audit (01-Aug-2026, IA-P2-01) says the gate "is constructed, started and cleared at EOD on every boot — and nothing has ever put an entry into it" and "The divert ... was never wired", citing runtime counts that no surviving evidence verifies (P06-S014); the gate's own comment ("dormant tripwire ... nothing has ever fed this gate"), the processor's comments ("the two resume-path dispatch sites are production-unreachable"; "This path is DORMANT today: nothing calls EntryGate.add(), so only gate_state rows rehydrated at boot can reach it") and the manifest's "expected-dormant" entry say the same; FACT: the manifest's line references do not match the baseline (P06-S015); a test docstring of 2026-07-14 says the opposite ("EntryGate pullback path active"); INTERPRETATION: the sources conflict, and neither side is evidence of production behaviour (P06-S016).

**`WatchEntry`, the constant and the constructor.**

- FACT: `_MAX_QUOTE_FAILURES = 3` is the only module-level setting, not configurable (P06-S018). `WatchEntry` is a frozen dataclass with fifteen fields: `signal_id`, `symbol`, `direction` (`"LONG" | "SHORT"`), `trigger_price`, `entry_price` ("derived entry from _derive_prices"), `sl_price`, `tgt_price`, `tolerance_pct` and `timeout_sec` (commented as the strategy's `pullback_wait_*` fields), `strategy_name`, `tier`, `scanner_name`, `intent`, `added_at` ("naive IST") and `extras` (P06-S019; HISTORICAL docstring P06-S020); it validates nothing, and its `extras` dict is mutable (P06-S021). INTERPRETATION: the intended producer is the processor after screening and price derivation, with the strategy's tolerance and time-out; no non-test code builds a `WatchEntry` outside the rehydration, so this intent is not implemented at the baseline (P06-S022).
- FACT: the constructor takes `quote_fn`, `logger`, `state_store`, `on_release`, `poll_interval_sec=5.0` and `worker_count=2`, raises `ValueError` for a non-positive interval, floors the worker count at 1, and creates the watchlist, one lock, the failure-count dict and a stop event (P06-S023). The only non-test construction (`main.py` L3839 to L3844) passes `broker_adapter.get_quote`, the store and `_make_gate_release_cb(signal_processor)`, and neither the interval nor the worker count; no configuration key feeds either (A02's `entry_gate` section declares slippage, liquidity, R:R and circuit-proximity keys that `main.py` gives to other components) (P06-S024). INTERPRETATION: the 5.0 s interval and the two workers are fixed by the code, and the `entry_gate.*` keys do not govern the gate itself (P06-S025). FACT: the gate's effect counter is incremented only in `add()` (P06-S026).

**`start()` and the rehydration from `gate_state`.**

- FACT: `start()` returns with a WARNING if already running; otherwise it rehydrates, clears the stop event, creates a two-worker pool (`eg-worker`) and a daemon poll thread (`eg-poll`), sets the running flag, starts the thread and logs (P06-S027); HISTORICAL: "A mid-session restart therefore resumes watching the same signals at the same prices/timeouts" (P06-S028).
- FACT (`rehydrate_from_gate_state`): it reads every row through `get_all_gate_state()` (a store failure logs ERROR and returns 0); per row it parses `added_at` (converting an aware value to naive IST), `extras_json` (`{}` when empty) and the numeric columns with `float()` and `int()`; a malformed row logs a WARNING and is skipped; under the lock it skips a `signal_id` already present and inserts the others; it returns the count (P06-S029). It reads no `signals` row or status, tests no age, elapsed time-out, trading date, direction, positive or finite price, tolerance range or strategy, writes no status, and does not delete a skipped row; an expired row is inserted like any other (P06-S030). FACT (A03's store, interface): the read is `SELECT * FROM gate_state ORDER BY added_at ASC` inside a transaction (which takes the write lock, A03 Q-13); the columns match the fields, `signal_id` is the primary key referencing `signals` (P06-S031).
- INTERPRETATION (current external context, Python `float()` and `int()`, read 2026-11-21): the rehydration passes a stored NaN or infinity into the entry without a test, truncates a fractional time-out, and accepts a JSON `extras` that is not an object; the gate's own writer would store such values only if its caller supplied them, and `add` has no non-test caller (P06-S032). FACT and INTERPRETATION: `start` makes no broker call and can raise only if the pool or thread creation fails; no broker or limiter exception can escape it (P06-S033). FACT (interface with A01): `main.py` L3975 calls `entry_gate.start()` once, after `signal_processor.start()` ("gate may call continue_from_gate() immediately on release; workers must be ready"), outside any `try` (A01 C15) (P06-S034). INTERPRETATION: any rehydrated entry is checked in the first poll cycle while the main thread continues the boot; the code fixes no order between a boot-time release and the later boot steps (P06-S035).

**`add()`, `_persist_gate_state()`, `remove()` and who can create a gate entry.**

- FACT (`add`): it increments the counter (before the duplicate test, so a rejected duplicate is still counted), raises `ValueError` on a duplicate, inserts in memory, then, inside one `try`, writes the `gate_state` row and the status `GATE_WAITING`; any failure is logged and not raised ("in-memory add still applies"); it works whether or not the gate is started (P06-S036, P06-S039; HISTORICAL docstring P06-S037). The memory insert precedes both writes, which are two transactions; a failed row insert skips the status update; the status write passes no reason and no `ts`, so `rejection_reason` becomes NULL (A03 P-11 case (5)) (P06-S038). `_persist_gate_state` writes the fifteen fields with `INSERT OR REPLACE`, using `now_ist().isoformat()` when `added_at` is `None` (INTERPRETATION: an aware string against the column comment "naive IST"; A03 H-11) (P06-S040). FACT: `remove` changes memory only and leaves the row and the status (P06-S041); `watchlist`, `size` and `is_running` are read-only accessors (P06-S042).
- FACT (callers at the baseline): the non-test calls of the gate's public methods are `start()` (`main.py` L3975), `stop()` (`main.py` L1748) and `clear_all()` (`orders/eod_squareoff.py` L343); no non-test code calls `add`, `remove`, `watchlist`, `size`, `is_running` or the rehydration (P06-S043). The only SQL that inserts into `gate_state` is reached only from `add`; history searches find no committed non-test caller of `add()` and no non-test construction of `WatchEntry` in any version up to the baseline; INTERPRETATION: no committed version contained a caller (P06-S044). FACT and UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, in the fixed wording: `EntryGate.add()` has no non-test caller at the baseline, so the normal live path that creates a new gate entry is not established; `EntryGate.start()` rehydrates any persisted `gate_state` rows and can poll and release them; whether such rows existed in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED; a row could still have come from outside the committed code (a restored database, manual SQL, a test run against the production database), none of which is established (P06-S045).
- FACT (A06's file, interface only): the only non-test reader of `pullback_wait_enabled` is the processor at L1082, which decides only whether the FIX-067 live-price re-anchor runs (A09's decision); its comment says "Pullback strategies skip this (EntryGate already waits for current price)"; no line routes a pullback strategy's signal to the gate; FACT about the committed files only: six of the sixteen strategy files set `pullback_wait_enabled: true` (P06-S046). INTERPRETATION: a pullback strategy's signal that passes screening continues on the direct path without the gate's wait and without the re-anchor (unless A08's retest diverter parks it first, under its own switch); the comment states a premise the code does not provide; the processor's code and comment are A06's, the gate side (nothing feeds the gate) A07's; whether this was the production behaviour depends on the commit and configuration that ran (UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED) (P06-S047).

**The poll loop and `_check_one`.**

- FACT (`_poll_loop`): while the stop event is not set it copies the watchlist under the lock, submits `_check_one(entry)` for each entry, waits for each future in turn with a 7 s limit (interval plus 2.0) inside `try` / `except Exception: pass`, logs DEBUG, and waits for the rest of the interval; the first cycle starts at once (P06-S048). INTERPRETATION (current external context): an exception escaping a check, and a wait time-out, are discarded with no log; a check still running after 7 s keeps running; the next cycle can submit a second check of the same entry, so two checks can run at once (P06-S049).
- FACT (`_check_one`; docstring "Never raises -- all exceptions caught and logged (EG12)"): (1) return if the entry is no longer in the watchlist; (2) time-out: the wall-clock elapsed time from `added_at` (IST attached when naive), `elapsed_sec >= timeout_sec` releases `TIMEOUT`; (3) the quote read inside a `try`: `float(quote.last_price)` for the entry's symbol, a missing symbol raising `KeyError`, a success clearing the failure count; (4) on any exception the count rises by one, and at three a release `QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` (ERROR), otherwise a WARNING; (5) the price trigger: `tolerance = entry_price * tolerance_pct`, and `lower <= ltp <= upper` releases `PRICE_HIT` with the price, otherwise the entry waits (P06-S050).
- FACT: the time-out test is inclusive and comes before the quote read; the price window is inclusive, symmetric and the same for both directions (the gate reads `direction` nowhere); `tolerance_pct` is a fraction of the entry price; A02's schema validates the two `pullback_wait_*` fields, but the gate does not apply those validations to a rehydrated row (P06-S051). FACT and INTERPRETATION: the elapsed time is wall-clock time, so a host clock change moves it, and an `added_at` in the future delays the time-out (P06-S052). INTERPRETATION: a rehydrated row whose time-out has already passed at the first check (which runs late in the boot) is released `TIMEOUT` without a quote read; only a restart within the row's `timeout_sec` (180 s at the schema default and in all sixteen files) can reach a `PRICE_HIT` (P06-S053).
- FACT (A04's quote read, interface): one `get_quote([symbol])` per entry per cycle, taking a rate-limiter token; the gate reads only `last_price` (0.0 when absent) (P06-S054); limiter refusals and aborts, broker errors and a missing symbol all count as quote failures (P06-S055). INTERPRETATION: the failure count is per entry and in memory only (a restart starts each rehydrated entry at zero); a 0.0 price counts as a success and, for an entry with a positive entry price and a tolerance below 1, never fires `PRICE_HIT`, so such an entry ends by `TIMEOUT` (A04 RD-12's "does not fire on 0.0" holds under those two conditions), while a row with a tolerance of 1 or more or an entry price of 0 would fire on 0.0; NaN values make both comparisons false (P06-S056). INTERPRETATION: the time-out and price arithmetic and the calls to `_release` are outside the `try`, so an exception there escapes `_check_one` and is discarded by the poll loop without a log (P06-S057); a quote read that blocks and then returns a price in the window releases `PRICE_HIT` even if the time-out passed during the read (P06-S058). FACT (A19, stated expectations only): the gate's tests expect hits at the window's boundaries, a time-out, a release after three quote failures, a reset on success and independence between symbols (P06-S059).

**`_release()`, the status map and the callback.**

- FACT (`_release(entry, reason, release_ltp=None)`, in order): (1) under the lock, return silently if the entry is gone, otherwise remove it and its failure count; (2) compute the elapsed time for the log; (3) map the reason: `PRICE_HIT` to `GATE_RELEASED_PRICE_HIT`, `TIMEOUT` to `GATE_RELEASED_TIMEOUT`, `QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` to `GATE_RELEASED_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE`, any other to `GATE_RELEASED_<reason>`; (4) inside a `try`, `release_gate_state(signal_id, status)`, a failure logged and not raised; (5) an INFO line; (6) for a price, a new entry with `extras["release_ltp"]`; (7) inside a `try`, `on_release(entry, reason)`, a failure logged and not raised (P06-S060). The module calls `_release` with the three literal reasons only, so the fallback status is not reached, and `release_ltp` is set only for `PRICE_HIT` (P06-S061).
- FACT (A03's store, interface): `release_gate_state` deletes the row and updates `signals.status` and `rejection_reason` (NULL) in one transaction, with no from-status condition, so it overwrites whatever status the row holds; the three statuses fall in the schema's `GLOB 'GATE_*'` family (P06-S062); HISTORICAL: FIX-010 made the two changes atomic "so a crash between the two cannot leave a zombie gate_state row" (P06-S063). FACT and INTERPRETATION: the code's order (remove, store, log, callback) matches EG7 in the module docstring; the in-code comments and the design file place the log after the callback; the sources disagree only on the log line's position (P06-S064).
- INTERPRETATION (A03 Q-11): when the store call fails, its transaction rolls back, the row and the old status remain, the entry is already out of memory and the callback still runs, so a `PRICE_HIT` resumption proceeds while the stored state shows the entry waiting, and at the next start the row can be rehydrated, released and resumed a second time; A19's test states the callback-after-store-failure behaviour as expected (P06-S065). INTERPRETATION: steps 2, 3, 5 and 6 are outside any `try`; for a `PRICE_HIT`, a rehydrated `extras` that is not a dict (a JSON list or string, or a stored `null`) makes step 6 raise after the store call and before the callback, leaving the signal at `GATE_RELEASED_PRICE_HIT` with no row, no watchlist entry, no resumption and no log; whether any such row existed is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P06-S066).
- FACT (the callback contract as the gate defines it): `on_release(entry, reason)` is called with the entry (carrying `release_ltp` on `PRICE_HIT`) and one of the three reasons, on the `eg-worker` thread, outside the lock, after the status write; its return value is ignored and its exceptions are logged and swallowed (P06-S067). FACT: `TIMEOUT` and `QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` leave the signal at their `GATE_RELEASED_*` status with no row and no further action on the gate path; `PRICE_HIT` leaves `GATE_RELEASED_PRICE_HIT` until `continue_from_gate` writes `PROCESSING` (P06-S068).

**`stop()`, `clear_all()` and the end-of-day call site (A12's, interface only).**

- FACT (`stop`): if running, it sets the stop event, joins the poll thread for 5 s (with no `is_alive()` test), shuts the pool down with `wait=False, cancel_futures=False`, sleeps an unconditional 3 s and clears the flag; it does not clear the watchlist, write to the store or wait for the callback's work (P06-S069); HISTORICAL: "join within 5s (poll thread + 3s bounded wait for workers)"; FACT: about 8 s at most (A01 C18) (P06-S070). INTERPRETATION (current external context): running and queued checks continue after `stop` returns and can still release and call back; a submission after the pool's shutdown would raise `RuntimeError` outside any `try` and end the daemon poll thread, which the code does not exclude and which is not expected in practice; `cancel_futures` dates from Python 3.9 (the production version is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED) (P06-S071).
- FACT (`clear_all`): under the lock it clears the watchlist and the failure counts; outside the lock and any `try` it calls `clear_all_gate_state()` (`DELETE FROM gate_state`); a store failure raises after memory is cleared; it writes no signal status, so a `GATE_WAITING` signal keeps that status; docstring: "Called by EOD squareoff to prevent stale signal rehydration next morning" (P06-S072). FACT (A12's `orders/eod_squareoff.py`, interface only): `_fire` begins with "Step 0: FIX-046 - Clear gate state", calling `clear_all()` only `if self._entry_gate is not None`, inside a `try` that logs and continues; `_entry_gate` is set only from a constructor parameter (P06-S073).
- FACT (the wiring, `main.py`, A01's file): the only non-test construction of `EodSquareoff` passes no `entry_gate` and runs before the gate exists; nothing assigns `_entry_gate` afterwards outside two test lines; the class has no setter for it; no committed version of `main.py` passed the gate to the end-of-day object (eight searches of the baseline and its history) (P06-S074). FACT and INTERPRETATION: in the `main.py` build `self._entry_gate` is `None`, the end-of-day step 0 skips the gate, and `EntryGate.clear_all()` has no reachable non-test caller; a persisted `gate_state` row, if one existed, is therefore not deleted at end of day by this path and stays until the gate releases it. This differs from the FIX-046 commit message, from `clear_all`'s docstring, from the legacy audit entry IA-P2-01 ("cleared at EOD on every boot"), and from two approved project records: A03 AUDIT H-50 ("the EOD clear empties it", L1037) and A03 AUDIT Q-11 ("FACT: the EOD `clear_all` calls delete all such rows", L2045). The difference is reported, not resolved; the A03 records are not altered (P06-S075; section 17; A07-SP points in section 18.1).
- FACT (A19, stated expectations only): the end-of-day test wires a mock gate by assigning `eod._entry_gate` and expects `clear_all` to be called; no test constructs the `main.py` wiring (P06-S076). FACT and INTERPRETATION: A12's `scripts/eod_cleanup.py` deletes `gate_state` rows only as children of pruned signals (`EXPIRED`, `DUPLICATE`, `REJECTED*`), so it does not remove a waiting entry's row; whether it ran is A12's and A16's (P06-S077). FACT and INTERPRETATION: at the FIX-046 commit, as at the baseline, the time-out test preceded the quote read and the rehydration restored the stored `added_at`, so a row from the previous day with a time-out shorter than the overnight gap is released `TIMEOUT` at the first morning check without a quote read and cannot "fire"; the problem the FIX-046 message states would need a row whose time-out exceeds that gap; the message's source is not given (P06-S129).

**Threading and locking.**

- FACT: the threads that touch the gate are the main thread (construction, `start`, `stop`), the daemon `eg-poll` thread, two `eg-worker` threads, the end-of-day thread that would call `clear_all` (not wired), and, after a `PRICE_HIT`, the `gate-release` pool threads, which run `continue_from_gate` and do not call back into the gate (P06-S078). The one lock guards the watchlist and the failure counts; every store call, quote read, log call and the callback run outside it; no path acquires it twice (P06-S079). HISTORICAL and INTERPRETATION: EG10 and EG8 match the code, except that the snapshot is taken on the poll thread, not the main thread (P06-S080).
- INTERPRETATION (races): the membership test in `_check_one` and the removal in `_release` are separate lock sections, so another check, `remove` or `clear_all` can intervene; the release happens at most once per watchlist insertion, with the first caller's reason; two concurrent checks can each add to the failure count, so `QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` can come in fewer than three cycles (P06-S081). The in-memory watchlist and the `gate_state` table are never changed atomically together; memory is "the source of truth for the running session" (P06-S082). A03's store opens one connection per thread, so the gate's writes run on the `eg-worker` and main-thread connections (P06-S083).

**The callback's wiring in `main.py` (A01's file, interface only).**

- FACT: a module-level `_gate_release_pool` (two workers, `gate-release`) is created when `main.py` is imported and never shut down (A01 C19) (P06-S084). `_make_gate_release_cb(signal_processor)` returns `_on_gate_release(entry, reason)`: for `PRICE_HIT` it reads `release_ltp` from `extras` and submits `_gate_release_worker(entry, release_ltp)` to the pool ("FIX-169 F25: run on dedicated pool, not gate worker thread"); for any other reason it logs "no action"; the worker calls `signal_processor.continue_from_gate(entry, release_ltp=release_ltp)` inside a `try` that only logs an error (P06-S085). FACT: the callback returns once the work is submitted, keeps no future and never observes the result; the gate's handler catches only exceptions of the callback itself (P06-S086). FACT: the callback matches MAIN18 (P06-S087).
- FACT (start and stop order, interface): `signal_processor.start()` precedes `entry_gate.start()`; at shutdown `_shutdown` sets the shutdown event first, drains the flatten worker, stops the webhook receiver, stops the processor, then other workers, then `entry_gate.stop()` in its own `try`; A01 C20 records the processor-before-gate stop as a documentation mismatch only, because `continue_from_gate` refuses placement once the processor's stop event is set (L2215 to L2222) (P06-S088). INTERPRETATION: between the shutdown event (normally already set by the signal handler or the end-of-day exit loop) and `entry_gate.stop()` the gate keeps polling; a quote read that must wait for a limiter token then counts as a failure, so an entry can be released `QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` during a long stop, its row deleted and not rehydrated; a `PRICE_HIT` released in that window, or by a check still running after `stop`, goes to the never-stopped pool and is refused at L2215 only if it reaches that point (A06 EV-11) (P06-S089). FACT and INTERPRETATION (A01 B-28's gate part; current external context): a `continue_from_gate` task queued on the pool before exit runs to its end after `_shutdown` and after the instance lock is released (P06-S090).

**`continue_from_gate` at the interface (A06's function; D-39 P2; not its internal orchestration).**

- FACT: `continue_from_gate(self, entry, release_ltp=None) -> None` (`signals/signal_processor.py` L2010); its only non-test caller is `_gate_release_worker`, reached only through the gate's `PRICE_HIT` callback; tests call it as stated expectations only (P06-S091). HISTORICAL: its docstring says the entry "already carries derived prices ... so Steps 1-4 of _process_one (screening + price derivation) are bypassed", that the kill switch and window are re-checked, that "in_flight tracking is NOT released here -- the EntryGate already manages it", and that `release_ltp` is passed to the placer for slippage protection (FIX-025) (P06-S092).
- FACT (inputs): it reads `signal_id`, `symbol`, `strategy_name`, `entry_price`, `sl_price`, `tgt_price`, `direction`, `tier` and `trigger_price` from the entry, and not `tolerance_pct`, `timeout_sec`, `scanner_name`, `intent`, `added_at` or `extras`; the intent used downstream is the loaded strategy's (P06-S093). FACT and INTERPRETATION: `side = "BUY" if direction in ("LONG", "BUY") else "SELL"`, so any other direction value from a rehydrated row is treated as `"SELL"`; HISTORICAL: the comment says the direction "is always "LONG"/"SHORT"", which neither the dataclass nor the rehydration enforces (P06-S094).
- FACT (the decisions it re-makes, recorded at the call level only; the order is A06's): `PROCESSING`; the kill switch; the global entry window; the shadow guard; the strategy lookup (`UNKNOWN_STRATEGY`); strategy control (L2062); the per-strategy window; the governor (`STRATEGY_CIRCUIT_BREAKER`); then the A09 steps; it makes no screening call, no `StepExecutor` call and no age check (A06 PQ-13, a MATCH), and re-derives no price (P06-S095). FACT (strategy control at L2062; logic in section 3): the same call and label mapping as at L959, the only difference being the source of `strategy_name` (the entry's) (P06-S096; P02-S036, P02-S038, P02-S040).
- FACT (handoff to A09 and A10, interface only): sizing, the per-strategy cap and the symbol-direction rule under the portfolio lock, risk approval and the reservation, then `RESERVED`; without a placer, release and `PROCESSED_NO_PLACER`; a late kill-switch check and the stop-event check (`SHUTDOWN`); the entry throttle (`ENTRY_THROTTLED`); then the placer's `place(...)` with the entry's prices, `release_ltp` and `signal_trigger_price`; the decisions on quantity, risk, capital and placement are A09's and A10's, and A07's gate hands over only the entry fields and `release_ltp` (P06-S097). FACT (statuses it writes): `PROCESSING`, `RESERVED`, `PROCESSED_NO_PLACER`, `TIMEOUT` (an ambiguous placement time-out), `PROCESSED`, `REJECTED_<check>` and `PLACEMENT_FAILED`; it returns `None` on every route (P06-S098).
- FACT and INTERPRETATION (failure contract): its two handlers write a status and release a held reservation; an exception inside them escapes to the pool worker, which only logs it, so no further status is written and the gate cannot learn of it (P06-S099). FACT: it reads no current status before writing `PROCESSING`, so nothing guards against a second resumption or against resuming a signal another route has ended, and the rehydration reads no status either (A06 EV-11) (P06-S100). HISTORICAL and FACT: its docstring's "the EntryGate already manages" in-flight tracking is not met, because the gate holds no in-flight or claim state; how the receiver's claim is released for a gated signal is A06's (A06 ET-13) (P06-S101). INTERPRETATION: a resumption that starts from a rehydrated row increments neither the processor's dispatch counter nor the gate's counter, so the manifest's dormancy tripwire would not show it (P06-S102).

**Statuses, rows and readers on the gate path.**

- FACT: the gate writes `GATE_WAITING` (from `add`, no non-test caller), `GATE_RELEASED_PRICE_HIT`, `GATE_RELEASED_TIMEOUT` and `GATE_RELEASED_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE`, and, unreachable from its own calls, `GATE_RELEASED_<reason>`; every gate status write sets `rejection_reason` to NULL; nothing is written on a cycle that does not release, on rehydration, on `remove` or on `clear_all` (P06-S103). FACT (the row's lifecycle): written by `add`; read at `start` and by A15's daily report; deleted by `_release`, by `clear_all` (not wired) and as a child of a pruned signal by A12's cleanup script; the store's `delete_gate_state` has no non-test caller (A03 Q-12) (P06-S104).
- INTERPRETATION (the status sequence of a gated signal; only the rehydration route is reachable): rehydration writes nothing; a release writes one `GATE_RELEASED_*`; for `PRICE_HIT` the resumption writes `PROCESSING` and then A06's later statuses; between the two, `GATE_RELEASED_PRICE_HIT` stands while the work waits in the two-worker pool (P06-S105). FACT (readers outside A07, interface only): `reports/signal_status.py` classifies any `GATE_` status as "other"; the dashboard renders any `GATE_` status as a "Signal Rejected" event of severity "Error" (INTERPRETATION: `GATE_WAITING` and `GATE_RELEASED_PRICE_HIT` would be shown as rejections); the crash-test validators list `REJECTED_ENTRY_GATE`, which no non-test code writes (P06-S106; section 9).

**Failure behaviour on the gate path (summary).**

- FACT (P06-S107):

| Condition | What the gate does |
|---|---|
| `gate_state` read fails at start | ERROR log; nothing restored; start continues |
| A row fails to parse | WARNING; skipped in memory, left in the table, skipped again at each start |
| A row is old, has an odd direction, non-finite or zero prices, or a tolerance out of the schema range | restored as it is; no test |
| Time-out passed | `TIMEOUT` release at the first check, before any quote read |
| Quote read raises (broker error, limiter time-out or abort, missing symbol) | count + 1; WARNING; at three consecutive, ERROR and `QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` |
| Quote price is 0.0 | treated as a price; resets the count; no hit unless the window contains 0 |
| Check raises outside its `try` | discarded by the poll loop, no log |
| A check takes longer than 7 s | wait abandoned silently; check continues; a second check may start |
| `release_gate_state` fails | ERROR log; row and old status stay; entry gone from memory; callback still runs |
| `_release` raises after the store call (non-dict `extras` on `PRICE_HIT`) | no callback; signal left at `GATE_RELEASED_PRICE_HIT`; no log |
| Callback raises | ERROR log; gate continues |
| `continue_from_gate` raises out of its handlers | ERROR log by the pool worker only |
| `add` persistence fails | ERROR log; memory insert kept (no non-test caller) |
| `clear_all` store call fails | memory already cleared; exception to the caller, which is not wired in the `main.py` build |
| `stop` with work in flight | 5 s join, 3 s sleep; running and queued checks continue |

- INTERPRETATION: no failure on the gate path is retried by the gate; failures are logged (or, in two routes, silently discarded) and become a later poll, a release or no action; the gate raises no alert (it imports no notifier) (P06-S108).

**Answers on the gate (Q12, Q10, Q11).**

- FACT (Q12): `step_executor.py` and `continue_from_gate` do not interact: `continue_from_gate` makes no screening or executor call, and the executor is reached only inside `screen()`, called only at L998. The resume path is: a `gate_state` row → `EntryGate.start()` (`main.py` L3975) → the rehydration → the poll loop → `_check_one` → `_release(entry, "PRICE_HIT", release_ltp=ltp)` → `release_gate_state` → `_on_gate_release` → `_gate_release_pool.submit` → `_gate_release_worker` → `signal_processor.continue_from_gate(entry, release_ltp=...)` (processor L2010) → A09's sizing, risk and reservation and A10's placement; `TIMEOUT` and `QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` end at the callback with no action; the only non-test caller of `continue_from_gate` is `_gate_release_worker`, and the only non-test entry into the watchlist is the rehydration; reachability is as the fixed wording states (P06-S126).
- FACT (Q10, for the gate): the gate has no reject, accept or skip outcome of its own; its outcomes are pending (nothing written), advance by `PRICE_HIT` (`GATE_RELEASED_PRICE_HIT` and the row deleted in one transaction, then the callback; `continue_from_gate` writes `PROCESSING` and later statuses), end by `TIMEOUT` or `QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` (their status and the row deleted; no further action), entry by `add` (`GATE_WAITING` and a row, best effort; no non-test caller), resume after a restart by rehydration (no write), removal by `remove` (memory only; no caller) and clearing by `clear_all` (memory and all rows; not reached in the `main.py` build); every gate status write sets the reason to NULL and stores no time (A03 P-02) (P06-S127).
- FACT and INTERPRETATION (Q11, for the gate): (a) bypass: a pullback strategy's signal bypasses the gate entirely on the direct path, because nothing routes it there; on the resume path the screening and age checks are bypassed by design (A06 PQ-13), while strategy control, both windows, the kill switch and the governor are re-checked; (b) a later step without an earlier one: a rehydrated entry goes to sizing with prices derived when its row was written, and its origin cannot be checked because the gate reads no status; (c) twice: two checks can run at once but the release happens once per insertion; a failed release followed by a restart can resume the same signal twice, with no guard on either side (A03 Q-11, A06 EV-11); (d) lost: a `TIMEOUT` or `QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` release ends with no further action; a `PRICE_HIT` whose `_release` raises after the store call is left at `GATE_RELEASED_PRICE_HIT` with no resumption and no log; a refused resumption is not returned to the gate (A06 ET-13); and the statuses of P06-S105 can be ambiguous to readers (P06-S128).

**Inherited items on this section's side** (section 16 has every row): A01 C14 (P06-S109), C15 (P06-S110), C20 (P06-S111) and B-28 (P06-S112); A02 T-14 (P06-S113: the gate names the `pullback_wait_*` fields only in comments); A03 H-50 (P06-S114: agrees that `add()` has no live writer; differs on "the EOD clear empties it" and on rows "written before the path went dormant"), P-02 (P06-S115), P-11 (P06-S116), Q-11 (P06-S117: agrees on the failed-release route; differs on "the EOD `clear_all` calls delete all such rows"), Q-12 (P06-S118) and Q-45 (P06-S119: the listed end-of-day caller is not wired to the gate in the `main.py` build); A04 RD-12 (P06-S120: agrees under two conditions; RD-12's cross-reference line L2779 and its handoff row L2895 differ on reachability, reported, not resolved) and RL-11 (P06-S121); A06 WA-07 (P06-S122), PQ-13 (P06-S123), EV-11 (P06-S124) and ET-13 (P06-S125).

## 9. Statuses written, companion rows, commit boundaries and their readers (questions Q7, Q10, Q16)

This section brings together, across all A07 components, the `signals.status` values that A07 code writes or decides, the companion rows and files (`screener_results`, `gate_state`, `strategy_metrics`, the registry state file), the order and commit boundaries of the writes (A03 P-02, P-11 case (5)), the readers of these statuses and rows outside A07 (interface only, by owner), and the mapping between the status vocabularies (Q16). The screener's own routes and writes are in section 4 and the gate's in section 8; this section cites them for the cross-component picture.

Evidence: `A07_STATUSES_PERSISTENCE_CONCURRENCY_AND_DUPLICATION_2026-11-21.md` (P09), which used the checked P01 to P08 files (P09-S002) and read A06's processor and the readers at the interface (P09-S007); with P03 and P06.

**The statuses that A07 code writes or decides.**

- FACT: of the thirteen A07 files only the screener (`_persist`: `update_signal_status`, `insert_screener_result`) and the entry gate (`clear_all_gate_state`, `update_signal_status(..., "GATE_WAITING")`, `insert_gate_state`, `release_gate_state`) write `signals` or its companion tables; the metrics script writes `strategy_metrics` and the officer the registry state file; strategy control, the taxonomy, the executor, the scorer, the HardGate, the direction accessor's other functions and the status table write nothing (P09-S011).
- FACT (the screener): exactly one of `PASSED`, `REJECTED_NOT_MIS_TRADABLE`, `REJECTED_CIRCUIT_PROXIMITY`, `REJECTED_AT_CIRCUIT` (enforce only), `REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE`, `REJECTED_STEP_ERROR`, `REJECTED_SCORE_<n>`, `SKIPPED_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE`, `SKIPPED_EXECUTOR_ERROR` and `SKIPPED_SCORER_ERROR` (OFF and shadow only), with the reason `rejected_step or status`, once per returning `screen()` call; nothing on the MIS shadow fall-through or on an exception out of `screen()` (P09-S012). FACT (the gate): `GATE_WAITING` (from `add`, no non-test caller) and `GATE_RELEASED_PRICE_HIT`, `GATE_RELEASED_TIMEOUT` or `GATE_RELEASED_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE`, each with a NULL reason, written on the `eg-worker` thread after the entry leaves the watchlist and before the callback (P09-S013).
- FACT (decided by A07, written by A06): strategy control's verdict becomes `REJECTED_TRADE_TYPE` or `REJECTED_STRATEGY_CONTROL` with the verdict's text, written by A06's handler on all three processor paths (D-38) (P09-S014). FACT (not written by A07, interface): the processor's `PROCESSING` before strategy control and screening, and every later status of a signal that passed screening (`RESERVED`, `PROCESSED_NO_PLACER`, `TIMEOUT`, `PROCESSED`, `REJECTED_<check>`, `PLACEMENT_FAILED`, the allocator worker's statuses, and the bare `REJECTED` with reason `QUEUE_FULL` from the worker wrapper's re-queue); A08's `RETEST_WAITING` and `REJECTED_RETEST_DUP`; the receiver's `QUEUED`; none of the three A07 scripts writes `signals` (P09-S015).
- FACT: the screener passes `ts`, the gate and the processor's control-reject write pass none, and the store discards `ts` (no time column), so no time of an A07-written or A07-decided status change is stored in `signals`; the screener's time is kept in `screener_results.ts` and the P3 record only (A03 P-02) (P09-S016). Neither store method tests the current status, and the file schema's only trigger is on `trades`, so every A07 status write replaces whatever the row holds and is replaced by the next write (P09-S017). The file schema's `signals.status` CHECK accepts every A07 status (`PASSED`, `REJECTED*`, `SKIPPED_*`, `GATE_*`); whether the production schema matched is A03's (P09-S018).
- INTERPRETATION (the status sequences on A07's routes): rejected or skipped by the screener: `QUEUED` → `PROCESSING` → the screener's status (final for the run); rejected by strategy control: `QUEUED` → `PROCESSING` → `REJECTED_STRATEGY_CONTROL` or `REJECTED_TRADE_TYPE`; passed: `QUEUED` → `PROCESSING` → `PASSED` → A08's `RETEST_WAITING` or `REJECTED_RETEST_DUP`, or a reject before any reservation, or `RESERVED` → `PROCESSED`, `PROCESSED_NO_PLACER`, `TIMEOUT`, `REJECTED_<check>` or `PLACEMENT_FAILED`; gate path (reachable only from a rehydrated row): the held status → `GATE_RELEASED_<reason>` → for `PRICE_HIT`, `PROCESSING` and A06's later statuses; a screener `PASSED` therefore stays in `signals` only until a later write reaches the row (P09-S019).

**Companion rows and files: order and commit boundaries (A03 P-02, P-11 case (5)).**

- FACT (A03's store, interface): every store method the screener and the gate call opens its own transaction (`BEGIN IMMEDIATE`; commit on success, rollback on an exception); connections use `isolation_level=None`, `check_same_thread=True`, a 30 s timeout and `busy_timeout` 30 s; the metrics script's reads use plain statements and its write its own transaction; the officer uses its own connection for three reads; so each A07 store write is one commit and no A07 code groups two store calls into one transaction (P09-S020).
- FACT (the screener, in order): the effect counter; one time stamp; inside one `try`, the status update (commit 1) and the `screener_results` insert (commit 2); on an exception one ERROR line; outside that `try`, the P3 evidence append with `fsync` where a recorder is wired; the row holds the score, tier, status, step results, latencies, snapshot, `ts` and `eligible_score`, with no column for the step statuses, error steps or `rejected_step`, no unique constraint on `signal_id` and a foreign key to `signals` (P09-S021). INTERPRETATION: the status commit precedes the row commit; a failure or stop between them leaves a status without a row, never the reverse; a failed status commit skips the row and leaves `PROCESSING` (A06 EV-02 case (c)); the P3 record is attempted whatever the commits did (P09-S022).
- FACT (the gate): `add` (unreachable) inserts in memory, then writes the row (commit 1) and `GATE_WAITING` (commit 2), the reverse of the screener's order; a failed row commit skips the status commit (P09-S023); the release removes from memory under the lock, then makes one commit that deletes the row and updates `signals` (the only A07 write that changes two tables in one transaction), then logs and calls back; a failed commit rolls back and the callback still runs (P09-S024); the rehydration reads all rows in one transaction (which takes the write lock, A03 Q-13) and writes nothing; `remove` changes memory only; `clear_all` clears memory and then deletes every row in one commit, writing no status, and is not wired (P09-S025).
- FACT (the strategy-control reject, A06's handler): a log line, an in-memory metric under a lock, the status write (one commit), the `P2_REJECT` evidence capture (added after `7d4970a`), a reservation release only if one exists (never here), and the tally; no `screener_results` row is written for a signal rejected before L998 (P09-S026).
- FACT (`strategy_metrics`): one `INSERT OR REPLACE` per processed strategy, each in its own transaction, so a failure part-way leaves the earlier strategies' rows; `UNIQUE(strategy, date)` makes a re-run replace that date's rows (P09-S027). FACT (the registry state file): written only when changed and not in a dry run, through a temporary file and a rename with no lock, before the notification; the officer's three reads are not in one transaction with each other or with the write (P09-S028). FACT (A12's MIS blocklist, interface): A07 reads it and does not write it; A10's placer records a block under the module's lock and persists the whole file by a temporary file and `os.replace`; one blocklist object is shared by the placer and the screener (P09-S029). FACT: A07's in-memory side effects that are not stored are the effect counters (unlocked `self.n += 1`), the processor's screener tallies (under a lock), the executor's rotation count and the gate's quote-failure counts (P09-S030).
- FACT (summary of the write units) (P09-S031):

| Write | Component (owner) | Commit unit | Order | Failure handling | Reachable on the `main.py` wiring |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `signals.status` + reason (screener) | screener (A07) | one commit | first | logged, swallowed; skips the row | every returning `screen()` route |
| `screener_results` row | screener (A07) | one commit | second | logged, swallowed | every route whose status commit succeeded |
| P3 evidence record | screener (A07) calling A15's recorder | file append with fsync | third, outside the DB `try` | logged, `note_failure`, swallowed | where the recorder is wired (after `7d4970a`) |
| `gate_state` row + `GATE_WAITING` | gate `add` (A07) | two commits | memory, row, status | logged, swallowed | no (no non-test caller of `add`) |
| `gate_state` delete + `GATE_RELEASED_*` | gate `_release` (A07) | one commit (two tables) | memory, commit, log, callback | logged, swallowed; callback still runs | only for rehydrated rows |
| all `gate_state` rows deleted | gate `clear_all` (A07) | one commit | memory, commit | raised to the caller | no (caller not wired) |
| `REJECTED_STRATEGY_CONTROL` / `REJECTED_TRADE_TYPE` | processor handler (A06) on A07's verdict | one commit | status, P2 evidence, tally | the handler's own write is not guarded | every processor path |
| `strategy_metrics` rows | metrics script (A07) | one commit per strategy | strategy by strategy | run ends with exit code 1; earlier rows kept | by the cron declaration only (runs UNKNOWN) |
| registry state file | officer (A07) through `save_registry` | temporary file and rename | file, then notification | exception ends the run | by the cron declaration only (runs UNKNOWN) |

- INTERPRETATION: apart from `release_gate_state`, every A07 operation that writes more than one thing writes them as separate commits with no rollback of the earlier one; none is retried; the caller is told of no write failure; none stores the time of a status change; for the gate this is its stated design (A03 AUDIT L1720) and for the screener what SS5 and SS10 state (P09-S032).

**Readers outside A07 of these statuses and rows (interface only, by owner).**

- FACT (searches): the non-test readers of `screener_results` are A03's store method for a date (no order), A15's daily report, trade review, dashboard reader, trade journal and forward-shadow and soak scripts, A19's replay script, A18's parity tool, and the deleters and lists of A03 and A12; the non-test readers of the status strings only A07 writes are A15's evidence classifier, report classifier, dashboard trade log, scanner attribution and CSV generator, the trade review and the replay script; outside `screening/`, `GATE_` statuses are named by the schema, the report classifier, the dashboard trade log and the dashboard's status-bucket SQL, whose `CASE` puts every status not matched earlier, `PASSED`, `GATE_*` and `RETEST_*` among them, in "accepted"; prefix-family readers are not all listed (P09-S033).
- FACT (readers by owner; each reader's own logic is its owner's) (P09-S034):

| Owner | Reader | What it reads from A07's outputs | How it treats a repeated or unusual value |
|---|---|---|---|
| A06 | the processor after L1005 | the returned `ScreeningResult`, not the stored rows (the `SKIPPED_` prefix, `passed`; then tier, score, step data, snapshot) | reads no stored A07 status or row |
| A06 (receiver) | the fingerprint path | `signals.status` of an existing row | re-queues only a `QUEUE_FULL` row; any A07 status gives `DUPLICATE` |
| A08 | the retest diverter | `screen_result.tier` and `.score` | overwrites `PASSED` with `RETEST_WAITING` or `REJECTED_RETEST_DUP` |
| A09 | the position sizer | the tier as `score_tier` | a multiplier per tier (module default HIGH 1.0, MEDIUM 0.7, LOW 0.5; configured values from `main.py`; a missing tier 1.0) |
| A03 | the store's date read; `gate_state` read; retention; drill | all rows of a date (no order); all gate rows; deletion of `screener_results` after 90 days | returns every row, duplicates included |
| A12 | `scripts/eod_cleanup.py`; `orders/eod_squareoff.py` | rows as children of pruned signals; the direction map from the strategy files | a `GATE_WAITING` or `PASSED` signal is not pruned; the map is display only |
| A15 | `core/evidence_contract.py` | the P3 record's status | the enforce path's `REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE` would be labelled a scored step reject |
| A15 | `reports/signal_status.py` | `signals.status` | `PASSED` "qualified", `REJECTED*` "rejected", `SKIPPED_*` "skipped", `GATE_*` "other"; `REJECTED_SCORE_<n>` collapsed |
| A15 | `reports/daily_report.py` | the date's rows; all `gate_state` rows; `eligible_score` | keeps the last row per signal of an unordered query; recomputes a per-strategy minimum when the column is NULL; the job is declared `enabled: false` |
| A15 | `reports/daily_trade_review.py` | the date's rows, twice | keeps the highest score per signal; its signal table's order-dependent rule replaces the kept row by a later `PASSED` row or a later higher score |
| A15 | the dashboard's `db_reader.py` | latencies, score, `eligible_score`, status, `ts`; status families | the latency reader and the trade-log reader keep the newest row; `MAX(score)` and `MAX(eligible_score)` per signal; `REJECTED_TRADE_TYPE` counted with risk rejects |
| A15 | the dashboard's status-bucket SQL | `signals.status` | every `GATE_*` status, including the two non-resuming releases, counted "accepted" |
| A15 | the dashboard's trade log | `signals.status` and the newest screener row | `REJECTED*`, `DROPPED_*`, `SKIPPED_*` and `GATE_*` shown as "Signal Rejected"; a "Signal Accepted" event at the screener row's time when its status does not start with `REJECT` |
| A15 | scanner attribution; the CSV generator; the trade journal | `signals.status`; the stored reason; the score | `REJECTED_SCORE_<n>` collapsed; the stored reason shown as it is; one score per signal, the last row of an unordered query |
| A15 | `scripts/system_manager.py`; `scripts/gemini_premarket_brief.py`; the daily report's taxonomy columns | `strategy_metrics`; the taxonomy map | a win-rate "review" warning; a query naming three columns the table lacks; display only |
| A16 | `scripts/cron_officer.py` | the status table's rows | briefing only |
| A18 | the forward-shadow recorder, the soak report, the parity tool, `v3_chain/` | the score, step results and snapshot | the recorder records both rows of a re-screened signal; the soak report and the parity tool read every row |
| A19 | `scripts/replay_signals.py`; the crash-test validators | the newest row; terminal-status lists | `scr_status != "PASSED"` is a non-pass; the validators list `REJECTED_ENTRY_GATE`, which no code writes, and no `GATE_` status |

- INTERPRETATION (readers and a second screening of one signal): the dashboard takes the highest score and threshold, its trade log, latency reader and the replay script the newest row, the trade review its own order-dependent choices, the forward recorder both rows, and the daily report and the trade journal whichever row their unordered query returns last, while `signals.status` holds the later screening's status; so the readers can present different screening outcomes for one `signal_id` (P09-S035). INTERPRETATION (the gate statuses at the readers): `GATE_RELEASED_PRICE_HIT` and `GATE_WAITING` are shown as "Signal Rejected" by the dashboard's trade log, "other" by the report classifier and "accepted" by the dashboard's funnel, and are absent from the validators; the two non-resuming releases are final statuses that the end-of-day prune does not select (P09-S036). INTERPRETATION (the skips): the dashboard's trade log shows a `SKIPPED_*` signal both as rejected and as accepted; the CSV shows the stored reason, which for a skip is the status itself (P09-S037). FACT: the dashboard names `eligible_score` the "Score Threshold" ("v14: per-strategy min_score threshold") and falls back to the configured `min_pass_score` when the column is absent, while the screener writes the route's effective minimum on five routes and NULL on ten (P09-S038; P03-S141).

**The status vocabularies and how they map onto one another (Q16).**

- FACT (the mapping, layer by layer) (P09-S039):

| Layer | Its own values | Where it goes next | Stored in `signals` as | Stored elsewhere |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| strategy control (`Verdict.cause`) | `OK`, `DISABLED`, `FORCE_BREAKER`, `TRADE_TYPE` | check label `STRATEGY_CONTROL` or `TRADE_TYPE` | `REJECTED_STRATEGY_CONTROL`, `REJECTED_TRADE_TYPE`; reason the verdict text | P2 evidence; tally keys |
| step executor (`step_statuses`) | `PASSED`, `REJECTED`, `ERROR`, `TIMEOUT` | `ScreeningResult.step_statuses` | not stored | P3 and P1 evidence records only |
| step executor (`rejected_at`, `error_steps`) | first 0.0 or ERROR step; ERROR step names | `rejected_at`: no reader; `error_steps`: R5/E4 | `REJECTED_STEP_ERROR`; reason the joined names | — |
| step 10 value | 1.0, 0.5, 0.0 | R8 (`== 0.0`, after the score test) | `REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE`; reason `signal_age` | `screener_results.step_results` |
| quality scorer | total; tier; `passed` (unread) | R7; the result's score and tier | `REJECTED_SCORE_<n>` or `PASSED` | `screener_results.score`, `tier`; A09's multiplier |
| re-scale helpers (enforce) | total; tier | E6 | `REJECTED_SCORE_<n>` or `PASSED` | as above |
| HardGate (`GateVerdict.reason`) | `AT_CIRCUIT`, `CIRCUIT_PROXIMITY`, `SIGNAL_AGE` (`GATE_STALE`) | E1 (enforce); the shadow line | `REJECTED_<reason>`; reason in lower case | evidence in the log only |
| circuit-proximity rule | reason text or `None` | R3 (OFF, shadow) | `REJECTED_CIRCUIT_PROXIMITY`; reason `circuit_proximity` | text in the log only |
| MIS blocklist decision | blocked or not; shadow or active | R1 | `REJECTED_NOT_MIS_TRADABLE` (active); nothing (shadow) | WARNING "... (shadow)" |
| screener (`ScreeningResult.status`) | the statuses above | the processor's prefix and `passed` tests | as written | `screener_results.status` |
| entry gate (release reason) | `PRICE_HIT`, `TIMEOUT`, `QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` | status map; callback | `GATE_RELEASED_*`; reason NULL | — |
| entry gate (`add`) | — | — | `GATE_WAITING` (unreachable) | `gate_state` row |
| playbook gates (A18's callers) | `passed`, `reason`, `evidence` | A18's would-be verdict | not stored by A07 | A18's records |
| status table | `WILL TRADE`, `WON'T TRADE`, `CONFIG ERROR`; `ENABLED`, `DISABLED`, `?` | the cron briefing | not stored | — |
| direction registry | `PENDING`, `CONFIRMED`; `OK`, `DIRECTION_CONFLICT` | notifications; no trading-path reader | not stored | the state file |
| metrics script | "PAPER_ONLY", "STRATEGY DEMOTED" (texts only) | a CRITICAL log line | not stored | `strategy_metrics` (no status column) |
| downstream (A06, A08, A09, A10) | `RESERVED`, `PROCESSED`, `PROCESSED_NO_PLACER`, `TIMEOUT`, `REJECTED_<check>`, `PLACEMENT_FAILED`, `RETEST_WAITING` | replaces the A07 status | as written by the owner | — |

- INTERPRETATION (lossy or merged translations): (a) `DISABLED` and `FORCE_BREAKER` become one status, told apart only by the reason text; (b) the step status `REJECTED` is not a signal reject except for step 10, and `rejected_at` reaches nothing; (c) the step statuses, `TIMEOUT` included, are stored in no table; (d) `REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE` and `REJECTED_CIRCUIT_PROXIMITY` each come from two mechanisms the stored status does not distinguish; (e) `SKIPPED_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` covers an absent quote, a broker or limiter error and a limiter wait aborted at shutdown; (f) unscored outcomes are stored with a fixed 0 and `LOW` in the score columns; (g) `rejection_reason` mixes lower-case names, the status itself, `PASSED`, the verdict text and NULL; (h) `TIMEOUT` names a step that overran, an ambiguous placement and, inside `GATE_RELEASED_TIMEOUT`, a gate wait that ended; (i) `LOW` is given both to unscored rejects and to passes with totals from 60 to 64, so at the committed files a pass reaches A09's sizer as `LOW` unless its total is 65 or more on the OFF and shadow paths (exactly 65 when no step times out, up to 78 with timeouts) or 56 or more on the enforce path; (j) once a gated signal resumes, `PROCESSING` replaces `GATE_RELEASED_PRICE_HIT` and `signals` keeps no history (P09-S040).
- INTERPRETATION (translations at the readers): (a) every `GATE_*` status is "Signal Rejected" in the dashboard's trade log, "other" in the report classifier and "accepted" in the dashboard's funnel; (b) a skip is both rejected and accepted in the trade log; (c) `REJECTED_TRADE_TYPE` counts with risk rejects and `REJECTED_STRATEGY_CONTROL` in neither family; (d) the classifier would mislabel the enforce path's age reject; (e) two readers collapse `REJECTED_SCORE_<n>`; (f) the CSV maps a `REJECTED_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` that no code writes beside the `SKIPPED_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` that the screener writes; (g) the validators list `REJECTED_ENTRY_GATE`, which no code writes, and no `GATE_` status; (h) the readers' handling of a second row differs; (i) the trade review's stage classifier, matching substrings, puts the pre-scoring rejects (MIS, at-circuit, step error) at the scoring stage when the signal has a screener row (P09-S041). INTERPRETATION: `PAPER_ONLY` and "STRATEGY DEMOTED" name no state any component holds; `CONFIRMED` and `DIRECTION_CONFLICT` are read by no trading-path module; a `WILL TRADE` row means only that strategy control allows the strategy (P09-S042). HISTORICAL and FACT: P18, SS3 and the `status` comment describe `REJECTED_<step>`, and only `REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE` names a step; the HardGate's "screen() maps these to REJECTED_<reason>" matches E1; `GATE_STALE` carries `SIGNAL_AGE` (P09-S043).

**Boundary summaries of the closed topics on statuses and rows (ADDENDUM 2).** FACT: A03 RTR L147 names A07's "screener and gate tables, `strategy_metrics`, the direction-registry state file", each recorded above with its readers (P09-S126); A06 RTR L214 names the screener and the gate among the writers of `signals` rows: both update `status` and `rejection_reason` of existing rows only and neither creates a row, and strategy control's rejects are written by A06's handler (P09-S129); A04 RTR L144 and A05 RTR L205 are recorded in sections 10 and 11 (P09-S127, P09-S128).

## 10. Concurrency, retries, duplicate evaluation and duplicated checks (questions Q11, Q23)

This section records the threads that run A07 code and the locks they take, what can interleave, the FIX-069 re-run that screens a signal again, the other ways one signal can be evaluated twice, and the checks and quantities that are computed in more than one place. The step pool's internals are in section 5 and the gate's in section 8.

Evidence: P09 sections 6, 7 and 11.3, with P04 and P06; current external context: the Python Library and Extension FAQ, "What kinds of global value mutation are thread-safe?", read 2026-11-21 (P09-S008).

**The threads that run A07 code (Q23).**

- FACT (P09-S044):

| Thread or pool | Created at (owner) | A07 code it runs |
|---|---|---|
| processor workers, `worker_count` (5 in the committed file) | `signals/signal_processor.py` L311 (A06) | strategy control at L959; `screen()` and everything it calls on the calling thread (MIS lookup, quote fetch, proximity rule, scorer, HardGate, `_persist`, evidence capture) |
| the step pool, one `step-exec` worker (replaced on a timeout) | `screening/step_executor.py` L74 to L76 (A07) | the ten step bodies of every signal being screened |
| the gate's `eg-poll` thread (daemon) and two `eg-worker` threads | `screening/entry_gate.py` L163 to L171 (A07) | `_poll_loop`, `_check_one`, `_release` and the callback |
| the gate-release pool, two `gate-release` threads (never shut down) | `main.py` L865 to L867 (A01) | `continue_from_gate` (A06's), with strategy control at L2062 |
| the main thread | `main.py` (A01) | the boot summary, the configuration auditor's two strategy-control probes, `entry_gate.start()` with the rehydration read, `entry_gate.stop()` |
| A08's retest threads | A08 | `continue_from_retest` with strategy control at L2411 (threading not traced) |
| separate processes | the cron declarations (A16) | the metrics script, the officer, the status table inside the cron officer |
