- FACT (locks inside A07 code): the step executor's lock, taken only to swap or retire the pool and to set the shutdown flag; the entry gate's one lock, guarding the watchlist and the failure counts and never held across a store call, quote read, log call or the callback; no lock in the screener, the scorer, the HardGate, strategy control, the taxonomy or the direction accessor; `save_registry` takes none; a search of the thirteen files for lock primitives matches only the gate and the executor (P09-S045). FACT (locks met in other topics' code, interface only): the processor's `_stats_lock`, `_in_flight_lock` and A09's `portfolio_lock`; A12's blocklist lock; A15's recorder lock with `fsync` on the calling thread; the telemetry handle lock for creation only, with `inc` unlocked; the store's per-thread connections and its `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` waits of up to 30 s; A04's rate limiter on every `get_quote`; and A06's receiver claim, keyed on the symbol only, held until the processor's `finally` and evicted by the sweeper after 60 s because no heartbeat is wired (A06 RTR L68, L116) (P09-S046). FACT: no A07 code takes a lock while holding another (P09-S055).

**What can interleave (Q23).**

- INTERPRETATION (one shared step worker, from the checked P04 statements): the steps of up to `worker_count` concurrent screenings run one at a time on one thread in submission order, so a signal's screening time includes the queue wait; a step that overruns its 5 s wait rotates the pool, and the queued steps of other signals are cancelled and recorded `ERROR` (a step submitted to the old pool just after its shutdown raises `RuntimeError`, also `ERROR`; a thread whose own wait times out first takes the TIMEOUT route), so those signals are written `REJECTED_STEP_ERROR` although their own steps did not fail; the executor thus links the outcomes of concurrent signals; whether any step timed out is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P09-S047; P04-S047, P04-S054).
- INTERPRETATION (no per-signal or per-symbol guard in A07): the screener, the gate and strategy control read no stored status and hold no claim; the only guard that keeps two signals of one symbol apart before screening is A06's receiver claim, evicted after 60 s whether or not the worker still holds it, and not held by a gate resumption (the gate keeps no claim, contrary to `continue_from_gate`'s docstring); two screenings for one symbol can therefore run at once, each writing its own rows; the one-trade-per-symbol-and-direction check inside the portfolio lock is A06's and A09's (P09-S048).
- INTERPRETATION (the store as the meeting point): the processor workers, the gate workers, the release threads, the main thread and other components write one SQLite file; each A07 write waits up to 30 s for the write lock and then raises; the screener turns such a raise into an unwritten status and no row, the gate's release into a kept row and the callback still run, and the rehydration read (which itself takes the write lock) into an empty watchlist; how long writers waited in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P09-S049).
- INTERPRETATION (the unlocked counters; current external context): `EffectCounter.inc` is `self.n += 1`, a read-modify-write of the kind the Python FAQ lists as not atomic; the screener's, HardGate's and scorer's counters are incremented from up to five processor threads, so a count can come out low; the counters decide nothing in A07; whether any count was lost is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P09-S050). INTERPRETATION (the serialised evidence append): every `_persist` that reaches the P3 capture appends and `fsync`s under one process-wide lock on the worker's own thread, so concurrent screenings wait for each other there too; with the shared step worker and the store's write lock this is part of the screening duration on which the order of approvals at A06's throttle depends (A06 ET-06); A07 sets no order among concurrent verdicts by receipt time, score or strategy (P09-S051).
- INTERPRETATION (shared quote capacity): the screener's quote fetch, the processor's FIX-067 re-anchor fetch for non-pullback strategies (A06's and A09's) and the gate's reads (one per watched entry per cycle) all take tokens from the same A04 limiter category, so a shortage delays or fails the screener's fetch (`SKIPPED_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` on a raise) and counts as a gate quote failure (P09-S052). INTERPRETATION (the gate's own interleavings): two checks of one entry at once, one release with the first caller's reason, quote failures counted twice, polling until `entry_gate.stop()` (P09-S053; section 8). INTERPRETATION (the shared MIS blocklist): a block recorded by A10's placer during the session takes effect at the screener's next lookup for that symbol; with the committed `shadow: true` the effect is a WARNING only; with `shadow: false` and the filter enabled, later MIS signals for that symbol are written `REJECTED_NOT_MIS_TRADABLE` while the block is younger than `ttl_days` (P09-S054).
- INTERPRETATION (scheduled scripts against the trading process): no scheduled A07 script and the trading path update the same row; the metrics script and the officer read in separate auto-committed statements, so one run can see different database states; the registry write has no lock; the status table can differ from the trading process's boot-time state after a file edit; none of these is established as having occurred (P09-S056; section 12).

**The FIX-069 re-run: a signal screened again (ADDENDUM 2; A06 RTR L130, L162, L347).**

- FACT: when A10's `place` raises `BrokerRateLimitError` with `retry_count < 3`, `_admit_and_place` increments the count and marks the signal re-queued (so the `finally` keeps the receiver's claim), builds a dict with the original `signal_id`, scanner, symbol, trigger price, `triggered_at` and the new count, puts it at once on the processor's own queue, releases the reservation and returns without writing a status (the status reached is `RESERVED`); if the queue is full it clears the mark and re-raises, and the outer handler writes `PLACEMENT_FAILED`; the dispatcher submits the dict again and `_process_one` runs from the start: `PROCESSING`, every intake check with the age measured against the original `triggered_at`, strategy control at L959 and `screen()` at L998; with `retry_count >= 3` the exception is re-raised and written `PLACEMENT_FAILED`; one signal can therefore be screened up to four times (P09-S088; P09-S125 confirms A06 RTR L130, L162 and L347 in the code).
- INTERPRETATION (what the re-run does to A07's records): each re-run writes the screener's status again over `PROCESSING` and appends another `screener_results` row and P3 record, with a new quote and a later age; at the committed files a re-run that reaches step 10 more than 60 s after the trigger cannot reach the minimum score when no step times out and ends `REJECTED_SCORE_<n>`, so a signal that was `PASSED` and `RESERVED` on its first run can end rejected by the screener on its retry, its reservation already released; a re-run that passes meets A06's throttle again, which at the file values admits it only 300 s after its own first admit, and A06 RTR L347 records that such a retry "ends `REJECTED_ENTRY_THROTTLED` unless it spends most of the cooldown before reaching the throttle"; the readers then see two or more rows (section 9); whether any retry occurred is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P09-S089). HISTORICAL and INTERPRETATION: the processor's comment "Processor does NOT double-write those statuses" concerns its own writes and still holds across a re-run, but the re-run makes the screener write its status a second time, after `PROCESSING` has replaced the first run's `RESERVED` (P09-S100).

**Other ways one signal is evaluated twice, accepted after a rejection or lost (Q11 across components).**

- INTERPRETATION ("evaluated twice"): by a FIX-069 re-run, up to four times; by a second resumption of a gated signal after a failed release and a restart; within one process, two checks of one gate entry at once with one release; and, on the shadow path, the proximity rule twice for one screening; two signals of one symbol screened at once when A06's claim has been evicted or is not held are distinct signals, not one signal twice (P09-S104).
- INTERPRETATION ("accepted after a prior rejection"): a re-run's status replaces the earlier one in either direction; a gate release overwrites any current status with `GATE_RELEASED_*`, and `continue_from_gate` then writes `PROCESSING` without reading the status; no A07 writer has a from-status condition; the readers resolve repeated rows differently (P09-S106). INTERPRETATION ("be lost"): a swallowed status or row write; an exception out of `screen()` (recorded `PLACEMENT_FAILED`); the MIS shadow fall-through (a log line only); a `PRICE_HIT` whose `_release` raises after its commit; a throttle refusal of a resumption; a malformed `gate_state` row never restored or removed; a queued step cancelled by another signal's rotation; none is retried or alerted by A07 (P09-S105).

**The same check or quantity in more than one place.**

- FACT (signal age; six places, two owners): (1) A06's receiver refuses an item older than `signal_queue.expiry_sec` (committed 600) as `EXPIRED`; (2) A06's processor rejects `REJECTED_EXPIRED` above its expiry (constructor default 60; `main.py` passes the 600); (3) step 10 scores 1.0 up to 30 s, 0.5 up to 60 s, 0.0 above (code constants); (4) the screener's R8 rejects `REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE` on a step-10 0.0, only after the score test; (5) the HardGate rejects (enforce) or logs (shadow) above `v3_freshness_max_sec` (60.0); (6) A18's parity script reconstructs the gate's verdict from a stored step-10 0.0; the gate's resume path has no age check (A06 PQ-13, a MATCH), its own time test being the entry's time-out; P9a's 90 s is in none of them (P09-S057). INTERPRETATION: A06's checks admit up to 600 s; on the OFF and shadow paths a signal above 60 s at step 10 cannot reach the minimum and is written `REJECTED_SCORE_<n>`, so R8 is not reached on the live route when no step times out; with steps 1 and 3 timed out and step 10 at 0.0 the sum can reach 67.5 (a total of 68), which passes the score test, and R8 then writes `REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE`; a timed-out step 10 records 0.5 whatever the age; the shadow gate reads the clock later than step 10; each check reads its own clock, and none has a lower bound (P09-S058).
- FACT (circuit): one `circuit_state` derived by the screener is scored by step 9 and tested by the HardGate's at-circuit test (reject on enforce, log on shadow); the proximity rule is called by R3 and by the HardGate with two switches from one key, so on the shadow path it is evaluated twice for a signal that reaches the comparison; its 2 % margin is A09's `DEFAULT_CIRCUIT_MARGIN_PCT`, also the default of A09's band clamp used at placement by A10's limit protocol; the screener imports the constant and does not use it (P09-S059). INTERPRETATION: on the OFF and shadow paths a quote at a limit lowers the score by step 9's weight and is not rejected for that reason, while the proximity rule rejects a trigger price within 2 % of a limit; on the enforce path both are pre-scoring rejects; the same margin is applied before the fill by A07's rule (on the trigger price) and after the fill by A10's placeability gate (on the exit legs); how the two compare is A09's and A10's (P09-S060).
- FACT (MIS; three deciders of "intraday product"): A04's product resolver maps `intent` to a product through `product_map`; the screener's pre-drop compares that product with `"MIS"` and consults A12's learned blocklist; strategy control's LAYER 0 makes a DELIVERY strategy dormant under the breaker; HISTORICAL: the control docstring places "MIS-only for what does trade" at the broker chokepoint (A04's) (P09-S061). FACT (the delivery predicate): `strategy_will_trade` is evaluated at seven call sites in four files with different flag sources and defaults, and none reads `delivery_enabled`, which A04's adapter lock and the auditor's A5 test separately (A02 K-05); P08-S059 records the differing defaults of `force_intraday_only` and `trade_type` across the readers (P09-S062).
- FACT (scoring thresholds): `min_pass_score` is read by the scorer for an unread `passed` and passed back for the screener's effective minimum; the per-strategy override is applied on the OFF and shadow paths and re-scaled on enforce; the daily report recomputes the same rule from the files when `eligible_score` is NULL; the dashboard falls back to the configured value; same-named `v3_chain` thresholds are read by no code (A02 K-27); A18's parity script copies the weights; `rescaled_total` repeats the scorer's arithmetic (P09-S063). FACT (the quote): for a strategy with `pullback_wait_enabled` false the processor fetches a second quote after `PASSED` and re-derives the entry and stop from it (A06's and A09's), so the placement basis comes from a later quote than the screening decision used; the quote's `average_price` is a second VWAP beside A05's `session_vwap` (A05 RTR L120) (P09-S064). FACT (other duplicated quantities): the market-open time (09:15 in the file, the schema and the executor's fallback; special sessions not applied by the executor); the dormant step fallbacks against the schema defaults; `mis_filter.ttl_days` 1 against the blocklist constructor default 5 (A12's); two `max_spread_pct` keys (the strategy field read by step 8 and `entry_gate.max_spread_pct` 0.5, not read by that name by A07 code); the 60 s freshness limit in step 10, the HardGate default and the scoring file (P09-S065). FACT: strategy control is made once per processing on each path, while the global window is tested twice on each path (A01's logic) (P09-S066).
- INTERPRETATION (summary): the age, circuit, MIS and delivery decisions are each taken in more than one component, with different thresholds, clocks, inputs or defaults, and no component records which of them decided a signal beyond its own status string; at the committed files the copies agree in value except where P08-S059 records a difference that cannot act and except the signal-age geometry, where the score test, not the age check, records the reject when no step times out (P09-S067).

**Inherited items on this section's side** (section 16 has every row): A06 ET-06 and A06-02 (P09-S123: A07 sets no order among concurrent verdicts or releases; the screening duration includes the shared step worker, a possible timeout and rotation, the limiter wait, the store's write-lock wait and the serialised evidence append; ET-06 stays UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED and no ranking rule is inferred; P04-S138); the FIX-069 handoff point of A06 RTR L130, L162 and L347 (P09-S125); A04 RTR L144 (P09-S127: A07's share is strategy control's use of `force_intraday_only` and `intent`; the throttle and the unwired FIX-007 pre-check are A06's code; A07 bears on the throttle only through the screening duration).

## 11. End-to-end paths (Q22, Q23) and the handoff to A09 (Q15); technical inputs (Q14)

This section maps, step by step across the parts, each representative path that Q22 lists ("Trace representative paths end to end: accepted; rejected at each distinct gate; advanced/resumed; strategy disabled/PAPER_ONLY; missing or invalid context; exception/database failure; and any scheduled status change affecting later signals"), gives Q23's attributes for every path, and records exactly what A07 hands to A09 and what it does not decide, and the technical-analysis and S&R information A07 consumes. Each step names its owner; A07's steps cite the part statements; other owners' steps are interface only. The mode on every OFF/shadow step is the committed `v3_hardgate_mode: "shadow"`; the effective production mode is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. Whether any path ran in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P09-S099).

Evidence: P09 sections 8, 9, 11 and 12, built on the checked P02 to P08 files.

**Accepted (the direct path).**

- FACT (the steps, in order) (P09-S081): (1) A06: the receiver creates the `signals` row at `QUEUED` and queues the 5-tuple; the dispatcher submits the worker task. (2) A06: `PROCESSING` (L897); kill switch, global window, age, shadow guard, strategy lookup. (3) A07 logic at A06's call site: `strategy_will_trade` returns `will_trade` true (`OK`); nothing is kept. (4) A01 and A09: the per-strategy window and the governor. (5) A07: `screen()`: R1 (shadow: a WARNING only if blocked), R2, the snapshot, R3 (`None`), the thresholds, R4 `run_all` of ten steps on the shared step worker, R5 no error step, R6 the scorer, the shadow comparison line, R7 total at or above the minimum, R8 step 10 not 0.0, R9 `PASSED`. (6) A07: `_persist`: `PASSED` with the reason `PASSED` (commit 1), one `screener_results` row with `eligible_score` (commit 2), the P3 record, one INFO line. (7) A06: the stats and the heartbeat. (8) A09, A08, A18, A06, A10 (interface): `_derive_prices`; A08's retest diverter if enabled (which may write `RETEST_WAITING`); the FIX-067 re-anchor for non-pullback strategies; sizing with the tier; the V3 observer and the allocator; `_admit_and_place` (caps, risk, reserve, `RESERVED`, target, alert, late checks, throttle, evidence, `place`, `PROCESSED`). (9) A06: the `finally` releases the receiver's claim unless the signal was re-queued or handed off.
- INTERPRETATION: the effective A07 decision is "proceed", recorded once as `PASSED` (replaced in the same run by A06's `RESERVED` and later statuses or by A08's `RETEST_WAITING`) and once in `screener_results`; the tier and score travel on in memory; with the allocator in enforce mode the processor returns with the status still `PASSED` until A18's worker admits or rejects (P09-S082).

**Rejected at each distinct gate.**

- FACT (each A07 reject; the claim is released in A06's `finally` in every row) (P09-S083):

| Gate (path) | Where | `signals.status` / reason | `screener_results` row | Processor after the reject | Logs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| strategy control: switch off or breaker (all paths) | `strategies/control.py` L81, L90; processor L959 to L972 | `REJECTED_STRATEGY_CONTROL` / verdict text (A06's handler) | none | P2 evidence; tally; no screening | A06's INFO |
| strategy control: trade type | L100 to L113 | `REJECTED_TRADE_TYPE` / verdict text | none | as above | as above |
| MIS pre-drop, active (all modes) | screener L148 to L164 | `REJECTED_NOT_MIS_TRADABLE` / `mis_tradable` | yes, score 0, `LOW`, snapshot `{}` | INFO, tally | screener WARNING |
| quote unavailable or fetch error | L167 to L196 | `SKIPPED_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` / the status | yes, snapshot `{}` | WARNING | INFO or ERROR with traceback |
| circuit proximity (OFF, shadow) | L208 to L232 | `REJECTED_CIRCUIT_PROXIMITY` / `circuit_proximity` | yes, score 0, `LOW` | INFO | WARNING with the reason |
| executor raised | L251 to L263 (E3) | `SKIPPED_EXECUTOR_ERROR` / the status | yes | WARNING | ERROR with traceback |
| step error (a step raised, or was cancelled by another signal's rotation) | L265 to L286 (E4) | `REJECTED_STEP_ERROR` / joined names | yes, score 0, `LOW`, step data | INFO | WARNING (OFF, shadow); executor ERROR per step |
| scorer raised (OFF, shadow) | L288 to L300 | `SKIPPED_SCORER_ERROR` / the status | yes | WARNING | ERROR with traceback |
| minimum score | L316 to L337 (E6) | `REJECTED_SCORE_<n>` / the status | yes, computed score and tier, `eligible_score` | INFO | none in the screener |
| signal age, step 10 = 0.0 (OFF, shadow; not reached on the live route at the committed files when no step times out) | L339 to L354 | `REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE` / `signal_age` | yes, computed score | INFO | none in the screener |
| HardGate (enforce only) | L444 to L459 | `REJECTED_AT_CIRCUIT`, `REJECTED_CIRCUIT_PROXIMITY` or `REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE` / lower-case reason | yes, score 0, `LOW`, no step data | INFO | WARNING with the evidence |

- INTERPRETATION: a screener reject is final for the run, because the processor writes nothing on these branches; the companion row and the P3 record carry the detail the status does not; the rejects after `PASSED` are A06's and A09's (P09-S084). FACT and INTERPRETATION (the remaining A07 gates): the five playbook gates reject on no live A07 path (their only callers are A18's, whose failures become A18's would-be verdicts or leave the PB-01 watchlist row waiting, and write no `signals` status); the gate's two non-resuming releases end a gated signal at `GATE_RELEASED_TIMEOUT` or `GATE_RELEASED_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` with no further write, reachable only for rehydrated rows; the MIS pre-drop in shadow mode rejects nothing; whether any of these rejected a signal in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P09-S137).

**Advanced or resumed.**

- FACT: no A07 component advances or defers a signal on the direct path; the parking after `PASSED` is A08's retest diverter and the hand-off to the allocator worker A18's (P09-S085).
- FACT and UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (the entry-gate resumption, in the fixed wording): a `gate_state` row → `EntryGate.start()` → the rehydration (no status read, no write) → the poll loop → `_check_one` (the time-out first; then the quote read and the window) → `_release(..., "PRICE_HIT", release_ltp=ltp)` (memory removal; one commit writing `GATE_RELEASED_PRICE_HIT` and deleting the row; log; callback) → `_on_gate_release` submits to the two-worker pool → A06's `continue_from_gate`: `PROCESSING`, kill switch, window, shadow guard, lookup, strategy control (L2062; A07 logic), per-strategy window, governor, then A09's sizing with the entry's prices and tier, reservation, throttle and A10's placement; `EntryGate.add()` has no non-test caller at the baseline, so the normal live path that creates a new gate entry is not established; `EntryGate.start()` rehydrates any persisted `gate_state` rows and can poll and release them; whether such rows existed in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, and so is whether any step of this path ran (P09-S086; section 8).
- FACT (the retest resumption, interface only): A08's monitor reaches `continue_from_retest`, which writes `PROCESSING` and calls strategy control at L2411 and makes no screening call; its reachability is A08's (P09-S087). FACT (the FIX-069 re-run): one signal can be screened up to four times (P09-S088, P09-S089; section 10).

**Strategy disabled, or "PAPER_ONLY".**

- FACT (disabled): a strategy with `enabled: false` gets `DISABLED` on every processor path and its signal is written `REJECTED_STRATEGY_CONTROL` before screening; at the committed files the only such strategy is `pb01_breakout_retest`, whose only scanner is routed to the end-of-day path before the queue, so its webhooks do not reach this step; the switch is read at boot only (P09-S090). FACT and INTERPRETATION (PAPER_ONLY): the metrics job (declared 16:15 Mon-Fri) writes `strategy_metrics` rows and, when the three latest stored Sharpe values are below -0.3, logs one CRITICAL line that under the declared crontab reaches the job's log file without the strategy name, sends no notification on the `main` route and writes no state; strategy control has no input for such a state; the path ends at the log line and the strategy's later signals are processed exactly as before (P09-S091; section 12).

**Missing or invalid context.**

- FACT and INTERPRETATION (P09-S092):

| Case | A07 handling | Status written | Consequence downstream |
|---|---|---|---|
| no strategy object | not reached: A06 rejects `UNKNOWN_STRATEGY` first | `REJECTED_UNKNOWN_STRATEGY` (A06's) | — |
| a strategy attribute missing on a non-validated object | not caught in the screener | none by A07 | A06 writes `PLACEMENT_FAILED` |
| no quote entry, or the fetch raised | skip | `SKIPPED_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` | final |
| quote fields absent (`average_price`, OHLC, limits) | `None` in the dictionary; step defaults | through the score | decided at the score test with the missing-input step values |
| last price 0.0 with a positive lower limit | used as a price; `lower_circuit` | through the score (OFF, shadow); `REJECTED_AT_CIRCUIT` (enforce) | — |
| NaN or infinite trigger price | the proximity rule admits a NaN and refuses a positive infinity on its upper-limit leg; no step reads it | as the other inputs decide | the price reaches A09's `_derive_prices` and A10's slippage guard |
| `triggered_at` later than receipt | negative age: step 10 1.0; the HardGate passes | as the other inputs decide | A06's age check also passes |
| quote of unknown age | not tested | — | — |
| rehydrated row with malformed fields | skipped, left in the table | none | skipped again at every start |
| rehydrated row with an old `added_at`, odd direction or non-finite prices | restored; time-out first | `GATE_RELEASED_TIMEOUT` for an expired row | an odd direction becomes `SELL` in `continue_from_gate` (A06's) |
| registry state file unreadable | seed fallback; nothing logged | none in `signals` | registry rows reset and re-announced |

**Exception or database failure.**

- FACT and INTERPRETATION (P09-S093):

| Case | What is stored | What the caller learns |
|---|---|---|
| the screener's status commit fails | `PROCESSING` stays; no row | nothing; after a reject the run ends `PROCESSING`; after `PASSED` A06's writes replace it |
| the row commit fails (including a foreign-key failure) | status written, no row | nothing |
| the store waits more than 30 s for the write lock | as the two rows above | nothing |
| an exception leaves `screen()` | `PLACEMENT_FAILED` by A06's handler; no row, no P3 record | A06's ERROR line |
| strategy control raises (not expected with validated inputs) | `PLACEMENT_FAILED` | ERROR line |
| the gate's `release_gate_state` fails | row and old status stay; entry gone from memory; callback runs | a second resumption after a restart |
| the gate's rehydration read fails | nothing restored | ERROR line |
| `_release` raises after the commit (non-dict `extras`) | `GATE_RELEASED_PRICE_HIT`, no row, no resumption | no log |
| `continue_from_gate` raises out of its handlers | whatever was last written | the pool worker's ERROR line |
| the metrics script fails part-way | earlier strategies' rows committed | `FAILED` heartbeat, exit code 1 |
| the officer's queries fail | empty inputs; conflicts cleared and written | nothing logged |

- INTERPRETATION (the common pattern): no A07 failure is retried by A07, none raises an alert from A07 code, and none is reported to the caller in the returned value; the visible traces are log lines, a missing or unchanged row, or A06's `PLACEMENT_FAILED`, which names a placement that was not attempted (P09-S094).

**Scheduled or time-driven status changes that affect later signals.**

- FACT: the metrics script writes `strategy_metrics`, the officer the registry state file and notifications, the status table nothing; none of these is read by strategy control, the screener, the gate or the processor, so no scheduled A07 script changes how a later signal is processed at the baseline (P09-S095). FACT and INTERPRETATION: A12's end-of-day step 0 skips the gate because `main.py` passes none, so a `gate_state` row, if one existed, survives to the next boot, is rehydrated and, with a time-out shorter than the overnight gap, released `TIMEOUT` at the first check, overwriting its signal's status with `GATE_RELEASED_TIMEOUT`; this changes a stored earlier signal, not the processing of a new one; whether any row existed is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P09-S096). FACT (other topics' scheduled deletions, interface only): A12's `scripts/eod_cleanup.py` and A03's `scripts/db_retention.py` delete A07's rows of pruned or old signals; neither changes the processing of later signals (P09-S097). INTERPRETATION (time-driven state outside the schedules): A12's MIS blocklist (acting as a reject only with `shadow: false`), A09's governor pause, and the boot-time reading of every strategy file and flag (so an edited file takes effect only at the next boot) are the stored or remembered states found that one event sets and a later signal's A07-side outcome reads; the shared rate limiter and the step pool's rotation also link signals that run close together (P09-S098).

**Steps that cannot be established, and conflicts between sources.**

- UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED: whether any path ran in production, how often, with which configuration and in which mode; in particular whether the gate path ran at all, whether a FIX-069 re-run occurred, whether any step timed out, whether any screener or gate store write failed, and whether the metrics condition or the officer's notifications occurred (P09-S099; section 18.2). HISTORICAL (conflicts preserved, not resolved): the end-of-day clear of the gate described as done by FIX-046's message, `clear_all`'s docstring, IA-P2-01 and the approved A03 records H-50, Q-11 and Q-45, while the `main.py` wiring does not reach it; the gate path described as active by a test docstring and as dormant by the integrity audit and later comments; `continue_from_gate`'s docstring saying the gate manages the claim, which it does not; A03 P-02's "no caller passes it" against the screener's `ts=ts` (P09-S100).

**Q23 for every path.**

- INTERPRETATION (P09-S101):

| Path | Effective decision | Status transitions | Persistence | Caller → callee | Concurrency and locking | Duplicate or loss risk | Logging and alerts | Downstream handoff |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| accepted | proceed | `QUEUED` → `PROCESSING` → `PASSED` → `RESERVED` → `PROCESSED` (or `RETEST_WAITING`, or `PASSED` kept while A18's worker holds it) | status commit, row commit, P3 record | processor worker → screener → step pool, scorer, HardGate (shadow) → store, recorder | shared step worker; store write lock; recorder lock; unlocked counters | a second screening on a FIX-069 re-run; swallowed writes | INFO pass line; shadow line; no alert from A07 | tier and score to A09, A08, A18 |
| rejected by strategy control | do not proceed | `PROCESSING` → `REJECTED_STRATEGY_CONTROL` or `REJECTED_TRADE_TYPE` | A06's status commit and P2 record | processor → `strategy_will_trade` (pure) → A06's handler | none in A07 | lossy label | A06's INFO | none |
| rejected or skipped by the screener | do not proceed | `PROCESSING` → the screener's status (final) | status, row, P3 record | processor → screener | as accepted | a swallowed status write leaves `PROCESSING` | WARNING, ERROR or none by route; A06's INFO or WARNING | none |
| rejected on the enforce path | do not proceed | as above, with the HardGate's statuses | as above | screener → HardGate | as above | an uncaught gate exception becomes `PLACEMENT_FAILED` | WARNING with the evidence | none |
| gate resumption | proceed after `PRICE_HIT`; end on `TIMEOUT` or `QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` | held status → `GATE_RELEASED_*` → `PROCESSING` → A06's statuses | one release commit; A06's writes | `eg-worker` → callback → `gate-release` pool → `continue_from_gate` | gate lock, two workers, two release threads; no claim | a second resumption after a failed release and a restart; a lost `PRICE_HIT` on a non-dict `extras`; a throttle drop not returned to the gate | gate INFO/ERROR lines; unlogged poll-loop discards | the row's prices and tier to A09 |
| FIX-069 re-run | screened again; may pass or be rejected | `RESERVED` → `PROCESSING` → the screener's new status → … | a further status, row and P3 record per run | processor queue → `_process_one` again | the claim kept across the re-queue, subject to the sweeper's 60 s eviction | up to four screenings and rows; a reject after an earlier pass | A06's WARNING "re-queuing"; the screener's lines again | as accepted, or none |
| strategy disabled or PAPER_ONLY | do not proceed (disabled); no effect (PAPER_ONLY) | as strategy control; none for PAPER_ONLY | as strategy control; `strategy_metrics` rows | processor → resolver; cron → metrics script | none; separate process | — | a CRITICAL text without the strategy name; no notification on `main` | none |
| missing or invalid context | per case | per case | per case | per case | as accepted | defaults hide missing inputs in the score | mostly none (silent defaults) | as accepted when the signal passes |
| exception or database failure | per case | `PROCESSING` kept, `PLACEMENT_FAILED`, or the gate's kept status | partial or none | per case | store write lock | lost records; second resumption | ERROR lines; no alert from A07 | none, or as accepted |
| scheduled change | none for later signals from A07's scripts | `GATE_RELEASED_TIMEOUT` for a surviving row at the next boot | `strategy_metrics`, registry file | cron → scripts | separate processes; registry write without a lock | rows deleted by A12 and A03 jobs | the officer's notifications | none |

- INTERPRETATION (Q11 "bypass", across components): yes in five recorded ways: (a) the checks that apply depend on the mode; (b) pullback strategies bypass the entry gate entirely, because nothing routes them to it; (c) the gate resumption bypasses screening and the age check by design while re-checking strategy control, both windows, the kill switch and the governor (A06 PQ-13); (d) the end-of-day route bypasses A07's strategy control and screening and reaches the playbook gates only inside A18's PB-01 stages (`gate_pullback` and `gate_confirm` in the entry stage, all five in the would-be runner); (e) in MIS shadow mode a would-drop continues (P09-S102). INTERPRETATION ("enter a later step without satisfying an earlier one"): every step runs whatever the earlier results; on the gate path the entry reaches A09's sizing with prices derived when its row was written and no status check of its origin; after `PASSED` the FIX-067 re-anchor replaces the entry basis with a later quote (P09-S103). The other parts of Q11 ("evaluated twice", "lost", "accepted after a prior rejection") are in section 10 (P09-S104 to P09-S106). INTERPRETATION ("left in an ambiguous state"): `PROCESSING` after a swallowed write; `PASSED` while A18's enforce worker holds the candidate; `GATE_RELEASED_PRICE_HIT` while the resumption waits; a kept row and old status after a failed release; statuses with more than one cause; `PLACEMENT_FAILED` where no placement was attempted; the readers' differing labels (P09-S107).

**The handoff to A09 (Q15).**

- FACT (what leaves A07 on the direct path): a `ScreeningResult`, with the screener's status and row already written; the processor continues only when `passed` is true and then uses `tier` as the sizer's `score_tier` and in the retest diverter and the candidate builders, `score` in the diverter, the signal alert, the S&R observer and the V3 signal, and `step_results`, `step_statuses` and the snapshot only in the P1 evidence record and the V3 signal; strategy control hands over only `will_trade`, its `product` being read by no processor site (P09-S073).
- FACT (what A09 and the other owners decide after A07, interface only): the entry and stop prices (`_derive_prices(trigger_price, strategy_obj, now_time=...)`, re-derived from a fresh quote for non-pullback strategies), the target, the quantity (with the tier multiplier), the risk approval, the reservation, the per-strategy cap and the one-trade-per-symbol-and-direction check, the entry throttle and the placement with the strategy's `tgt_risk_reward` are A09's, A06's and A10's; none of the inputs of `_derive_prices` is an output of A07 (P09-S074).
- FACT (the gate path): the gate hands the `WatchEntry` and the reason to the callback; `continue_from_gate` reads the entry's prices, direction, tier, strategy, identifiers and trigger price and passes `release_ltp` to the placer, re-deriving no price; those prices come from the `gate_state` row, which no non-test code writes (P09-S075). FACT (price-like values A07 computes and does not hand to A09): the proximity rule's comparison of the trigger price with the limits less 2 %, returning only admit or reject with a reason naming the target or stop leg; `gate_rr`'s stop, target and R:R from the zone edges its A18 callers pass, returned in its evidence and recorded by A18 beside the live values; none reaches the processor's prices or the sizer (P09-S076). FACT (the scripts): the metrics script hands nothing to A09's governor or capital logic, which read `trades` themselves; the index's sentence that A09 "may cite the capital and governor handoff" of the script refers to a handoff the code does not contain (boundary, reported; sections 12 and 17) (P09-S077).
- INTERPRETATION (the A07-to-A09 contract at the baseline): A07 decides only whether a signal proceeds (strategy control's verdict, the screening outcome, the gate's release reason); it hands A09 the tier (which A09's sizer turns into a multiplier) and the score (which reaches observers, alerts and the allocator); it does not decide the entry price, the stop, the target, the R:R, the quantity, the risk approval, the reservation or the capital; two A07 checks anticipate later decisions without making them: the proximity rule rejects a trigger price at which "no profitable TGT" or "no valid SL" would fit the band, before A09 has derived any price, and the tier A07 computes is a sizing input in A09 (P09-S078).

**Technical-analysis and S&R information that A07 consumes (Q14; A08 owns the calculation).**

- FACT: on the only non-test screening route the steps and the HardGate receive only the dictionary built from the broker quote (`ltp`, `bid`, `ask`, `volume`, `circuit_state`, the two limits, `vwap` from the quote's `average_price`, `open`, `day_high`, `day_low`), with `atr`, `rsi`, `sector`, `prev_close` and `avg_volume_20d` fixed at `None`; no A07 file names `candle_math`, `sr_detector`, `sr_level`, `ZoneCache`, `zone_cache`, `daily_symbol`, `get_daily_symbol_stats`, `fno_ban`, `is_symbol_fno_banned`, `fetch_fno_ban` or `backfill`; the non-standard-library imports of the A07 modules and scripts include no technical-analysis or S&R module (P09-S068). FACT: A07 computes itself only the circuit state, the ten step values, the proximity test and, in the playbook gates, arithmetic on caller-supplied inputs; no A07 code computes an indicator from candles or a support or resistance level (P09-S069).
- FACT: the S&R zones, ATR, EMA, swings, regime state, five-minute candles and baseline that the playbook gates use are computed and passed by A18's callers (for example `near_sup.band_low` and `near_res.band_low` as `gate_rr`'s edges); A07's only contact with `regime/` is `gate_extreme`'s read of `extreme_flag`; on the live direct path A08's retest diverter runs after A07's `PASSED`, reads its own zone cache and receives the screening tier and score (P09-S070).
- INTERPRETATION (how A07 treats absent, stale, invalid or conflicting technical values): absent values become fixed step values without a log line, so on the live route steps 1 and 3 always score 0.0 and steps 4 and 6 always 0.5 and the total cannot exceed 65 without a timeout; stale values are not detected; an invalid 0.0 last price is used as a price and can set `lower_circuit` (A04 RD-12); conflicting values are used as received; the quote's `average_price` and A05's `session_vwap` are not compared, since A07 reads only the first; in the playbook gates a missing or NaN input is treated gate by gate, and a NaN can pass `gate_confirm` (those inputs are A18's) (P09-S071). FACT (A05 RTR L205; ADDENDUM 2): `atr` and `rsi` are `None`; the screener's `vwap` is the quote's `average_price` through A04's adapter; no A07 file reads the F&O ban list, so the ban list has no meaning for A07's trading decisions at the baseline (P09-S072).

**Inherited items on this section's side** (section 16 has every row): A01 B-08 (P09-S108: no A07 side found in the validation logic; the boundary point is reported, not resolved, section 17); A03 H-16 (P09-S109), H-17 (P09-S110: no A07 side found), P-02 (P09-S111), P-11 (P09-S112), Q-22 (P09-S113), U-24 (P09-S114: no A07 side found) and U-25 (P09-S115: no A07 side found); A05 FB-01, FB-02, FB-07 and FB-14 (P09-S116 to P09-S119: no A07 reader of the ban list); A06 WA-06 (P09-S120: no component on A07's path bounds the age from below), WA-07 (P09-S121) and EV-02 (P09-S122); A06 TG14's end-of-day excluded-symbol check (P09-S124: no A07 code performs one on any route; which A07 code, if any, the A06 treatment meant is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED); A05 RTR L205 (P09-S128).

## 12. The three strategy scripts and the direction registry (questions Q18 to Q21; Q4 for PAPER_ONLY)

This section covers the three scripts that Appendix A of the index assigns to A07 — `scripts/compute_strategy_metrics.py` (the end-of-day metrics script and its "demotion"), `scripts/strategy_registry_officer.py` (the direction-registry officer) and `scripts/strategy_status.py` (the per-strategy status table) — and the module they share with the end-of-day summary, `core/strategy_direction.py` with its seed and state files. Declared invocations are declarations only (A16's), never evidence that a job ran.

Evidence: `A07_STRATEGY_SCRIPTS_2026-11-21.md` (P07), which read the three files in full (352, 305 and 221 lines) and their callees at the interface (P07-S001 to P07-S004); and P02 section 9 for the direction module. Current external context, read 2026-11-21: Sharpe's and Lo's papers (method only), the Python `logging`, logging HOWTO, `sys` and `argparse` pages, the SQLite "ON CONFLICT" page and, for one statement, the checker's local Python 3.13 `argparse` source (P07-S008). Only the officer changed after `7d4970a` (the alert title's account tag; production use UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED) (P07-S005, P07-S006). The `TRADING_MODE` "PAPER" label of the metrics script is paper-only and not traced (P07-S007).

**`scripts/compute_strategy_metrics.py` (Q18).**

- HISTORICAL: the docstring (unchanged since `03ac3b0db2e01a357e40d411d44cdc94dab794a2`, 2026-05-31) says it "computes per-strategy performance metrics (Sharpe ratio, win rate, average P&L) and stores them in the strategy_metrics table", that "If Sharpe < -0.3 for 3 consecutive days, auto-sets strategy to PAPER_ONLY and sends Telegram CRITICAL alert", and that it is "Run by cron at 16:15 IST -- after EOD report and reconciliation" (P07-S009). FACT: three module constants, `_SHARPE_DEMOTION_THRESHOLD = -0.3`, `_CONSECUTIVE_BAD_DAYS = 3`, `_ANNUALIZATION_FACTOR = sqrt(252)`, not configurable (P07-S010).
- FACT (arguments and entry): `--config` ("config"), `--db` (default `None`; help "data_store/trading_system.db"), `--date` (default today IST; not validated by the parser), `--lookback` (30) and `--dry-run`; the command-line entry `_cron_main()` runs `main(argv=None)` inside A16's heartbeat wrapper, the options coming from the process command line (current external context, `argparse`) (P07-S011, P07-S012). The database defaults to `_ROOT / "data_store" / "trading_system.db"`, anchored to the repository, while the help text names a relative path (P07-S013).
- FACT (inputs): `load_config(config_dir)` (A02's `load_all`) is called and its result discarded, so it acts only as a gate, a failure logging ERROR and returning 1; this matches the handler A02 AUDIT L540 hands to A07 (P07-S014). The `StateStore` open is A03's (it re-applies the schema on every open, refuses pending migrations, builds a fresh database for a missing file and attaches `analytics.db`); INTERPRETATION: a `--db` naming a missing file creates a new database and finds no trades (P07-S015). The script reads `trades` (`strategy`, `status`, `net_pnl`, `updated_at`) and `strategy_metrics`, with no `trades.mode` filter (paper-only, not traced) (P07-S016). The strategies processed are the distinct `trades.strategy` values with a `CLOSED` trade whose `updated_at` date is within 30 days of the run date (a fixed 30, not `--lookback`; no upper bound; no `net_pnl IS NOT NULL` condition) (P07-S017). All three queries count only `CLOSED`, while the schema also allows `CLOSED_MANUAL`, which A09's governor, A09's performance allocator and several store helpers count; INTERPRETATION: a `CLOSED_MANUAL` trade is excluded from every metric while those readers include it (P07-S018). HISTORICAL: `docs/report_data_contract.md` describes the table as "a thin, derived, CLOSED-only EOD rollup" and "intentionally NOT used — it drops CLOSED_MANUAL"; the records describe the exclusion, none states whether it was intended in the script (P07-S098; section 19).
- FACT (calculations): the window is `as_of_date − lookback` to `as_of_date`, inclusive (31 dates by default), on the first ten characters of `updated_at`; INTERPRETATION: a later update of a closed row moves it to a later date (P07-S019). The Sharpe ratio sums `net_pnl` per date (dates with no closed trade absent), returns `None` with fewer than two dates, and otherwise `(mean / std) × sqrt(252)` with the sample (n − 1) variance, 0.0 when the standard deviation is 0; no risk-free rate is subtracted (P07-S020). Current external context (Sharpe, "The Sharpe Ratio"; Lo, "The Statistics of Sharpe Ratios"): the article defines the ratio on a differential return over a riskless rate and notes the zero-serial-correlation assumption of time scaling; Lo's abstract states that ratios cannot generally be annualised by the square-root rule (P07-S021). INTERPRETATION: the script's figure differs from the article's definition in three recorded ways (rupee P&L totals, not returns; no benchmark; only dates with a closed trade against a 252-day factor); whether it was meant as the textbook ratio or an internal score is stated by no source (P07-S022; section 19). FACT: the win rate is the share of closed trades with `net_pnl > 0` (a zero is not a win) and the average P&L the mean of `net_pnl`; the stored values are rounded to 4, 4 and 2 places, and the demotion check reads the rounded `sharpe` (P07-S023, P07-S024). FACT (A19, stated expectations only): the tests expect `None` with fewer than two days, 0.0 when every day has the same P&L, and a stored row after a run; INTERPRETATION: identical negative daily totals also give 0.0, which is not below the threshold (P07-S025).
- FACT (writes): the only write is, per processed strategy and not in a dry run, one `INSERT OR REPLACE INTO strategy_metrics (...)` inside its own transaction, under `UNIQUE(strategy, date)` (P07-S026); current external context (SQLite "ON CONFLICT"): REPLACE deletes the conflicting row first; INTERPRETATION: a re-run for the same date replaces that date's rows of the strategies it processes (P07-S027). FACT: no file, YAML, strategy file or other table is written by the script's own code (P07-S028). INTERPRETATION: each strategy's row is committed before the next is computed, so a failure part-way leaves the earlier rows (P07-S029).
- FACT (the demotion rule): `_check_demotion` reads the strategy's three most recent stored `sharpe` values (no date condition); with fewer than three rows, or unless all three are non-null and strictly below -0.3, it returns False; otherwise it logs CRITICAL `strategy_metrics.demotion_triggered` (strategy, threshold, count and values as `extra` fields), sends a notification only if a notifier is passed, and returns True (P07-S030). The three rows are the latest stored dates, not consecutive days; the check runs right after the strategy's own insert and not in a dry run; INTERPRETATION: "3 consecutive days" means the last three stored rows, possibly weeks apart (P07-S031). With a notifier the alert has severity `"ERROR"` (the docstring promises a "Telegram CRITICAL alert"), the title "STRATEGY DEMOTED -- <strategy>" and the sentence "Strategy set to PAPER_ONLY." (P07-S032). `main` passes `notifier=None`, the return value of the check is not used, and no other non-test caller exists; INTERPRETATION: on every route through `main`, the declared cron route included, a met condition produces only the CRITICAL log line, and the notification can be sent only by the test's mock (P07-S033). FACT: when the condition is met nothing is written, and no version of the file ever wrote a strategy state (P07-S034); A19's tests expect the boolean and the mock notifier, and no strategy state (P07-S035).
- FACT (heartbeat and non-trading days, A16's helpers): `_cron_main` returns 0 on a non-trading day (decided from today's IST date, not `--date`, with a weekday fallback) and otherwise runs `main` inside `HeartbeatTimer("compute_strategy_metrics", alert=True)`, recording `FAILED` on a non-zero return (P07-S036). Neither helper is given a database path, so the heartbeat goes to the relative `data_store/trading_system.db` whatever `--db` says, and is written even in a dry run; this matches A03 D-09 and A03 U-17 (P07-S037). The timer records an exception as `FAILED` and calls A14's alert with `alert=True` (the job's registry entry has `critical: false`) (P07-S038). The failure routes of `main` are the configuration gate, the store open and any exception inside the run, each an ERROR line and exit code 1; INTERPRETATION (current external context, `argparse` and `sys`): an unparseable `--date` takes the third route; an invalid option, and also `--help`, raise `SystemExit` inside the timer, which records `FAILED` (P07-S039).
- FACT and INTERPRETATION (logging; current external context, Python `logging` and the logging HOWTO): the script configures no logging and no module attaches handlers or calls a module-level logging function, so the handler of last resort writes WARNING and above as the bare message to standard error; the INFO lines are not output, and the demotion line is output as the bare text `strategy_metrics.demotion_triggered`, without the strategy name, threshold or Sharpe values, which are passed only as `extra`; under the declared crontab line standard error is appended to `logs/cron-strategy-metrics.log` (P07-S040, P07-S041).
- FACT (declared invocation, declarations only): `config/cron_registry.yaml` L589 to L602 (16:15 Mon-Fri, `critical: false`, `market_day_only`, `monitored`, `enabled`, the log target); `deploy/cron/trading-system.cron` L120 to L121 (cited by line only: it changes to the project directory, loads an environment file, sets `PYTHONPATH=.`, runs the script with no option and appends both streams to the log); INTERPRETATION: under that line the options take their defaults, and the anchored database and the relative heartbeat path name the same file when the project directory is the script's root (P07-S042). The jobs declared from 15:40 to 16:15 and whether any of them changed `trades` after 16:15 are A16's and the owners' to establish (P07-S043).

**What the metrics script changes downstream (Q18, Q4).**

- FACT (answer to Q18, "Can it set PAPER_ONLY or otherwise alter eligibility?"): no. Strategy control decides eligibility from the files' `enabled` and `intent` and the two flags read at boot, and has no input for PAPER_ONLY; `PAPER_ONLY` occurs on no module of the trading path; the script writes only `strategy_metrics` rows, nothing when the condition is met, and no module of the trading path reads `strategy_metrics`; on the `main` route the condition's only effect is a CRITICAL log line (P07-S044; P02-S075 to P02-S079).
- FACT (the readers of `strategy_metrics`, interface only): A15's `scripts/system_manager.py` reads today's win rate and trade count and warns "review" below 0.35 with at least 3 trades, writing nothing back; A15's `scripts/gemini_premarket_brief.py` selects three columns the table does not have (`sharpe_ratio`, `trade_count`, `status`) and returns `[]` on the resulting error (INTERPRETATION: at the baseline schema its query gives no rows); A03's drill lists the table; A15's trade review states it is "NOT used" (P07-S045). FACT and HISTORICAL: no non-test dashboard file names the table; a legacy audit's claim that it fed a dashboard period ranking differs from the code at both the audit's commit and the baseline (P07-S046).
- FACT (the "exact handoff to A09's governor/capital logic"): no file under `capital/` reads `strategy_metrics`, a Sharpe value, `PAPER_ONLY` or anything the script writes; A09's `StrategyGovernor` reads `trades` directly and keeps its pause in memory; A09's `PerformanceAllocator` computes its own win rate from `trades` and is instantiated by no non-test code; the processor's `perf_weights` is passed by no non-test caller; INTERPRETATION: at the baseline the script hands nothing to A09; the script and those modules share only the `trades` table, each with its own status set, window and arithmetic. Boundary (reported, not resolved): the index's sentence that "A09 may cite the capital and governor handoff of `scripts/compute_strategy_metrics.py`" (index L114) refers to a handoff the code does not contain; A09 owns the governor and capital side (P07-S047).
- HISTORICAL and FACT: the legacy audit of 05-Jul-2026 (`docs/audit/audit_05jul2026.md` L445, "Auto-demotion is a dead safety net that claims otherwise"; the script is identical at its commit and the baseline) states that no state is written, that the return value is discarded and that the cron path passes `notifier=None`; each agrees with the baseline; it does not state that the line is printed without the strategy name (P07-S048). HISTORICAL: A03 handed "the PAPER_ONLY demotion (A07)" over (A03 RTR L419; A03 AUDIT L3185; its evidence asks "Was a database write of the PAPER_ONLY demotion intended ...?"); FACT: the code agrees with A03's evidence; the intent is stated by no source (P07-S049). FACT (project record against the code): the index's Appendix A describes the script as "end-of-day strategy metrics; may set a strategy to PAPER_ONLY" (L303) and its A07 entry lets A09 cite a handoff (L114); at the baseline the script sets no strategy state and hands nothing to A09; the index text and the code differ, and the index is not changed (P07-S099; boundary, section 17).

**`scripts/strategy_registry_officer.py` (Q19).**

- HISTORICAL: the docstring calls it a "daily strategy-direction registry maintainer" that enumerates "the ACTUAL strategy set = loaded YAMLs (StrategyConfig) ∪ distinct signals.scanner / signals.strategy", diffs it against the runtime state file, "falling back to the git-tracked SEED ... only while no state file exists yet", registers each unseen strategy PENDING and notifies, advances PENDING to CONFIRMED at the first filled trade, silently, and sets `DIRECTION_CONFLICT` where a realized side differs, "NEVER overwriting the declared value"; "The daily job OWNS registry writes"; "DATA/REGISTRY ONLY — no trading decision changes, and direction is NEVER read from the registry to place a trade" (P07-S050).
- FACT (arguments and paths): `--config-dir` ("config"), `--db-path` (anchored default), `--registry` (default the state file under the repository's `data_store/`) and `--dry-run`; the seed is `config_dir / strategy_direction_registry.yaml`; `main` calls `run(...)`, logs INFO and always returns 0 (P07-S051; P02-S111). FACT (load-time effects): at import the script loads an environment file at the repository root (`.env*` pattern, not opened) and imports A01's `primary_account_tag`, which reads the account file under `config/` (not opened) and "NEVER raises", returning `"UNKNOWN"` on any failure; INTERPRETATION: a failed import there ends the process before any heartbeat (P07-S052).
- FACT (inputs): the direction map from the strategy files (`name`, `direction`; this is the `validate_strategy` call that A02 AUDIT L1320 hands to A07, matching) (P07-S053); the registry from the state file, else the seed, else `{}` (P07-S054); and, only when the database file exists, A03's write-capable `db_connect.connect` with the analytics attach (not `connect_readonly`), closed in a `finally` (A03 D-05) (P07-S055). The three queries (distinct `signals.scanner` and `signals.strategy`; strategies with `qty_filled > 0`; the upper-cased realized directions of filled trades), each swallowing every exception and returning what it had collected, with no status, date or mode filter (P07-S056). INTERPRETATION: a database that cannot be queried gives the same inputs as an empty one; every existing row then takes `health = "OK"`, so a recorded `DIRECTION_CONFLICT` is cleared, written and not announced, and announced again as new on a later run whose queries succeed (P07-S057).
- FACT (the reconcile rules, pure): NEW: each name of the actual set without a row gets the direction from the map, else a single realized side, else a WARNING "seen but direction unresolvable ... not registered" and no row; a new row is `PENDING`, `OK`, `first_seen` today and an evidence mapping (P07-S058); CONFIRM: a `PENDING` row with a filled trade becomes `CONFIRMED`, and nothing moves it back (P07-S059); CONFLICT: a row whose declared direction differs from any realized side becomes `DIRECTION_CONFLICT` (announced only when newly so), otherwise `OK`; the declared direction of an existing row is never changed (P07-S060). No row is removed and no direction is refreshed from the file; the status and health values are string literals and the module's `VALID_STATUSES` and `VALID_HEALTH` are read nowhere (P07-S061). INTERPRETATION: one differing filled trade keeps a row in conflict on every later run unless the trade rows change; for trades written through the trading path, a conflict arises when a file's direction was edited after its registry row was written, or for a signal-only name; a row for a removed file stays indefinitely (P07-S062).
- FACT (writes and notifications): in a dry run it prints one line and returns; otherwise it calls `save_registry` only when the registry changed (a temporary file and a rename, no lock) and then always calls `_notify` (P07-S063). `_notify` returns at once unless something was newly registered or newly conflicted (newly confirmed rows are never notified); otherwise it builds a body and the title "[<primary_account_tag()>] Strategy registry — <n> new, <m> conflict" (the account tag is not reproduced), sends a Telegram message through A14's notifier when its two environment variables are set (names only: `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN`, `TELEGRAM_CHANNEL_PRIMARY`), and writes A14's critical sentinel file for e-mail, each failure logged and not raised (P07-S064). FACT: its own code issues no SQL write; the other writes of a run are A03's open-time effects, A16's heartbeat and alert and the crontab's marker file (P07-S065). INTERPRETATION: an INFO-level registration is delivered through the path A14's writer describes as the one for a CRITICAL alert; the file is written before the notification, so a run failing after the write leaves the state written and unannounced, and the next run does not announce it (P07-S066).
- FACT (heartbeat, failures and logging): as for the metrics script, a non-trading-day skip and `HeartbeatTimer("strategy_registry_officer", alert=True)`; because `main` returns only 0, a `FAILED` heartbeat arises only from an exception (P07-S067). Neither helper is given a path, so the heartbeat goes to the relative default whatever `--db-path` says; this matches A03 D-09 and U-18, and the A03-10 history question stays OPEN (P07-S068). FACT: `run` and `main` have no handler of their own; errors in the queries and the two channels are swallowed, and every other exception is recorded `FAILED` and reported by the interpreter (current external context, Python `sys`); INTERPRETATION: `_notify`'s "Never raises" holds for the code read (P07-S069). INTERPRETATION (logging): the unresolvable-name WARNING is output with the name; the two ERROR lines are output without their error text; the INFO summary is not output; a normal run that changes the registry leaves no log line of its own (P07-S070). FACT (A03 D-05; A03-11): the comment "DB reads (read-only; the officer only writes the registry YAML)" stands above the write-capable open; the officer writes no row of its own, and its heartbeats go through A03's `StateStore`; this matches D-05, and the A03-11 history question stays OPEN (P07-S071).
- FACT (declared invocation, declarations only): `config/cron_registry.yaml` L626 to L640 (16:22 Mon-Fri, `critical: false`, a marker name), with a comment saying it "Runs 16:22 = after compute_strategy_metrics (16:15)" and "writes config/strategy_direction_registry.yaml", which differs from the code's state path; `deploy/cron/trading-system.cron` L126 to L127 (cited by line only) runs it with no option and writes a marker file whatever the exit status; INTERPRETATION: the officer reads nothing produced by the metrics job, so the declared order expresses no data dependency in the code (P07-S072).
- FACT and INTERPRETATION (the officer after an unreadable state file; A03 E-08): the officer always passes the seed path, so on such a run its registry is the seed's rows (all `PENDING` and `OK`) (P07-S073); then every strategy of the actual set not in the seed is registered again and announced, every seeded strategy with a filled trade is confirmed again silently, every differing row is announced again as a new conflict, rows of the lost state that are in neither the seed nor the actual set are absent, and, when the result differs from the seed, the state file is overwritten with the seed-derived rows; when it does not differ, nothing is written and the next run falls back again; nothing in the officer's output names the fallback; whether this occurred is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P07-S074).
- FACT (A19, stated expectations only): the officer's tests expect registration, confirmation, conflict flagging and self-healing, a dry run writing nothing, the seed listing the configured strategies with their directions, state written under `data_store/`, and a silent first run from the seed; no test covers an unreadable (as opposed to missing) state file (P07-S075). HISTORICAL: legacy records note the feature commit and its 16:22 schedule, a deployment on 18-Jul-2026, and that before the seed and state split the tracked file was "rewritten daily" and "nothing reads it but its own writer"; none is evidence that the job ran (P07-S076).

**`core/strategy_direction.py`, the seed and the state file (P02's study of the module).**

- HISTORICAL: the docstring says `StrategyConfig.direction` "is the SINGLE source of truth for a strategy's LONG/SHORT" and the module gives "ONE canonical accessor"; the registry is "SPLIT IN TWO": the tracked SEED `config/strategy_direction_registry.yaml`, "read-only in production", and the RUNTIME STATE `data_store/strategy_direction_registry.yaml`, "the only thing production writes"; "NOTHING reads direction FROM the registry to place a trade" (P02-S098); the split (`b27cf6a`, 2026-08-10) was made because the officer's writes into the tracked file made the deployed tree differ from HEAD (P02-S099).
- FACT: the constants (`VALID_DIRECTIONS`, `VALID_STATUSES`, `VALID_HEALTH`, two default paths, both relative, and the alias `DEFAULT_REGISTRY_PATH`), of which `VALID_STATUSES`, `VALID_HEALTH` and `DEFAULT_REGISTRY_PATH` have no reader (P02-S100). `build_direction_map(config_dir="config")` validates every strategy file, skips a failing one and keeps LONG or SHORT directions ("never raises") (P02-S101); `canonical_direction` has only a test caller (P02-S102); `_read_registry` returns `None` for a missing or unparseable file, and `load_registry` returns the state's rows, else the seed's when a seed path is given, else `{}` ("The seed is a FALLBACK, never an override"); INTERPRETATION: an unparseable state file is treated like an absent one (P02-S103); `save_registry` writes `<path>.tmp` and renames it, with no lock, and its only caller is the officer (P02-S104); `registered_direction` has no caller (HISTORICAL: `PATHS.md` L40, "NOT retired") (P02-S105).
- FACT (consumers): the only non-test importers are A12's `orders/eod_squareoff.py` and the officer; no module of `signals/`, `screening/`, `strategies/`, `capital/` or `main.py` imports it (P02-S106); A12's end-of-day summary calls `build_direction_map("config")` in a `try` and uses the map only to group its summary text (INTERPRETATION: display only; the relative path resolves against the working directory, A02 F-11) (P02-S107); the trading path takes a trade's direction from the strategy object or the parked object and never from the registry (P02-S108).
- FACT and HISTORICAL (the seed and state files): the seed (last changed 2026-07-18) holds 16 rows, all `PENDING` and `OK`, with the same names and directions as the strategy files; its header says it is "auto-maintained by scripts/strategy_registry_officer.py", which predates the split (P02-S109); `data_store/` is ignored by Git and holds no tracked file; whether a state file existed on the production machine, and what it held, is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P02-S110); the officer's state path is anchored to the repository while the seed and the strategy files resolve against the working directory unless `--config-dir` is given (P02-S111); the cron declaration's comment says the officer writes the tracked file, which differs from the code (P02-S112); INTERPRETATION: two concurrent runs would share the fixed temporary file; whether runs overlapped is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P02-S113). FACT (A03 E-08, the module side): `_read_registry` returns `None` on any exception without a log line, `load_registry` then falls back to the seed, and the module imports no logger (P02-S129).

**`scripts/strategy_status.py` (Q20).**

- HISTORICAL: the docstring says it "Builds the per-strategy 'will it trade today?' table from the SAME resolver the entry gate uses ... so the table can NEVER disagree with live behaviour", renders an HTML table ("full 15 rows"), a compact Telegram view and a plain-text mirror, and turns malformed YAML into "a CONFIG ERROR row (never crash the report)" (P07-S077).
- FACT: the file has no command-line entry; it is a library of seven public functions and the `StatusRow` class (P07-S078). Its only inputs are the strategy files validated at the call and the two flags its caller passes; it reads `name`, `intent`, `enabled`, the entry window, `sl_pct`, `tgt_risk_reward`, `direction`, `pipeline`, `horizon` and `v3_playbook` (the list A02 AUDIT L1320 hands to A07, matching); rows are sorted WILL TRADE first; the verdict is the resolver's, which ignores the windows and the governor (P07-S079). The outputs are a summary line, a footnote (which does not read its `force_intraday_only` parameter), a 12-column HTML table with no reason column, name lists and a plain-text view; `render_telegram` has no caller, the cron officer's briefing building its own Telegram text from the name lists, as the function's docstring says (P07-S080). FACT: the module writes nothing and reads neither `strategy_metrics`, the registry, `system_config.yaml` nor `delivery_enabled` (P07-S081). FACT (caller, A16's): only `_strategy_status_payload` in `scripts/cron_officer.py`, for the report that is not the end-of-day one; the cron officer's own declarations are 09:20 daily and 18:50 Mon-Fri (declarations only) (P07-S082). FACT: `StatusRow.will_trade`, the sort key and the escaping helper are as P07-S100 records.
- INTERPRETATION (answer to Q20, "Is its output authoritative, derived, stale, or inconsistent with the control path?"): the table mutates nothing; its verdict is derived (the resolver computed again at the table's own run time, from fresh file reads and raw flags), not the trading process's boot-time state; it can be stale or inconsistent under the conditions of section 3 (a file or flag edited after boot, a raw value of another type, an out-of-set `trade_type`, the omitted playbook strategy, the absent `delivery_enabled`), and at the baseline file values the table and the gate agree; it shows no window, governor, metric or registry state, so a WILL TRADE row states only that strategy control allows the strategy; which topic applies the KNOWN DEFECT test to its "can NEVER disagree" claim is a boundary question (P02-AP04) (P07-S083). FACT (A02 K-05): `build_status_rows` takes and passes only `trade_type` and `force_intraday_only`, and neither it nor its caller reads `delivery_enabled`; this matches A02 K-05's description, which records the effect as INTERPRETATION and names A16 for the status rows (P07-S084).
- FACT (A19, stated expectations only): the strategy-control and taxonomy tests expect each verdict to equal the resolver, a malformed file to become one CONFIG ERROR row, and 15 rows with the playbook strategy filtered out (P07-S085). HISTORICAL and FACT: `PATHS.md` L291 describes the module's three views, the G0 report calls it "READ-ONLY status" and lists `strategy_metrics` as a data source for a planned dashboard screen (which the module does not read), and a configuration guide says it "never lies" (A02 K-05); the module is read-only and its own Telegram view is unused (P07-S086).

**Scheduled scripts and live signal processing: sources of truth, semantics and timing (Q21).**

- FACT (P07-S087):

| Component | Strategy set | States or verdicts it produces | Data read | When read |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Trading path (strategy control) | the strategy objects loaded and validated at boot | `will_trade` with a cause; `REJECTED_STRATEGY_CONTROL` or `REJECTED_TRADE_TYPE` | the files' `enabled` and `intent`; the validated flags | once, at boot |
| Metrics script | distinct `trades.strategy` with a `CLOSED` trade in the last 30 days | `strategy_metrics` rows; a "demotion" condition that only logs | `trades` (`CLOSED` only), `strategy_metrics` | each run |
| Registry officer | validated strategy files ∪ `signals.scanner` ∪ `signals.strategy` | `PENDING`/`CONFIRMED`; `OK`/`DIRECTION_CONFLICT` | strategy files, the registry state or seed, `signals`, filled `trades` | each run |
| Status table | strategy files validated at the call, less any `v3_playbook` strategy | WILL TRADE, WON'T TRADE, CONFIG ERROR | strategy files; raw flags from the cron officer | each briefing call |

- INTERPRETATION (answer to Q21, "Do scheduled functional scripts and live signal processing use the same source of truth and status semantics?"): no single source of truth is shared; only the status table applies the trading path's rule, to inputs read separately; the metrics script and the officer produce states that no module of the trading path reads, so at the baseline no scheduled script changes how a later signal is processed; the vocabularies do not map onto one another ("active" means a closed trade in 30 days, "enabled" the file switch, the officer's "actual set" includes signal names, the table shows "the 15 live strategies"; "PAPER_ONLY" and "STRATEGY DEMOTED" name no state; "CONFIRMED" has no meaning in strategy control) (P07-S088). FACT and INTERPRETATION (names): the metrics script groups by `trades.strategy`, the officer joins file names with signal names, the trading path maps scanners through A06's map; at the file values a scanner name and its strategy coincide; an unmapped name in `signals` is registered only with exactly one realized side and otherwise produces the WARNING on every run; what names production held is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P07-S089).
- FACT (declared timing, declarations only; A16's): the briefing with the status table at 09:20 daily; the metrics job at 16:15 and the officer at 16:22, Mon-Fri, market days only; A15's `system_manager_eod` at 18:45 and the premarket brief at 08:55; the global entry window 10:00 to 15:00 at the file values; the trading process reads the files and flags once at boot, and when it booted is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P07-S090). FACT: the only tracked non-test writer of `strategy_metrics` is the metrics script, the only caller of `save_registry` the officer, and the status table writes nothing; the heartbeat rows go to a table every instrumented job shares (P07-S091).
- INTERPRETATION (race conditions and timing gaps the code allows; none established as having occurred): (a) the metrics script's separate auto-committed reads can see different database states; (b) each metrics row waits up to 30 s for the write lock and a longer lock ends the run with earlier rows kept; (c) a trade closed or updated after the 16:15 run with that day's date is absent from that day's row and enters the next day's window; (d) the officer's three queries have no common transaction; (e) the officer's registry write has a fixed temporary name and no lock, so a manual run overlapping the cron run could interleave; (f) the 09:20 table can differ from the trading process's boot-time state after an edit; no scheduled script and the trading path update the same row (P07-S092). UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED: whether either scheduled script ran, on which days, with which options and with what outcome; HISTORICAL: a legacy health audit reports "compute_strategy_metrics ... SUCCESS" on 15-Jul-2026 and other records name rows and runs, none verified; a SUCCESS heartbeat records only that `main` returned 0, which a run that found no strategies also gives (P07-S093, P07-S101).

**Inherited items and handoff points on this section's side** (section 16 has every row; P07-S094 to P07-S096): A02 K-05 (P07-S084), A03 D-05 and A03-11 (P07-S055, P07-S065, P07-S071), A03 D-09 and A03-10 (P07-S037, P07-S042, P07-S068), A03 E-08 (P07-S054, P07-S073, P07-S074; module side P02-S129), A02 F-11 (P02-S107), the PAPER_ONLY demotion handed over by A03 RTR L419 (P07-S030 to P07-S035, P07-S044, P07-S049, P07-S099), A02 AUDIT L540 (P07-S014) and A02 AUDIT L1320 for the officer and the status table (P07-S053, P07-S079). A03 U-17, U-18 and E-34 are among the records outside the 51 that name A07 (P07-S095). Each item keeps its owners, ID, classification, treatment and status; the A03-10 and A03-11 history questions stay OPEN, and no intent is inferred (P07-S096).

## 13. Configuration read by A07: configured, committed and effective values (question Q9)

This section records every configuration key, strategy field, path, option and environment input that A07 code reads: where and how it is read, the schema default (A02's, interface only), the committed value (FACT about the file only), any fallback in A07 code, the readers that disagree, and the trace from the configured value to the value A07's code uses. The step, scoring, gate and control arithmetic itself is in sections 3 to 7 and is cited, not repeated (P08-S073).

Evidence: `A07_CONFIGURATION_KEYS_AND_EFFECTIVE_VALUES_2026-11-21.md` (P08), which read the thirteen A07 files for every configuration read and each caller that constructs an A07 class (P08-S001, P08-S002, P08-S008), with a checker's own inventory search that added three reads (P08-S075); a helper script parsed the 16 strategy files as data (P08-S004). Current external context, read 2026-11-21: the Pydantic conversion table and fields pages (latest release; the baseline pins 2.13.0), the PyYAML documentation (pinned 6.0.3) and the python-dotenv page (shown 1.2.4; pinned 1.2.2) (P08-S005). The four credential-holding patterns were not opened; where A07 code names such a file, the key or variable and the file's type and location only are recorded (P08-S006).

**Three levels of "effective" (P08-S019).** INTERPRETATION: (1) the committed value is FACT about the file at the baseline; (2) the code-effective value at the committed files is what A07's code would compute from it through the routes below, including defaults, fallbacks, units and whether it reaches a decision at all, a reading of the code and not evidence of a run; (3) the production-effective value is what a process on either machine actually used, which for every key is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (A02 RTR L179; A02 T-21); because some committed values changed over time, (1) is not evidence of (3) for any day.

**How configuration reaches A07 code: the routes.**

- FACT (route L): `main.py` loads `app_config = load_all(config_dir)` once at boot from `--config` or `config` (exit code 5 on any failure); `load_all` reads exactly eight files with `yaml.safe_load` and validates each with its Pydantic model, merging no second file and reading no environment variable ("No env-var interpolation, no includes") (P08-S009). FACT (route S): the strategies are loaded once by A02's loader through `validate_strategy` (`extra="forbid"`) and passed to the boot summary and the processor ("Strategies are loaded ONCE; no hot reload") (P08-S010). FACT (route V): `build_direction_map`, `build_taxonomy_map` and `build_status_rows` validate the strategy files at the time they are called, not using the boot-time objects (P08-S011). FACT (route R): A16's cron officer reads `system_config.yaml` raw with `yaml.safe_load` and its own defaults for the status table, with no validator (P08-S012). FACT (route C): A07's classes in the trading process receive configured values only as constructor arguments from `app_config` (the scorer, the executor's market open, the HardGate's freshness and switch, the screener's switches, MIS settings, resolver closure, hard gate and scoring object), and the resolver its two flags through the processor's stored attributes; where `main.py` passes nothing, the A07 constructor's own default applies (the step timeout 5.0 s; the gate's poll interval 5.0 s and two workers); the one A07 function that opens strategy files inside the trading process is `build_direction_map`, when A12's end-of-day summary calls it (P08-S013). FACT (route O): the scripts' own options and environment (the metrics script's `--config`, `--db`, `--date`, `--lookback`, `--dry-run` and `TRADING_MODE`; the officer's `--config-dir`, `--db-path`, `--registry`, `--dry-run` and its `load_dotenv` of the repository's environment file); under the declared cron lines the option defaults apply (P08-S014).
- FACT and INTERPRETATION (coercions; current external context): on routes L, S and V the text is parsed by `yaml.safe_load` and validated by Pydantic; on route R it is parsed only; a bare `off` for `scoring.v3_hardgate_mode` would load as `False`, fail the `str` field and stop the boot with exit code 5, which the file's own comment guards against ("quoted — YAML reads bare off as false"); on route R `bool("false")` is `True` and an out-of-set `trade_type` reaches the resolver unchanged and acts as BOTH (P08-S015).
