**Scoring keys (`config/scoring_weights.yaml`, `ScoringConfig`).**

- FACT: every scoring key A07 reads is as section 6 tabulates (P05-S033), confirmed line by line; the ten weights and the two tier thresholds are unchanged since the initial commit, `min_pass_score: 60` dates from 2026-07-10 ("55->60"), `v3_hardgate_mode: "shadow"` from 2026-07-12, and the other `v3_*` lines from 2026-07-12; the earlier values of `min_pass_score` are HISTORICAL (P08-S036). FACT and INTERPRETATION: one validated `ScoringConfig` object goes to the scorer (read per call; the step names once from the model's fields), the screener (copies once) and the HardGate (`float()` once), so within one process the scorer and the screener cannot see different values and the copies are fixed for the process's life (P08-S037).
- INTERPRETATION (code-effective use at the committed mode `"shadow"`) (P08-S038):

| Key | Committed | Where its value acts | Effect on the screening result |
|---|---|---|---|
| `steps.*` | 15, 10, 10, 10, 15, 10, 5, 5, 10, 10 | the scorer's total; the shadow log's re-scaled total | decides the score; ceiling 65 on the live route without timeouts |
| `min_pass_score` | 60 | the screener's effective minimum when `min_score` is 0 | the live pass bar for all 16 strategies; the scorer's own `passed` unread |
| `high_score_threshold` | 80 | the scorer's tier | `HIGH` unreachable under the ceiling |
| `medium_score_threshold` | 65 | the scorer's tier | `MEDIUM` only at exactly 65 |
| `v3_hardgate_mode` | `"shadow"` | the branch tests | the shadow comparison runs; the enforce branch does not; the result follows the OFF path |
| `v3_gate_steps` | `[circuit_check, signal_age]` | the shadow log's re-scaled total | none on the result |
| `v3_freshness_max_sec` | 60.0 | the HardGate's freshness test, in the shadow log only | none on the result |
| `v3_min_pass_score` | 50 | only the enforce path | none |
| `v3_high_score_threshold`, `v3_medium_score_threshold` | 75, 56 | the NEW tier in the shadow log | none on the result |

- INTERPRETATION (readers that could disagree): inside A07 the fallbacks equal the schema defaults, so a disagreement could arise only if a fallback were reached, which the validated object prevents; the scorer has no fallback (a missing field would raise); the disagreeing readers are outside A07 and are A02's records: the daily report's fallback 60, the dashboard's fallback to a key that does not exist in `system_config.yaml`, the parity script's default weights (A02 T-20) and the `v3_chain` seed thresholds (A02 K-27) (P08-S039). FACT and HISTORICAL (the strategy field that overrides a scoring key): `min_score` replaces `min_pass_score` on the OFF and shadow paths when above 0 and `v3_min_pass_score` on the enforce path through the re-scale; no committed strategy sets it; `gap_fade_long` carried 40 and then 30 ("TEMP: lowered for paper testing") from 2026-04-28 until 2026-06-08 (P08-S040). FACT: the units, rounding and boundaries are those of section 7's summary; the production-effective value of every scoring key is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, and the legacy records name moments when a machine's value differed from the committed file (P08-S041).

**From configured to effective: the trace (Q9).**

- FACT and INTERPRETATION: P08-S060 traces every key and field from its committed value through its route and transformation to its code-effective value at the committed files and whether it acts on a strategy-control or screening decision; for every row the production-effective value is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P08-S060). INTERPRETATION (what the trace shows): at the committed files and the `main.py` wiring, the configured values that act on a strategy-control or screening decision are `trade_type`, `force_intraday_only`, the strategies' `intent` and `enabled` (control); `mis_filter.enabled` with `mis_filter.shadow` (which together keep R1 from rejecting), `entry_gate.circuit_proximity_reject_enabled` (R3), the step weights, `min_pass_score` (selected by `min_score` 0), `trading_hours.market_open` (step 7), the strategies' `direction` (through step 2) and `max_spread_pct` (screening), and `v3_hardgate_mode`, which selects the decision path (at `"shadow"` the decision follows the OFF path). Configured values that reach A07 code but act on no decision at the committed values are `mis_filter.ttl_days` and `product_map` (log only while shadow is on), `v3_gate_steps`, `v3_freshness_max_sec`, the two V3 tier thresholds (log only), `v3_min_pass_score` (unused in shadow), `min_volume_surge` and `min_adr_pct` (their steps' inputs are always `None`), the tier thresholds as far as the pass decision goes, the display-only strategy fields, and the scripts' options, paths, environment and registry seed. Configured values that do not reach A07 code at all are `delivery_enabled`, the other `entry_gate` keys, `special_sessions`, the `pullback_wait_*` fields, `order_protocol`, the other strategy fields and the three `v3_chain` seed thresholds; the `v3_chain` gate keys reach A07's playbook gates only through A18's callers, and three of them not at all (P08-S061).
- FACT: the A07 code on the strategy-control and screening path re-reads no configuration or strategy file during the life of the trading process; the status table, the taxonomy map, the direction map and the two scripts read files at their own run time, and the direction map is also built inside the trading process by A12's end-of-day summary, which validates the strategy files under the relative `"config"` at that moment and uses the map only to label its summary (P08-S062). UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED: the production-effective value of every key on either machine on any day; what would settle it: a surviving copy of the deployed `config/` tree with its date, the boot log lines that print configuration (`strategy_control.summary`; the `EntryGate started` line), the evidence recorder's recorded file hashes, or stored screener rows with `eligible_score` and scores (P08-S063).

**Inherited items on this section's side** (section 16 has every row): A01 C11 (P08-S064: the resolver's `force_intraday_only` comes from the caller's argument, not from either load; the executor's 09:15 constant is a fallback that the `main.py` wiring does not reach; C11's intent source P1 is met for the regular open and not for a special-session date); A02 V-06 (P08-S065: at the committed values A5 cannot block), T-14 (P08-S066), T-20 (P08-S067: no configured key carries P9a's 90 s; the configured 60.0 key acts only in the shadow log at the committed mode), F-11 (P08-S068: latent on A07's side), K-05 (P08-S069) and K-27 (P08-S070: the `v3_chain` seed copies have no effect on A07's side); A04 OA-22 (P08-S071: no A07 file reads `order_protocol`) and CG-05 (P08-S072: no A07 configuration key sets a threshold per direction).

## 14. Unused, unreachable, partially wired and test-only components (question Q25)

This section answers Q25 ("Are there unused, unreachable, partially wired, test-only or planned components? Distinguish repository presence, declared intent and proven production reachability.") across the parts. **The 36 tests: what each states as expected behaviour for A07 code (A19's; never run).**

- FACT (method): the 36 files of inventory section 6 were read as text; no test was imported or run; a test states an expectation under its own conditions (mocks, stubs, temporary databases, paper-mode wiring) and is never evidence of production behaviour (P10-S067). HISTORICAL (one line per file): the table of P10-S068 records each file's stated expectations, for example: the gate's tests validate EG1 to EG15 (the symmetric inclusive band, the time-out, three quote failures, the entry removed before the callback, the callback still called when the store fails, rehydration skipping malformed rows, the atomic release); the end-of-day test wires a mock gate into the end-of-day object; the evidence tests cover the screener's P3 capture; the HardGate tests cover the three tests, the re-scale helpers and the three modes; the MIS tests cover the active and shadow modes; the M-S3 test calls the timeout "INERT IN PRODUCTION -- measured, not assumed"; the pre-fill test covers R3; the Phase-4, Slice 2 and PB-01 tests cover strategy control and the status table; the scorer's, screener's and executor's tests cover QS, SS and SE; the V3 tests cover the five playbook gates; the taxonomy test covers the 16 strategies' labels (P10-S068).
- FACT and INTERPRETATION (counts): nine of the 36 match the inventory's pattern by name only and state no expectation about A07 code, two only wire or mock A07 classes, and 25 state expectations about A07 code; the pattern matches A08's `screening.retest_monitor`, A02's `EntryGateConfig` and test names; the list of 36 is complete for its pattern (P10-S069). FACT (differences between test expectations and the code or other sources): the age tests are named for 90 s and use ages above 90 s, so the stated expectation and the 60 s rule agree on the inputs used; two tests count 15 live strategies against 16 files; a test docstring calls the gate path "active"; an evidence test assumes the hard gate is not enforcing; no test constructs the `main.py` wiring of the gate into the end of day, and no test exercises a live caller of `EntryGate.add()` (P10-S070).

**Q26: is anything A07-owned missing from the source file inventory?**

- FACT: no further A07 file was found; every non-test import of an A07 module lies inside a range the inventory lists (P10-S071). FACT (call sites and inputs in other topics' files that the inventory does not list; owners keep them): `main.py` L3620 to L3621 (the two strategy-control flags passed to the processor), L3157 to L3162 (the strategy load), L3248 to L3280 and L3297 (the evidence recorder), and L3055 to L3059 (the MIS blocklist's construction); the processor's reads of the `ScreeningResult` after L1028; `config/expected_managers.yaml` L296 to L308 (the scorer's two telemetry units); `orders/order_placer.py` L896 (the use of a gate's `release_ltp`) and the placer as the writer of the MIS blocklist (A04 CS-31) (P10-S072). FACT (dependencies the inventory's related-path list does not name): `core/time_authority.py` and `core/account_registry.py` (A01), `core/logger.py` (A15), `orders/price_math.py` (A09), `core/config_loader.py` (A02), A14's two alert modules, the officer's environment file (type and location only), and the third-party `yaml` and `dotenv` (P10-S073). FACT (consumers of A07 outputs the inventory does not list): A15's reports, scripts, evidence classifier and six dashboard backend files; A18's forward-shadow and soak scripts and module; A19's replay script; A03's migrations, drill and retention scripts; A12's cleanup script; A08's detector; and files naming A07 code (P10-S074). FACT and INTERPRETATION (code outside A07 that repeats A07 logic): A18's `v3_chain/forward_shadow.py` "Mirrors screening/step_executor _step_1/_step_3/_step_4/_step_6 exactly" with its own defaults, while `v3_chain/score.py` says it is "NOT a second scorer"; whether the copy matches A07's code over time is not A07's to decide (boundary) (P10-S075). FACT: 24 further test files and six dashboard tests name A07 tables, statuses or scoring keys without naming an A07 module, and were not read in detail (P10-S076).
- FACT and INTERPRETATION (P01's 31 records outside the 51, ADDENDUM 1): none names an A07 file the inventory lacks; six name lines in other topics' files that pass inputs to A07 logic and that the inventory does not list (A01 S-30, S-36; A02 K-40, K-41, K-44; A04 CS-31), and two name another topic's code with A07 as an owner (A01 W-23, the governor builder; A01 S-53, the start-up check), which are boundary points (section 17); P01-S122's question whether the pattern search found every record remains open (P10-S077, P10-S078). FACT (summary): no A07-owned file, script or test is missing for the stated patterns; not listed are the input and output lines, dependencies, consumers, duplicate formulas and further tests above; the exclusions and their owners are those of the inventory and the index (P10-S079; section 20).

## 16. The 51 inherited items and the 10 Open Issues Register entries that mention A07

This section lists, one row per item, the 51 items that the approved A01 to A06 RECONCILED TOPIC RECORDS hand to A07 and the 10 Open Issues Register entries that mention A07, with the A07 side found at the baseline. Every ID, owner, classification, treatment and status stays as recorded (D-24, D-30; Topic Completion Register, entry A07, Scope); A07 records its own side only, and no item is re-owned, re-classified or closed here (P01-S011, P01-S089). KNOWN DEFECT is not used (P01-S001).

Evidence: `A07_PRIOR_TOPIC_HANDOFFS_2026-11-21.md` (P01), which read the project registers and the A01 to A06 records and located the A07 code of each item without tracing it (P01-S001 to P01-S009), and the tracing statements of P02 to P09 named in the last column.

### 16.1 The records as handed over

- HISTORICAL (record): the Topic Completion Register lists the 51 IDs by source topic (A01 6, A02 7, A03 14, A04 5, A05 6, A06 13), each keeping "its original owners, ID, classification, status and treatment; A07 records its own side only" (P01-S011); the six lists equal the A07 rows of the six RECONCILED TOPIC RECORDS and the AUDITs' collected indexes (P01-S012). The records' handoff rules list a joint cell under every topic it names, so an item can be in A07's list without the record stating an A07 part (P01-S013).
- FACT: 39 of the 51 have an Audit Findings Register row; the other 12 are MATCH results, which the register does not enter (B-08, V-23, Q-45, PR-23, CM-22, FB-14, PQ-13, PQ-14, PQ-15, EV-19, ET-13, XA-11). Of the 39, 35 are classified ISSUE and 4 UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (RL-11, CM-07, FB-07, ET-06); 34 status cells read "AGREED", 1 "AGREED in part; OPEN QUESTION in part" (C11, the open part being Open Issues Register A01-09) and 4 "UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (no treatment)"; no row is CLOSED (P01-S014, P01-S123).
- HISTORICAL (record): the A06 records written before 20-Nov-2026 still describe the A06-to-A07 line as OPEN FOR RAMA; D-38 decided reading (b), the hand-over at the screening call `signals/signal_processor.py` L998, with A07 covering the logic of `strategy_will_trade(...)` called before it, and the approved A06 records "are not edited: this entry and the Open Issues Register govern" (P01-S010).
- FACT (where the source does not state the A07 side, P01 section 4; reported, not resolved): no A07 part is stated for A03 U-24 and U-25, which name A07 only in the joint row A03 AUDIT L3185 (P01-S090, P01-S091), nor by A03's own records for H-17, for which A05's records state one (P01-S092); A07 is named without its part in A02 K-27 and A01 C14 (P01-S093, P01-S094); the A07 part stated points at code that the index gives to another topic in A01 B-08 (A01's and A02's validation path), A01 C11 (the loads in `utils/startup_checks.py` and `main.py`) and A04 OA-22 and CG-05 (A02's strategy files) (P01-S095 to P01-S097); for 15 of the 51 the record's own Owner field does not name A07 while a handoff row does, and PQ-14 and XA-11 name A07 only "under reading (a)", which D-38 did not choose (P01-S098). These points are carried to section 17 and to section 18.1.

## 17. Cross-topic boundaries and handoffs

This section records, by owner topic, what crosses between A07 and each other topic at the baseline and every boundary point that the parts reported. The owners are those of the Master System Investigation Index; each other topic's code is read at the interface only (P09-S004). A boundary point is reported here, not resolved: no record of another topic is edited, and no item is re-owned or re-classified (P01-S089; P09-S004). Each point named below is carried in full, with its source IDs, into section 18.1.

### 17.1 The start line and the two Rama decisions (D-38, D-39)

- HISTORICAL and FACT (D-38): A07 begins at the screening call `signals/signal_processor.py` L998 and covers the logic of `strategy_will_trade(...)` called at L959, L2062 and L2411; the strategy lookup (L933 to L953) and the processor's orchestration up to L998 are A06's, and PQ-14 stays an A06 MATCH with A06 as owner; the approved A06 records written before D-38 still describe the line as OPEN FOR RAMA and are not edited: D-38 and the Open Issues Register govern (P01-S010, P01-S086, P02-S128, P09-S004). Whether every A07 statement that relies on the start line should cite D-38 is P01-S102.
- HISTORICAL and FACT (D-39, P1): the five playbook gates in `screening/hard_gate.py` are studied in full (section 7); their call sites are A18's and are recorded at the interface only; `gate_rr`'s arithmetic is recorded as written, while the live entry, stop, target and R:R decisions are A09's and the S&R inputs A08's (P05-S004; P05 sections 8 and 9, P05-S093 to P05-S098).
- HISTORICAL and FACT (D-39, P2): `screening/entry_gate.py` is studied in full (section 8); the callback into `continue_from_gate` is recorded at the interface only; the `strategy_will_trade` call inside it (L2062) is A07's logic (P02-S036 to P02-S040; P06 section 11).
- HISTORICAL and FACT (D-39, P3): the comparison of the screening components with the actual Chartink scanner conditions is made only when Rama supplies the conditions; until then it is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, and no scanner condition is inferred from names, URLs, comments or memory (P01-S084, P01-S125, P04-S139).

### 17.2 By owner topic

**A01 (start-up, wiring, windows, shutdown; `main.py`).** FACT (interface): `main.py` builds the entry gate, starts it after the signal handlers are installed (L3975) and stops it at L1748; it owns the two-worker `_gate_release_pool` and the callback (L865 to L893), which is never shut down (P06-S084 to P06-S090, P06-S109 to P06-S112); it builds the end-of-day object without `entry_gate`, so the gate's `clear_all` is not reached (P06-S074, P06-S075); it passes `market_windows.market_open` to the step executor, not the special-session time (P04-S018 to P04-S022, P08-S032); it performs the two strategy loads of A01 C11 and the boot summary that calls `strategy_will_trade` (P02-S041, P02-S056, P02-S127); A01's per-strategy entry window precedes screening and its logic is A01's (P02-S086 to P02-S090, P02-S124); the boot-abort path of A01 B-08 runs A01's `check_strategy_configs` and A02's loader, with no A07 function (P09-S108). FACT (records): A01 W-23 names A07 as an owner of the strategy-governor builder (`main.py` L846 to L862) and A01 S-53 of `check_strategy_configs`; A01 S-30 and S-36 name `main.py` lines that pass inputs to A07 logic (P01-S073, P10-S077, P10-S078). Boundary points reported: P06-AP02 (the unwired end-of-day clear), P09-AP25 (a surviving row released at the next boot), P06-AP07 (`stop()` against its docstring and A01 C18's shutdown budget), P03-AP26 (the FIX-060 event and shutdown skips), P04-S156 (an abandoned worker at stop; A01 C20, C18), P02-AP08 and P02-AP19 (the boot summary and the window label), P09-AP18, P01-S100 and P01-S124 point 2 (which topic records B-08's "strategy validation"), P10-AP12 (W-23, S-53).

**A02 (configuration files, schema and loader).** FACT (interface): the strategy files, the schema and the loader are A02's; A07 reads the fields recorded in section 13 (P08 section 5); A02 AUDIT L1320 names the callers through `validate_strategy`: the direction map (`core/strategy_direction.py` `build_direction_map` L52 to L71) and the taxonomy (`strategies/taxonomy.py` L23 to L38), already traced in P02 (P02-S100 to P02-S102, P02-S068 to P02-S074), the officer (L230) and the status script (L56 to L109), traced in P07 (P07-S053, P07-S079), with the fields each reads in P08 (P08-S043, P08-S078); A02 AUDIT L540 names the metrics script's `load_config` call and its handler (P07-S014, P07-S096, P08-S054) (P01-S126 items 1 and 2; ADDENDUM 2). FACT (records): A02 K-40, K-41 and K-44 name `main.py` lines that pass inputs to A07 logic (P10-S077, P10-S078). Boundary points reported: P02-AP02 (A02's texts; T-13 not reclassified), P02-AP04 and P07-AP19 (the "never lies" claim and its KNOWN DEFECT test, A02 K-05 handing it to A16), P02-AP09 (the delivery predicate), P02-AP14 (the seed file and its header), P02-AP16 and P08-AP14 (the configuration-directory contract, A02 F-11), P03-AP13 and P05-AP20 (comments and manifest lines in A02's files), P05-AP22 (configuration validation), P06-AP01 (the strategy files' `pullback_wait_enabled`), P07-AP08 (the metrics script's configuration gate, A02 AUDIT L540), P04-S140 (the unit of `min_adr_pct` and `max_spread_pct`) and P04-S159 (step constants outside the weights file, A02 T-20); and, added at the STUDY check, P08-AP03 (the `entry_gate` section's name against what it governs; A02 and A10), P08-AP15 (the gate functions' side of the `v3_chain` key validation, shared with A02's schema and A18's callers), P10-AP04 (which topic examines the configuration guide's breaker text), P10-AP13 (stale line references in A02's configuration files) and P01-S101 (A07's part of A02 K-27, whose owner field names "A07 and A18" without a part).

**A03 (store, schema, connections).** FACT (interface): A07's status and row writes go through A03's methods (`update_signal_status`, `insert_screener_result`, the `gate_state` methods); the store discards the `ts` the screener passes; every A07 write waits for A03's write lock (P09-S016 to P09-S032, P09-S049); the officer opens the database through A03's write-capable `connect` (P07-S055, P07-S071); the A07 scripts' heartbeats go through A03's `StateStore` (P07-S037, P07-S068); A03's retention deletes `screener_results` after 90 days (P09-S034); no A07 file reads `daily_symbol_stats` or calls `delete_gate_state` (P03-S122, P06-S119, P09-S113). FACT (records): A03 RTR L147 gives A07 "the screener and gate tables, `strategy_metrics`, the direction-registry state file"; each is recorded with its readers (P09-S126). Boundary points reported: P06-AP02 and P06-AP19 (differences with the approved A03 records H-50, Q-11 and Q-45, which are not edited), P03-AP14 and P09-AP09 (P-02's "no caller passes `ts`"; Open Issues Register A06-12), P03-AP07 and P09-AP07 (commit boundaries and swallowed failures; P-11 (5)), P09-AP06 (store contention), P03-AP25 (the foreign key in production), P09-AP24 (retention of A07's rows), P07-AP05 and P07-AP06 (status sets and the trade date in `trades`), P07-AP10 and P07-AP12 (heartbeats and the officer's open; D-09, D-05), P09-AP20, P01-S099 and P01-S124 point 1 (H-17, U-24 and U-25 with no A07 side), P01-S109.

**A04 (broker adapter, quote, limiter).** FACT (interface): the screener's market data comes from A04's `Quote` (`vwap` from Kite's `average_price`; zero or absent OHLC values and limits mapped to `None`) (P04-S055 to P04-S060, P04-S128); a limiter refusal reaches the screener as `SKIPPED_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` and the gate as a counted quote failure (P03-S038 to P03-S042, P06-S121); the MIS gate uses A04's product resolver (P03-S031 to P03-S036, P03-S127). FACT (records): A04 RTR L144 gives A06 and A07 "the entry throttle and the unwired pre-check; `force_intraday_only` and the strategies' intents"; A07's share is strategy control's use of `force_intraday_only` and `intent`, while the throttle and the pre-check are A06's (P09-S127); A04 CS-31 links the placer as the blocklist's writer to A07's pre-drop (P10-S077, P10-S078). Boundary points reported: P03-AP05, P04-S147, P05-AP18 and P01-S112 (A07's handling of a 0.0 last price, A04 RD-12), P06-AP19 (A04 AUDIT L2779 against L2895), P03-AP06 (a terminal skip on a transient quote failure), P03-AP26 (the limiter at shutdown), P02-AP09 (the adapter lock tests `delivery_enabled` alone), P09-AP15 (three deciders of the intraday product), P10-AP12 (CS-31).

**A05 (market data, candle maths, the F&O ban list).** FACT (interface): no A07 file computes or compares `session_vwap`, imports `core/candle_math.py` or reads the ban list (P04-S128, P04-S130, P09-S110, P09-S116 to P09-S119); A05 RTR L205's A07 share (the None `atr` and `rsi`, the VWAP from the broker quote, the ban list's meaning) is recorded in P09-S072 (P09-S128). FACT (records; ADDENDUM 2): A05 RTR L702 records that the A05 AUDIT's section 1.3 gives "the alias consumer and screening" to A06, while the index makes A07 the owner of screening; this is a records note left as recorded, and the index governs: A07 (P01-S126 item 7). Boundary points reported: P10-AP10 (A05-01's Phase 2 input and A18's duplicate step formulas), P09-AP20 (FB-01, FB-02, FB-07 and FB-14 as the absence of an A07 reader), P04-S141 (the absent producers of four step inputs, with A05 CM-22 and A03 H-16), P04-S148 and P01-S108 (the screener's VWAP, A05 CM-07), P04-S158 and P01-S110 (the A05-25 comparison, D-39 P3).

**A06 (intake and the processor).** FACT (interface): under D-38, A06's processor calls strategy control at L959, L2062 and L2411 and writes its rejects (L1201), calls `screen()` at L998 and consumes the returned `ScreeningResult` (L1005 to L1028) (P02-S031 to P02-S040, P03-S108 to P03-S118, P09-S014, P09-S034); A06 AUDIT L4009's "the strategy-control gate before it (P04-S032)" (`signals/signal_processor.py` L959 to L972) is already traced in P02 (P02-S031 to P02-S035) (P01-S126 item 3; ADDENDUM 2); a FIX-069 retry runs strategy control and `screen()` again for the same signal, as A06 RTR L130, L162 and L347 state (P09-S088, P09-S089, P09-S125); A06 RTR L214's "screener and entry gate" among the writers of `signals` rows: neither creates a row (P09-S129); no A07 code performs the end-of-day route's excluded-symbol check that A06 TG14 (A06 RTR L670, L763) names (P09-S124). FACT (records; ADDENDUM 2): A06 RTR L663 and L735 (treatment group TG7) state a Phase 2 rule on whether any signal may proceed "through screening, sizing, risk and reservation during shutdown", "led by A01 with A07, A09, A10 and A12"; it is a Phase 2 input, not a legacy finding, and is not traced; the shutdown facts are recorded with A01 C20 and A06 PQ-02 (sections 4, 8 and 16) (P01-S126 item 5). Boundary points reported: P06-AP01 (pullback strategies and the unfed gate), P06-AP10 (the resume docstring's in-flight premise), P06-AP11 (the telemetry blind spot), P06-AP12 (the direction mapping in `continue_from_gate`), P05-AP16, P05-AP17, P03-AP20 and P04-S149 (WA-06 and WA-07 values passed through unchecked), P03-AP06 (FIX-069 and quote skips), P03-AP07 (EV-02 case (c)), P03-AP09 and P09-AP01 (repeated screening of one signal), P03-AP25 (screening a signal with no row), P03-AP26 (the drain at shutdown), P09-AP04 (A06's claim as A07's only guard), P09-AP09 (A06-12), P09-AP12 (six age checks), P09-AP14 (two quotes for one passed signal), P09-AP19 and P01-S104 (TG14's excluded-symbol check), P09-AP22 (A06's comments against the code), P02-AP08 (the handlers), P02-AP19 (the window label at the call site), P04-S157 (the order of concurrent signals; ET-06, A06-02), P01-S102 (D-38 citations).

## 18. Points the AUDIT may examine, and what cannot be established

This section carries every Point for the AUDIT and every UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED item of the ten parts, in the parts' own wording as corrected by their checks, so that none is dropped, softened or turned into a judgement. Where two or more parts raise the same point, the point is listed once under one number with every source ID and each source's wording, marked [ID]; nothing else is merged. No point is resolved or classified here: the classification is the AUDIT's (Document 04, section 4; Document 08). P01-S114, re-labelled FACT in P01's check because ADDENDUM 1 settled it, is carried with its note. The list is ordered by part; the STUDY section where the subject is described is given for each entry. Points are numbered A07-SP-01 onward and unknown items A07-SU-01 onward; the hyphen after the letters keeps the three-digit numbers clear of the repository's identifier check.

(P04-S028, P04-S138)

(P01-S074)
- **A07-SP-17** (P02-AP01; P08-AP06; STUDY sections 3, 13) — [P02-AP01] Out-of-set `trade_type`: the resolver treats any value other than INTRADAY or DELIVERY as BOTH, and `VALID_TRADE_TYPES` is defined but read nowhere (P02-S011, P02-S020). On the trading path the `SystemConfig` validator refuses such a value (P02-S058), but the status-table route reads the raw YAML without it, where a value of another type also falls through without a raise (P02-S049, P02-S051, P02-S052, P02-S122). Two legacy audits recorded the fall-through and the code was not changed after them (P02-S121; added in the check). Is the permissive fall-through the intended behaviour of the "single authority", and is the unused constant intended? [P08-AP06] The status table reads `trade_type` and `force_intraday_only` from the raw YAML without the `SystemConfig` validator and passes them to the resolver, which treats an out-of-set `trade_type` as BOTH; the trading process would refuse the same value at boot (P08-S012, P08-S015; P02-S051, P02-AP01). Is the A07 side of this already covered by P02-AP01, or does the configuration route need its own point?
- **A07-SP-18** (P02-AP02; STUDY sections 3) — Texts that predate Option A: the `Verdict` docstring ("post-force-rewrite") and the "(effective) intent" comment in `strategies/control.py` (P02-S022), the auditor's "post-coercion product" comment (P02-S045, A02's file) and `core/config_loader.py` L2038 to L2043 and the L2030 field comment (P02-S062, A02's file, not listed in A02 T-13) describe a load-time rewrite that `8116b74` removed. Do these texts still describe the code? Boundary: the A02 texts stay A02's; T-13 is not reclassified.
- **A07-SP-19** (P02-AP03; STUDY sections 3) — The breaker verdict carries `product: "INTRADAY"` while `will_trade` is False (P02-S021, P02-S022), and no processor site reads `product` (P02-S022); the only reader uses it for will-trade verdicts only (P02-S045). Is a product on a won't-trade verdict meaningful, and does the docstring's statement that the placement path uses it hold?
- **A07-SP-20** (P02-AP04; P07-AP19; STUDY sections 3, 12) — [P02-AP04] "Can NEVER disagree": the control and status-table docstrings and the processor comment state that the gate and the table cannot disagree (P02-S008, P02-S052, P02-S118), while the table differs from the gate in its file read time, its flag source and defaults, its omission of playbook strategies and its lack of `delivery_enabled` (P02-S051). Under which conditions can the table and the gate differ in practice, and is the claim accurate? Boundary: A02 K-05 hands the "never lies" KNOWN DEFECT test to A16 for `cron_officer.py`, while `scripts/strategy_status.py` and `strategies/control.py`, which carry the claim, are A07's files; which topic applies that test to the claim is a boundary question. [P07-AP19] Status table as a "will it trade today?" view: the table is derived from fresh reads and raw flags, omits the playbook strategy and `delivery_enabled`, and shows nothing of the windows, the governor or any metric or registry state (P07-S083, P07-S084). Is the "can NEVER disagree with live behaviour" claim accurate, and which topic applies the KNOWN DEFECT test to it? Boundary: A02 K-05 (A16 for the status rows), P02-AP04.
- **A07-SP-21** (P02-AP05; STUDY sections 3) — Playbook strategy outside the table: the status table omits `pb01_breakout_retest`, which the boot summary lists and which the gate would reject as `STRATEGY_CONTROL` if an item for it reached the processor; its only scanner is routed to the end-of-day capture path before the queue (P02-S041, P02-S048, P02-S063, P02-S080, P02-S120). Is the 15-row table the intended operator view of "will it trade today?", and do the table, the boot summary and the gate describe the playbook strategy consistently?
- **A07-SP-22** (P02-AP06; STUDY sections 3) — Lossy labels: `DISABLED` and `FORCE_BREAKER` share `REJECTED_STRATEGY_CONTROL` and differ only in the reason text; the table shows no cause (P02-S091, P02-S093). The dashboard counts `REJECTED_TRADE_TYPE` among risk rejects and `REJECTED_STRATEGY_CONTROL` in neither named family (P02-S096). Is the mapping complete and consistent for the readers? Boundary: the dashboard reader is A15's.
- **A07-SP-23** (P02-AP07; STUDY sections 3) — Precedence and the TRADE_TYPE label: the breaker branch is tested before the trade_type branch, so a DELIVERY strategy under the breaker is labelled `STRATEGY_CONTROL` even when `trade_type` is INTRADAY, and under the shipped defaults no DELIVERY strategy would produce `REJECTED_TRADE_TYPE` (P02-S019, P02-S063). The Phase-4 comment says the distinct label exists "so a delivery go-live shows 'rejected because trade_type disallows' distinctly" (P02-S012). Does the order serve that stated aim? (Question for Rama R-1.)
- **A07-SP-24** (P02-AP08; STUDY sections 3) — A raise recorded as a placement failure: an exception inside strategy control on any processor path would be written as `PLACEMENT_FAILED` (P02-S035, P02-S036, P02-S037), although no placement was attempted; the boot summary and the status table would lose the whole output, not one strategy (P02-S041, P02-S050). With validated inputs a raise is not expected (P02-S026, P02-S027). Is the status vocabulary adequate for such a failure? Boundary: the handlers are A06's (processor), A01's (`main.py`) and A16's (`cron_officer.py`).
- **A07-SP-25** (P02-AP09; STUDY sections 3) — `delivery_enabled` outside strategy control: the resolver and the processor receive no `delivery_enabled`, so with the delivery lock off a DELIVERY strategy can pass strategy control and be refused only at the broker (P02-S015, P02-S061). Is that division intended, and do the texts that describe the three-flag rule match it? Boundary: A02 K-05; the adapter lock is A04's.
- **A07-SP-26** (P02-AP10; P07-AP01; STUDY sections 3, 12) — [P02-AP10] PAPER_ONLY: the metrics script's docstring and alert say that it sets a strategy to PAPER_ONLY, but no state that strategy control or the trading path reads is written, and nothing on the trading path reads `strategy_metrics` (P02-S076 to P02-S079). Does the alert describe what the code does? Boundary: the script is P07's (Q18) and the governor handoff A09's. (Question for Rama R-3.) [P07-AP01] PAPER_ONLY: the metrics script's docstring says it "auto-sets strategy to PAPER_ONLY" and its alert body says "Strategy set to PAPER_ONLY.", while no version of the script writes any strategy state, `main` passes no notifier so the alert is never sent on the declared route, and the only trace of a met condition is a bare log line without the strategy name (P07-S030 to P07-S035, P07-S041, P07-S044). Do the docstring and the alert text describe what the code does, and does the rule work as the safety measure the docstring describes? (P02-AP10; P01-S105; Questions P07-R1 and P02-R3.)
- **A07-SP-27** (P02-AP11; STUDY sections 3) — No run-time switch: the dashboard's control client expects a `/control/status` endpoint that no tracked trading-side code implements, and a legacy record says it was never built (P02-S084). Is a strategy's on/off state meant to be changeable only by editing the file and restarting? Boundary: A15 (dashboard).
- **A07-SP-28** (P02-AP12; STUDY sections 3) — A02 V-06 handoff answered on A07's side: on the code reading of P02-S026, the resolver does not raise on validated strategies, and the raw-YAML route adds no raise case (P02-S122, added in the check); the auditor's silent-pass case needs a failure of the deferred import or of A02's own statements (P02-S026, P02-S027, P02-S046). Every caller of `audit()` that passes strategies passes loaded and validated objects (P02-S025, as corrected in the check). Does this settle the V-06 OPEN QUESTION for A07, leaving the handler itself with A02?
- **A07-SP-29** (P02-AP13; STUDY sections 3, 12, 14) — Unused accessors and constants: `canonical_direction`, `registered_direction`, `VALID_STATUSES`, `VALID_HEALTH` and `DEFAULT_REGISTRY_PATH` have no tracked non-test reader (P02-S100, P02-S102, P02-S105); the package initialisers' export lists are used by no importer (P02-S114 to P02-S117). Are these planned interfaces, leftovers or intended (Q25)?
- **A07-SP-30** (P02-AP14; P07-AP17; STUDY sections 12) — [P02-AP14] Registry file texts and writers: the seed's header and the cron declaration's comment say that the officer writes the tracked file, while the code writes the state file under `data_store/` (P02-S109, P02-S111, P02-S112). Do the texts describe the code? Boundary: the cron comment is A16's; the seed file is in A02's `config/`. [P07-AP17] Texts against code for the officer: the cron registry comment says the officer "writes config/strategy_direction_registry.yaml" and runs after the metrics job, while the code writes the state file under `data_store/` and reads nothing from the metrics job (P07-S072; P02-S112, P02-AP14). Boundary: A16 (the comment).
- **A07-SP-31** (P02-AP15; P07-AP14; STUDY sections 12) — [P02-AP15] Registry fallback on a corrupt state file: `load_registry` treats an unparseable state file like an absent one and, when given a seed path as the officer does, returns the seed's rows (all PENDING) (P02-S103, P02-S109, P02-S111). Could a damaged state file silently revert confirmed rows for a run, and what would the officer then announce? Boundary: the officer's behaviour is P07's. [P07-AP14] Officer after an unreadable state file (A03 E-08): the run works from the seed, may re-announce registrations and conflicts, drops rows that were only in the lost state, and, when anything changed, overwrites the state with seed-derived rows (otherwise the unreadable file stays and every run falls back again), with no log line naming the fallback (P07-S073, P07-S074; P02-AP15; condition added in the check).
- **A07-SP-32** (P02-AP16; STUDY sections 12) — Path bases: the registry state path is anchored to the repository, while the seed, the strategy files, `build_direction_map("config")` in the end-of-day summary and `build_taxonomy_map`'s default resolve against the working directory (P02-S070, P02-S100, P02-S107, P02-S111). Do these mixed bases hold under every declared invocation? Boundary: A02 F-11 (the configuration-directory contract); A12 (`eod_squareoff.py`).
- **A07-SP-33** (P02-AP17; STUDY sections 12) — Registry write without a lock: `save_registry` uses a fixed temporary file name and no lock (P02-S104, P02-S113). Is a single writer guaranteed? Boundary: the schedule is A16's.
- **A07-SP-34** (P02-AP18; STUDY sections 3) — Text against code for the strategy-control call: the processor's comments name "LAYERS 1+3" while the call also applies LAYER 0 (P02-S118); the control docstring names two importers while four files call the function (P02-S030). Do these texts describe the code?
- **A07-SP-35** (P02-AP19; STUDY sections 3, 17) — Window reject label: the global and the per-strategy window share the label `OUTSIDE_ENTRY_WINDOW` and differ only in the reason text (P02-S086); comments give `gap_fade_long` an 11:30 cut-off that its file does not have (P02-S090); and at the file values the per-strategy window changes no outcome for the 15 strategies because the global window (10:00 to 15:00) already contains it (P02-S124, added in the check). Is the shared label intended, and is a per-strategy window wider than the global one intended? Boundary: the window logic is A01's, the label at the call site A06's; the comment is already handed to A01 by A02 T-11.
- **A07-SP-36** (P03-AP01; STUDY sections 4) — Step errors: any one error step gives `REJECTED_STEP_ERROR`, with no test of the step's weight, while SS4 step 4 conditions the reject on "that step has weight > 0" (P03-S012, P03-S060). Does the code follow the locked decision, and does it matter at the committed weights (every step has a positive weight in `config/scoring_weights.yaml` L11 to L20; part P05)?
- **A07-SP-37** (P03-AP02; STUDY sections 4) — The "REJECTED_<step>" vocabulary: the `status` comment, P18, SS3 and module 29's tests describe step-named rejects, but only `REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE` names a step, the executor's `rejected_at` is never read, and a step that returned 0.0 acts only through the score (P03-S012, P03-S067, P03-S071). Do the comment and the locked texts still describe the code?
- **A07-SP-38** (P03-AP03; P04-S142; P09-AP12; STUDY sections 4, 5, 10, 11; merged by subject at assembly, the signal-age rule; the three questions differ in part: P04-S142 also covers P9a's circuit reject and P09-AP12 asks which of six checks decides; noted at the STUDY check) — [P03-AP03] Precedence and the age check: R7 (score) runs before R8 (signal age), so a stale signal below the minimum is recorded as a score reject; R8 tests exact equality with 0.0 and writes no log line (P03-S064, P03-S066, P03-S067). SS4 orders the two this way and calls R8 a "defense-in-depth" that gives an "explicit rejection reason" (P03-S012). Is the reason recorded for a stale signal the one the design meant to make visible? (Step 10's rule and threshold: part P04; A02 T-20; A06 PQ-08 (6).) [P04-S142] OPEN QUESTION (for the AUDIT): P9a asks the screener to reject a signal older than 90 s and a stock at a circuit; the code scores step 10 with 30 s and 60 s limits and step 9 with 0.0, rejects on age only after the score test, and at the committed file values never reaches `REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE` on the live route. How are P9a, SE4, SS8 and the weights file's comments to be compared with this behaviour, alongside A02 T-20 and A06 PQ-08 (6)? (P04-S014, P04-S094, P04-S095, P04-S099, P04-S108, P04-S113, P04-S124, P04-S136) [P09-AP12] Six age checks: the receiver, the processor, step 10, R8, the HardGate and A18's reconstruction apply different limits (600, 30 and 60 s, 60.0 s) at different moments, none with a lower bound, and P9a's 90 s in none (P09-S057, P09-S058, P09-S120). Which check is meant to decide a stale signal, and is the recorded reason (`REJECTED_SCORE_<n>` at the committed files when no step times out; condition added in the check) the one meant? (Boundary: A06 for the first two; P03-AP03.)
- **A07-SP-39** (P03-AP04; STUDY sections 4) — A quote at a circuit limit is a score input on the `off` and `shadow` paths and a hard reject (`REJECTED_AT_CIRCUIT`) on the `enforce` path; SS8's "normal" and "unknown" circuit states are not written (P03-S044, P03-S050, P03-S103). Are the mode-dependent meaning and the empty-string state as intended? (Boundary: step 9, part P04; the HardGate, part P05.)
- **A07-SP-40** (P03-AP05; STUDY sections 4) — A `0.0` last price is used as a price and, with a positive lower limit, sets `"lower_circuit"` (P03-S045). How should the screener's side of A04 RD-12 be recorded in the AUDIT (boundary: A04 owns the convention; parts P04 and P05 own the uses of `ltp` and `circuit_state`)?
- **A07-SP-41** (P03-AP06; STUDY sections 4) — `SKIPPED_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` covers both an absent quote entry and a raised error, including a rate-limit refusal; neither is retried, and the processor's FIX-069 retry applies only at placement (P03-S038 to P03-S042). Is a terminal skip on a transient quote failure consistent with SS10 and with A06's retry design? (Boundary: A04's limiter, A06's FIX-069.)
- **A07-SP-42** (P03-AP07; STUDY sections 4) — Swallowed store failures: a failed status write leaves `PROCESSING`, a failed analytics write leaves no row, and the caller is not told (P03-S081 to P03-S083). SS5 and SS10 state this behaviour as intended, while P18 says "No silent drops" (P03-S012). How should the conflict be recorded (boundary: A06 EV-02 case (c); A03 P-11 case (5))?
- **A07-SP-43** (P03-AP08; P05-AP01; STUDY sections 4, 6, 7) — [P03-AP08] Exceptions that the screener does not catch (the blocklist calls, the circuit-proximity rule, the strategy attribute reads, the enforce gate and re-scale helpers) leave `screen()` and are recorded by the processor as `PLACEMENT_FAILED`, with no screener row and no P3 record (P03-S096, P03-S098, P03-S105). SS10 names error routes only for the quote, the executor, the scorer and the store. Is that coverage complete for the routes added later (MIS, proximity, V3)? [P05-AP01] The HardGate's docstrings say "evaluate() never raises", but `evaluate` has no `try`; on the enforce path an exception would leave `screen()` and be recorded by the processor as `PLACEMENT_FAILED` (P05-S047; P03-S098, P03-AP08). - **A07-SP-44** (P03-AP09; P09-AP01; STUDY sections 4, 10) — [P03-AP09] Double evaluation: the screener has no idempotency check; a second screening of the same signal appends a second `screener_results` row and overwrites the status, so a reject can be followed by `PASSED` (P03-S079, P03-S104). (Boundary: whether the processor screens a signal twice, A06 RTR L130, L162, L347, is part P09's.) [P09-AP01] Repeated screening of one signal: a FIX-069 retry re-runs strategy control and `screen()` for the same `signal_id` up to three more times, each run adding a `screener_results` row and a P3 record and replacing the status, so a signal that passed and was reserved can end rejected by the screener, or the reverse (P09-S088, P09-S089). The screener has no idempotency check (P03-AP09). Is a full re-screening the intended meaning of a retry? (Boundary: A06 for the FIX-069 branch, A06 RTR L130, L162, L347; question P09-R1.)
- **A07-SP-45** (P03-AP10; STUDY sections 4) — `REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE` and `REJECTED_CIRCUIT_PROXIMITY` each come from two mechanisms depending on the mode, and A15's P3 classifier would label the enforce path's `REJECTED_SIGNAL_AGE` as a scored reject (P03-S072, P03-S075). - **A07-SP-46** (P03-AP11; P05-AP21; STUDY sections 4, 6, 7) — [P03-AP11] On the enforce path, a positive `min_score` for which `rescale_min_score` returns 0 leaves no score floor at all, while on the `off` path the same override is a floor (P03-S052, P03-S064). - **A07-SP-47** (P03-AP12; STUDY sections 4) — Logging: R7, R8, E4 and E6 write no log line in the screener, the OFF step-error route logs a WARNING while E4 logs nothing in the screener (the executor logs an ERROR for each error step on both paths; qualified in the check), and the processor logs every reject at INFO (L1017 to L1019) (P03-S049, P03-S064, P03-S102, P03-S111). SS11 asks for an INFO line from the screener on every call (P03-S012). Is the combined logging as intended?
- **A07-SP-48** (P03-AP13; STUDY sections 4) — Texts that no longer match the code: the configuration comment's line numbers for the MIS shadow test (P03-S037), `config/expected_managers.yaml` L68's line for `_persist` and L66's line for the constructor (P03-S089; L66 added in the check), and the shadow WARNING whose text names a status that is not written (P03-S034). - **A07-SP-49** (P03-AP14; STUDY sections 4) — Boundary (A03): A03 AUDIT P-02 states that no caller passes `ts` to `update_signal_status` ("grep of the 32 calls"), while the screener passes `ts=ts` at L619; the effect recorded by P-02 (no status time is stored) is unchanged (P03-S084, P03-S123). - **A07-SP-51** (P03-AP16; STUDY sections 4) — Completeness of what is recorded: the result and the rows carry fixed `score=0`, `tier="LOW"` for unscored outcomes in the same columns as computed scores, `rejection_reason` holds `PASSED` for a pass, and the effective minimum, the reject detail texts, the gate evidence and the quote's time stamp are not in the result (P03-S025, P03-S026, P03-S073, P03-S080). - **A07-SP-55** (P03-AP20; STUDY sections 4) — Malformed or impossible signal values (a not-a-number or infinite `trigger_price`; a `triggered_at` later than receipt) pass through the screener unchecked to the rule, the steps, the gate and the P3 record (P03-S099, P03-S100). - **A07-SP-59** (P03-AP24; P09-AP23; STUDY sections 4, 9) — [P03-AP24] (added in the check) — The `screener_results.eligible_score` column is described as the "per-strategy min_score threshold" (schema and store docstring), while the screener writes the route's effective minimum, which at the committed files is always a global value, and NULL on ten routes (P03-S141). - **A07-SP-60** (P03-AP25; STUDY sections 4) — (added in the check) — With the foreign key on `screener_results.signal_id` enforced, a screening of a `signal_id` with no `signals` row writes no status (no-op UPDATE) and no analytics row (failed INSERT, swallowed), while the result is still returned (P03-S142, P03-S078, P03-S081). - **A07-SP-61** (P03-AP26; STUDY sections 4) — (added in the check) — At shutdown the FIX-060 event is set before the processor's stop, so a drained signal whose screening quote fetch has to wait for a limiter token is recorded `SKIPPED_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` with an ERROR line and traceback, the same status and log level as a broker failure (P03-S039, P03-S040, P03-S143). - **A07-SP-62** (P04-S140; STUDY sections 5) — OPEN QUESTION (for the AUDIT): the steps compare `min_adr_pct` and `max_spread_pct` with percentages, while S4 calls percentage fields decimals and the legacy guides describe the 0.005 default as "0.5%" or "%"; at the committed 0.005 the spread step demands a spread of at most 0.005 % of the mid price on every live screening. - **A07-SP-63** (P04-S141; STUDY sections 5) — OPEN QUESTION (for the AUDIT): four step inputs are always `None` on the live route (`avg_volume_20d`, `atr`, `rsi`, `sector`), so steps 1 and 3 always score 0.0, steps 4 and 6 always 0.5, the per-strategy `min_volume_surge` and `min_adr_pct` never act, and the weighted sum cannot exceed 65 of 100 at the file weights; SS7 promised "v2.1" providers for these fields. - **A07-SP-64** (P04-S143; P04-S144; P09-AP03; STUDY sections 5, 10; merged by subject at assembly, the shared step worker; P04-S143 (the rotation's effect on concurrent signals) and P04-S144 (what the 5 s wait bounds) are separate points in P04, and P09-AP03 covers both; noted at the STUDY check) — [P04-S143] OPEN QUESTION (for the AUDIT): the M-S3 rotation shuts the old pool with `cancel_futures=True`; can that turn a queued step of another, concurrent signal into an ERROR (and that signal into `REJECTED_STEP_ERROR`), or make a just-snapshotted `submit` raise, as P04-S047 reads the code and the Python documentation, and does any source state the intended effect on concurrent signals? - **A07-SP-67** (P04-S148; STUDY sections 5) — OPEN QUESTION (for the AUDIT; A05 CM-07): the screener's VWAP is the broker's `average_price`; when it is absent or zero, step 2 scores 0.0 (counted against the signal), unlike the neutral value other steps give to missing input; does any source state which treatment was intended, beyond the code comment "No VWAP data (ETFs, bonds) -> fail gate"? - **A07-SP-69** (P04-S150; STUDY sections 5) — OPEN QUESTION (for the AUDIT): the executor receives the regular-session open and never the special-session time, while the FIX-114 comment gives special sessions as the reason for injecting the open; is this an issue at the committed file values (no special session) or only a latent one? (P04-S018, P04-S020 to P04-S022)
- **A07-SP-70** (P04-S151; STUDY sections 5) — OPEN QUESTION (for the AUDIT): the spread step scores a crossed or locked quote 1.0, a quote with an empty bid 0.0 (+200 %), a quote with an empty ask 1.0 (−200 %) and an empty book 0.5 (corrected in the check with P04-S092), and the Kite documentation's example shows depth levels with price 0; SE4 defines the spread over the last price while the code uses the mid price (FIX-043). - **A07-SP-71** (P04-S152; STUDY sections 5) — OPEN QUESTION (for the AUDIT): P04-S110 lists eleven places where names, docstrings, locked decisions or the weights file's comments describe the steps or the executor differently from the code (statuses named REJECTED that do not reject; "Reject" for step 9; P18's step-named statuses; P9a (c); "pure"; "Stateless"; "never raises"; the missing `TIMEOUT`; the weights file's step descriptions; ATR or ADR; the "signal_age" log text and comment). - **A07-SP-78** (P05-AP02; STUDY sections 6, 7) — The module and the class make liquidity a no-op ("A8"), while the plan the module cites lists liquidity as a live, fail-closed rule (P05-S010, P05-S011, P05-S042). No file defines "A8" (P05-S012). Was the plan's rule superseded, and where is that recorded?
- **A07-SP-79** (P05-AP03; STUDY sections 6, 7) — The five playbook gates cite a specification ("V3 DECISION CONTENT SPECIFICATION v1.0 §3") that does not exist in the repository, and the legacy register records that their values were "OBSERVED-FROM-CODE" and that "A DECISION is missing"; the committed thresholds are labelled "SEEDS" (P05-S013, P05-S090, P05-S091); other legacy records say Step 10a was "Ratified via" that specification and call it "The AUTHORITY" for PB-01's thresholds (P05-S116, added in the check). (Boundary: the ratification of PB-01's gates, item G4 of the legacy register, is A18's context.)
- **A07-SP-80** (P05-AP04; STUDY sections 6, 7) — Among the must-have gates, a NaN input can pass `gate_confirm` (its tests fail only when a comparison is true) while it fails `gate_pullback` and `gate_rr` (P05-S080, P05-S087, P05-S089, P05-S092; one exception added in the check: a NaN `proximity_atr_mult` is dropped by `max()` in `gate_pullback`). - **A07-SP-81** (P05-AP05; STUDY sections 6, 7) — `gate_confirm` measures the body as `abs(close − open)`, so a candle that closes below its open but above the level, with a large body, confirms (P05-S087). - **A07-SP-82** (P05-AP06; STUDY sections 6, 7) — `gate_extreme`'s docstring says "status UNKNOWN → PASS", but the code reads only `extreme_flag` and fails on any truthy value (P05-S085). - **A07-SP-83** (P05-AP07; STUDY sections 6, 7) — `gate_rr` treats any side other than `BUY` or `LONG` (including `None`, an empty or unknown text) as a short; accepts a negative ATR or multiplier without a test; uses no buffer when the ATR is missing or 0; and returns its computed values in the evidence on two of its five failure routes only (P05-S077, P05-S080, P05-S081). - **A07-SP-84** (P05-AP08; STUDY sections 6, 7) — `gate_rr` decides on the unrounded ratio but describes a failure with two decimals, so a failing ratio can be described as equal to the floor ("R:R 2.00 < floor 2.0") (P05-S079). Is the record clear enough for the would-be analysis that reads it?
- **A07-SP-85** (P05-AP09; STUDY sections 6, 7) — QS3 and module 28 state that a missing step contributes 0.0, QS4 and the weights file describe a sum, and FIX-042's docstring describes a timed-out step as excluded; the code is proportional over the steps present and counts a timed-out step at 0.5 (P05-S009, P05-S030; P04-S111); QS8's import list is not the scorer's (P05-S117, added in the check). - **A07-SP-86** (P05-AP10; STUDY sections 6, 7) — On the live route the tier thresholds are unreachable or reachable only at the maximum (HIGH 80 and MEDIUM 65 against a ceiling of 65; v3 HIGH 75 and MEDIUM 56 against a ceiling of 56; ceilings that hold only when no step times out, 78 and 72 otherwise, qualified in the check), and the v3 thresholds are stated to need data-fitting before enforce (P05-S029, P05-S031, P05-S068; HISTORICAL IA-P2-03, IA-P2-05). - **A07-SP-87** (P05-AP11; STUDY sections 6, 7) — The scorer and the re-scale round half to even at decision boundaries (a weighted sum of 59.5 is recorded as 60 and passes; 64.5 as 64, LOW), and the OFF and enforce pass decisions differ in a rounding band (P05-S020, P05-S029, P05-S069); binary floating point also moves a boundary: a kept-step sum of exactly 44.4 gives 55.49999999999999 and a total of 55, not 56 (P05-S068, added in the check). - **A07-SP-88** (P05-AP12; STUDY sections 6, 7) — Unused results and objects: `ScoreResult.passed`, `step_scores`, `missing_steps` and `tier_thresholds` are computed and read by no non-test code, and the processor stores a scorer that it never uses (P05-S026, P05-S027, P05-S028). - **A07-SP-100** (P05-AP26; STUDY sections 6, 7) — The scorer derives its step names by reading `model_fields` from a model instance, which current Pydantic documentation calls deprecated, with a fallback branch for objects without it (P05-S017). - **A07-SP-101** (P05-AP27; STUDY sections 6, 7) — (added in the check) — The circuit-proximity rule's decision does not depend on the direction it is given, and when `upper / lower` is at most about 1.0408 its ceiling and floor meet or cross, so every positive entry is refused (limits 102 and 98: ceiling 99.96, floor 99.96000000000001) (P05-S119). - **A07-SP-102** (P05-AP28; STUDY sections 6, 7) — (added in the check) — The ceilings of 65 (OFF) and 56 (V3) and the comments and manifest entries that call a score above 65 "algebraically impossible" hold only when no step times out: the executor records any timed-out step as 0.5, including the always-0.0 steps 1 and 3, which can raise the totals to 78 and 72 and make `MEDIUM`, the v3 `MEDIUM` above 56 and the `score_gt_ceiling` tripwire reachable; a timed-out step 2 alone allows a V3 pass at 50 (P05-S029, P05-S068, P05-S104 (r)). - **A07-SP-104** (P06-AP02; STUDY sections 8) — The end-of-day clear is not wired (boundary: A01 for the `main.py` wiring, A12 for the end-of-day module; A03 for the records H-50, Q-11 and Q-45): `EodSquareoff` is built without `entry_gate`, so `clear_all` is never reached, against the FIX-046 commit message, the `clear_all` docstring, the legacy entry IA-P2-01 and the project records A03 H-50 (L1037), A03 Q-11 (L2045) and the A03 MATCH row Q-45 (L2272, which lists `orders/eod_squareoff.py` L342 to L346 among the callers) (P06-S013, P06-S072 to P06-S076, P06-S114, P06-S117, P06-S119; the searches that establish the wiring are in P06-S074, completed in the check, and Q-45 was added to this point in the check). - **A07-SP-105** (P06-AP03; P09-AP25; STUDY sections 8, 9) — [P06-AP03] Rehydration without status, age or value tests: rows are restored whatever the signal's current status, age or trading date, with no validation of direction, prices or tolerance, and malformed rows are skipped at every start but never removed (P06-S012, P06-S029, P06-S030, P06-S032). With no end-of-day clear (P06-AP02), is the per-entry time-out the only protection against stale rows (P06-S053), and does the implementation meet Audit 4.4's stated design ("query signals with `status='GATE_WAITING'`")? Should the AUDIT record this as A07's side of the stale-row question? (Boundary: A01, A12 for the wiring.)
- **A07-SP-106** (P06-AP04; STUDY sections 8) — Second resumption (A03 Q-11 and A06 EV-11, A07's side): a failed release leaves the row and the old status while the callback still resumes, and neither the rehydration nor `continue_from_gate` reads the signal's status (P06-S065, P06-S100, P06-S117, P06-S124). - **A07-SP-107** (P06-AP05; STUDY sections 8) — Silent discards against EG12 and the `_check_one` docstring: the poll loop discards any exception and any wait time-out with `except Exception: pass` and no log (L396 to L397), while EG12 says "ERROR on exc" and `_check_one` says "Never raises -- all exceptions caught and logged" (P06-S049, P06-S057). Does the code meet its stated logging design?
- **A07-SP-108** (P06-AP06; STUDY sections 8) — A `PRICE_HIT` lost after the status write: a non-dict `extras` from a rehydrated row makes `_release` raise after `GATE_RELEASED_PRICE_HIT` is written and the row deleted, before the callback, with no log (P06-S032, P06-S066). (Wording made neutral and qualified in the check, P06-S066.)
- **A07-SP-109** (P06-AP07; STUDY sections 8) — `stop()` against its docstring (boundary: A01 C18 for the shutdown budget): "join within 5s" against about 8 s with an unconditional 3 s sleep, no `is_alive()` test, a `RuntimeError` on the poll thread that the code does not exclude but that is not expected in practice, and a `cancel_futures` argument that needs Python 3.9 or later (production version UNKNOWN) (P06-S069 to P06-S071).
- **A07-SP-110** (P06-AP08; STUDY sections 8) — Releases during the stop window: limiter aborts after the shutdown event can turn waiting entries into `QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE` releases that delete their rows before `entry_gate.stop()`, so a restart would not restore them, against the stated purpose that "A mid-session restart therefore resumes watching the same signals" (P06-S028, P06-S055, P06-S089). Is this consistent with Audit 4.4's intent?
- **A07-SP-111** (P06-AP09; STUDY sections 8) — Time-out and concurrency details: a `PRICE_HIT` can follow a time-out that passed during a slow quote read; overlapping checks of one entry can count two failures in one cycle; the first caller's reason wins (P06-S049, P06-S058, P06-S081). Are these within the design (EG6 "hard" time-out, EG9 "3rd consecutive")?
- **A07-SP-112** (P06-AP10; STUDY sections 8) — The resume docstring's premise (boundary: A06): "in_flight tracking is NOT released here -- the EntryGate already manages it", while the gate holds no in-flight or claim state (P06-S101; A06 ET-13). Which component releases the receiver's claim for a gated signal, if any?
- **A07-SP-113** (P06-AP11; STUDY sections 8) — Telemetry blind spot (boundary: A15 for the counters, A06 for the comment L213 to L216): a resumption from a rehydrated row increments neither the processor's dispatch counter nor the gate's admission counter, so the `expected-dormant` tripwire would not show it, against the comment that the resume sites' "awakening surfaces at the gate units" (P06-S026, P06-S102).
- **A07-SP-114** (P06-AP12; STUDY sections 8) — Direction passed through unvalidated (boundary: A06 for the mapping): the gate never reads or validates `direction`; `continue_from_gate` maps anything other than `"LONG"` or `"BUY"` to `"SELL"`, under a comment that states the premise "WatchEntry.direction is always "LONG"/"SHORT"" (L2102 to L2103) (P06-S030, P06-S051, P06-S094). - **A07-SP-115** (P06-AP13; STUDY sections 8) — A 0.0 or non-finite price in the window test (A04 RD-12's A07 side): a 0.0 quote resets the failure count and never fires under the usual row values, but would fire for a row with `tolerance_pct >= 1` or `entry_price` 0, which the rehydration does not exclude; NaN values make the entry time out (P06-S056, P06-S120).
- **A07-SP-116** (P06-AP14; P09-AP08; STUDY sections 8, 9) — [P06-AP14] Status vocabulary at readers (boundary: part P09 for the full map; A15 and A19 for the readers): the dashboard renders every `GATE_` status, `GATE_WAITING` and `GATE_RELEASED_PRICE_HIT` included, as "Signal Rejected"; the crash-test validators list `REJECTED_ENTRY_GATE`, which no code writes, and no `GATE_` status (P06-S106). - **A07-SP-117** (P06-AP15; STUDY sections 8) — Conflicting legacy statements on whether the gate path was active: a test docstring of 14-Jul-2026 says "EntryGate pullback path active", while the 01-Aug-2026 audit entry and later code comments say nothing ever fed it (P06-S014 to P06-S016). - **A07-SP-123** (P07-AP03; STUDY sections 12) — The demotion trigger as coded: "3 consecutive days" is evaluated as the three most recent stored rows, which need not be consecutive days, are not tied to `--date`, are overlapping 30-day rolling values and are rounded to four decimals before the strict `< -0.3` comparison; a strategy whose every day loses the same amount gets 0.0 and never triggers (P07-S020, P07-S024, P07-S025, P07-S031). - **A07-SP-124** (P07-AP04; STUDY sections 12) — The Sharpe figure: it is computed on daily rupee P&L totals, only over dates with a closed trade, with no riskless rate or benchmark, and scaled by √252, while the cited definition is on differential returns and the √n scaling assumes no serial correlation (P07-S020 to P07-S022). Is the figure the intended measure, and is the DDL label "annualized Sharpe ratio" (schema L937) accurate for it? (Question P07-R2.)
- **A07-SP-125** (P07-AP05; STUDY sections 12) — Status sets: the metrics script counts only `CLOSED` trades, while the governor, the allocator and several store helpers count `CLOSED` and `CLOSED_MANUAL` (P07-S018); the legacy report contract records that the table "drops CLOSED_MANUAL" and does not use it for that reason (P07-S098, added in the check). Are the per-strategy figures consistent across components? Boundary: A09 (governor, allocator), A03 (the status values). (Question P07-R3.)
- **A07-SP-126** (P07-AP06; STUDY sections 12) — Trade date: trades are dated by `updated_at`, not `exit_time`, so a later update of a closed row moves it to another date bucket (P07-S019). Is `updated_at` the intended date, and which writers update closed rows? Boundary: A03 and the writers' owners.
- **A07-SP-127** (P07-AP07; STUDY sections 12) — Strategy set: the set of strategies processed uses a fixed 30-day window that ignores `--lookback`, has no upper date bound and no `net_pnl` condition (P07-S017). Is the difference from the metric windows intended?
- **A07-SP-128** (P07-AP08; STUDY sections 12) — Configuration gate: the script loads and validates the whole configuration and discards the result; `--config` does not reach A16's helpers, which use their own relative `config` (P07-S014, P07-S036). Is the load meant only as a gate? Boundary: A02 AUDIT L540 (the handler), A16 (the helpers).
- **A07-SP-129** (P07-AP09; STUDY sections 12) — Observability without logging configuration: neither script configures logging, so their INFO lines are not output, `extra` fields (the demoted strategy's name and Sharpe values; the officer's Telegram and e-mail error texts) are dropped, and a registry change by the officer leaves no log line (P07-S040, P07-S041, P07-S070). Are these outputs as intended? Boundary: `core/logger.py` is A15's; the log targets are A16's.
- **A07-SP-130** (P07-AP10; STUDY sections 12) — Heartbeats (A03 D-09, U-17, U-18): both scripts write their heartbeat to the relative default database whatever `--db` or `--db-path` says, and a dry run still records a heartbeat (P07-S037, P07-S068, P07-S095). The records are A03's (treatment AGREED, TG1); what does A07 record on its side for the scripts' options? Boundary: A16 (helper), A03 (the write).
- **A07-SP-131** (P07-AP11; STUDY sections 12) — Exits inside the timer: an `argparse` exit (an invalid option, or `--help`) raises `SystemExit` inside the heartbeat timer, which records `FAILED` (P07-S039); a `--help` run would therefore record a failed job and, with `alert=True`, a FAILED alert under the helper's stated policy (P07-S038). Is that the intended behaviour? Boundary: A16.
- **A07-SP-132** (P07-AP12; STUDY sections 12) — Officer's "read-only" comment (A03 D-05): the comment at L51 says the database reads are read-only, while the open is A03's write-capable `connect` with the analytics attach, and `connect_readonly` exists unused here (P07-S055, P07-S071). Boundary: A03 (open modes); Open Issues Register A03-11.
- **A07-SP-133** (P07-AP13; STUDY sections 12) — Silent empty inputs: every query error in the officer gives an empty set without a log line, which the reconcile step cannot tell from a database without signals or filled trades, and on such a run every recorded DIRECTION_CONFLICT is reset to OK and written without a notification, to be announced again as new on the next successful run (P07-S056, P07-S057; consequence added in the check). Does this match the project rule cited in A03 E-08 ("Never fail silently")?
- **A07-SP-134** (P07-AP15; STUDY sections 12) — Registry semantics: a DIRECTION_CONFLICT persists while any differing filled trade remains in the whole history; rows are never removed; an existing row's direction is never refreshed from the file (P07-S059 to P07-S062). Are these the intended semantics of "Recomputed each run" (L112)? (Question P07-R4.)
- **A07-SP-135** (P07-AP16; STUDY sections 12) — Notification route and order: INFO-level registrations go to e-mail through the writer that its docstring describes as the CRITICAL-alert sentinel, and the registry is written before the notification, so a failure in between leaves rows registered and never announced (P07-S064, P07-S066). Boundary: A14 (the sentinel path).
- **A07-SP-136** (P07-AP18; STUDY sections 12) — Status-table outputs and texts: the HTML table has no Reason column; `render_telegram` has no caller, the briefing building its own Telegram text from the name lists as the function's docstring states, while `PATHS.md` L291 lists the module's Telegram view without saying it is unused (re-worded in the check); `footnote` ignores its `force_intraday_only` parameter; the docstring fixes "full 15 rows" (P07-S077, P07-S080, P07-S086). Do the outputs and texts match the stated intent?
- **A07-SP-137** (P07-AP20; STUDY sections 12) — `strategy_metrics` readers: A15's pre-market brief selects three columns that the table does not define and so gets no rows; A15's system manager applies its own win-rate rule under the heading "Demotions / disables" (P07-S045). Are these readers consistent with the table and with the script's rule? Boundary: A15.
- **A07-SP-138** (P07-AP21; STUDY sections 12) — Index boundary: the index says that A09 may cite "the capital and governor handoff" of the metrics script, while the code at the baseline contains no such handoff; the governor and the allocator read `trades` directly and the allocator and `perf_weights` are unwired (P07-S047); the index's Appendix A also describes the script as one that "may set a strategy to PAPER_ONLY" (index L303), which the code does not do (P07-S099, added in the check). Are the index sentences to be read as intent or as descriptions? Boundary: the index; A09.
- **A07-SP-139** (P07-AP22; STUDY sections 12) — Declared order: the docstring says the run is "after EOD report and reconciliation", while the end-of-day report job is declared `enabled: false` ("RETIRED 29-Aug-2026") at the baseline (P07-S043). Does the docstring still describe the schedule? Boundary: A16, A15.
- **A07-SP-140** (P07-AP23; STUDY sections 12) — Partial runs and concurrency: per-strategy commits, separate read statements and lock timeouts allow a run to end with some strategies written and others not, and a strategy's figures to come from different database states (P07-S029, P07-S092). Is a partial run detectable from the outputs (the heartbeat says only `exit code 1`)?
- **A07-SP-141** (P07-AP24; STUDY sections 12) — Help text against code: the metrics script's `--db` help names a relative default, while the code's default is anchored to the repository (P07-S013). Does the text describe the code?
- **A07-SP-142** (P08-AP01; STUDY sections 13) — Two per-strategy screening thresholds, `min_volume_surge` (set differently per strategy, 1.2 to 2.0) and `min_adr_pct`, reach steps whose market-data inputs are always `None` on the live route, so no configured value can change a score (P08-S042, P08-S048, P08-S060, P08-S074; P04-S070, P04-S076). Does this meet the intent stated for the fields (the schema and the strategy guide describe them as filters), and how does the AUDIT classify a configured value that cannot act? (wording made neutral in the check)
- **A07-SP-143** (P08-AP02; STUDY sections 13) — `pullback_wait_tolerance_pct` and `pullback_wait_timeout_sec` are required to validate (`> 0` and `< 1`; `>= 1`) and are set in all 16 files, but nothing reads them; the gate's own tolerance and time-out come only from `gate_state` rows (P08-S044; A02 T-14; P06-S022). Is this configured-but-unread state, on A07's side, part of the gate wiring point (P06's points) or a separate configuration point?
- **A07-SP-144** (P08-AP03; STUDY sections 13) — The `entry_gate` section of `system_config.yaml` configures order placement and the circuit-proximity reject, not the `EntryGate` class, whose poll interval, worker count and quote-failure limit are code values; three of its keys (`max_spread_pct`, `min_depth_qty`, `liquidity_check_enabled`) have no reader by name (P08-S029, P08-S030; P06-S024, P06-S025; A02 RTR L95). Is the section's name, against what it governs, a point for the AUDIT on A07's side, or only on A02's and A10's?
- **A07-SP-145** (P08-AP04; STUDY sections 13) — The strategy field `max_spread_pct` (0.005, compared with a percentage by step 8, so 0.005 %) and `entry_gate.max_spread_pct` (0.5, commented as a percentage) share a name and differ by a factor of 100 under the step's arithmetic (P08-S031; P04-S103 to P04-S105; A02 K-21). P04 records the unit conflict for the step; does the AUDIT treat the same-name keys as one point with it or as a separate consistency point?
- **A07-SP-146** (P08-AP05; STUDY sections 13) — The defaults of `force_intraday_only` differ between its readers (`SystemConfig` `True`, processor constructor `False`, auditor `False`, status table `True`, loader parameter `False`; the loader added in the check), and those of `trade_type` between `"INTRADAY"` and `None` (P08-S021, P08-S022; A02 K-05). None is reached at the committed file and the `main.py` wiring. Is a default that differs from the schema's safety default (`True`, "blocks accidental CNC/DELIVERY orders", `core/config_loader.py` L2030) in a constructor of the trading path a point for the AUDIT, given that only other constructions (tests) can reach it?
- **A07-SP-147** (P08-AP07; STUDY sections 13) — At the committed mode `"shadow"`, `v3_min_pass_score` is read and stored but used by no code path, and `v3_gate_steps`, `v3_freshness_max_sec` and the two `v3_*` tier thresholds act only on a log line (P08-S038). Is that consistent with the file's statement that the v3 thresholds "MUST be data-fit before enforce" (`config/scoring_weights.yaml` L36 to L38), and does a soak that logs the NEW tier but not a NEW pass or reject against `v3_min_pass_score` give the evidence that statement asks for?
- **A07-SP-148** (P08-AP08; STUDY sections 13) — The MIS filter compares the resolver's product code with the literal `"MIS"` written in A07's code, while the code itself is configured in `product_map` (P08-S027, P08-S028; A04 PR-23). Is the second source of the same string a point for the AUDIT (it agrees at the committed map)?
- **A07-SP-149** (P08-AP09; STUDY sections 13) — In the metrics script, `TRADING_MODE` reaches no output because `main` passes no notifier, and the configuration that `main` loads is discarded after the load succeeds (P08-S052, P08-S054; A02 AUDIT L540). Are these configuration inputs, as declared, meaningful? (The script's logic is P07's.)
- **A07-SP-150** (P08-AP10; STUDY sections 13) — Comments next to configured values state behaviour or values that the code and the files do not have: the load-time intent rewrite under `force_intraday_only` (`config/system_config.yaml` L138 to L141), `delivery_enabled` "Stays false" (L151 to L154, value `true`), the 90 s age limit (`config/scoring_weights.yaml` L20, against 30 s and 60 s in code and 60.0 in the key), and the `mis_filter` comment's line references (P08-S025, P08-S067; P03-S037; A02 T-13, T-20). - **A07-SP-151** (P08-AP11; STUDY sections 13) — The screener's reads of the strategy `name` for its stored rows and evidence were added after the last recorded deployment (`8d27fdb0a70865765a08df11a43728a00f69d18c`, 2026-09-10), so the deployed version, if it was `7d4970a26991fd40774023f5293a00556228e14b`, did not pass a strategy name to `_persist` (P08-S018). Does the AUDIT note that any later evidence that relies on a strategy name in screener rows cannot be assumed for production before that commit? (wording made neutral in the check)
- **A07-SP-152** (P08-AP12; P10-AP10; STUDY sections 13, 15) — [P08-AP12] A18's research module `v3_chain/forward_shadow.py` recomputes steps 1 and 3 with its own defaults (`min_volume_surge=1.5`, `min_adr_pct=0.5`, L38 to L39), which are A07's executor fallbacks, not the strategies' configured values (P08-S042, P08-S059). (P10-S075)
- **A07-SP-153** (P08-AP13; STUDY sections 13) — No configured key carries P9a's 90 s; on the live OFF and shadow paths a signal's age is judged by step 10's code constants (30 s and 60 s) and the R8 rule, while the configured `scoring.v3_freshness_max_sec` acts only in the shadow log (P08-S038, P08-S067; A02 T-20; P04 section 7.10).
